# Terra Firma — Revival Tracker

Working list for finishing the mod. Update as things land.

**Resolved history lives in TODO-archive.md** (split 2026-08-06; major trim pass 2026-08-11; completed-task prune 2026-08-22): build records, verify-session logs, superseded specs, decomp ground truth, harness rounds. **Held content lives in TODO-quarantine.md** (created 2026-08-11): the v1 fissure rock catalog, cooking effects, Lithovore — out of design conversations until explicitly reopened. RULED markers in both still count as provenance - grep all three files.

**TEXT + PUNCTUATION HARD RULES (hardened 2026-08-15 at the user's direction, "i feel like we should harden that ruling"). Two rules that look alike and are not the same thing:**

1. **NO EM DASHES IN SHIPPED TEXT** — anything a player reads: `manifest.json` description, blueprint `Description`/`DisplayName`, ability and level text, popups, message-queue lines. Standing since the manifest description sitting; it had lived only in session-prompt docs and one buried manifest note, which is why it kept needing re-stating. **Scope answered 2026-08-15 (user asked): it was NEVER a code rule.** Comments, commit messages and tracker prose are free — and stay free, because the strip pass deletes comments from published builds anyway.
2. **NO `--` ANYWHERE INSIDE AN XML COMMENT** — not style, *syntax*: XML forbids it outright and the file fails to parse. This is the curse that has now struck **TEN times** (twice in the 2026-08-15 sitting alone, both times reaching for an em-dash-flavored aside and typing `--`). Write "i.e.", a semicolon, a comma, or a real — character instead.

**Enforcement, so neither rule depends on memory again:** `tools\check-text-rules.ps1` fails closed on both, scanning every mod XML, .cs and the manifest. It runs inside `publish-beta.ps1` before anything is zipped or staged, and should be run before any deploy. Strict XML validation already catches rule 2 at parse time; the checker catches it *and* names the file and line, and catches rule 1 which nothing was checking at all.

**Tags:** `[bug]` broken behavior · `[polish]` text/UX cleanup · `[design]` needs a creative/balance decision · `[verify]` needs in-game confirmation · `[stretch]` stretch goal

**How we work (2026-07-21):** each mutation gets **one audit pass** — knock out its small fixes, settle its design questions, restyle its description text (base-game voice, see style notes under Gorgon's Gaze), then mark it ready to verify. In-game verification happens in batches, not per-fix. ~~Fissure is vetoed until every other mutation's audit is complete.~~ **Veto condition met; lifted 2026-07-22 — Fissure is the active workstream.**

**Provenance discipline (2026-07-25, after the "gear-warfare doesn't cook" creep — a Claude
proposal hardened into a cited "rule" through nothing but repetition):** every design-shaping
statement in the trackers carries a status tag — **RULED** (date + the user's words) · **LEAN**
(user's, explicitly not a ruling) · **CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED**. Citing a rule as precedent
requires finding its RULED marker; no marker = it's a proposal and gets cited as one. Scope:
design claims only — delegated code/plumbing decisions need no ratification ceremony.

The family lives on as granitefur on the `items` trunk, TF2-bound; the granite baboon there (renamed 2026-08-22, troop-wired) is untouched — the pull is master-side only. `publish-beta.ps1` green end to end after the widening (text-rule check OK, 53 .cs stripped, 14 .xml stripped + strict-valid, compile check clean): `publish\CoQ_TerraFirma_beta_2026-08-29.zip` + `Mods\TerraFirmaBeta` restaged. Verified in the staged copy: zero cobble/cobblefur hits outside the unrelated caromcobble, part files absent, manifest 0.1.2.5 id `Cleo_TerraFirmaBeta`, `workshop.json` parses (WorkshopId 3778701376 intact). **TWO-MOD SPLIT RULED 2026-08-16.** User, verbatim: *"i'm thinking about having the current mod be a mutations only one at the end of the day, and releasing a new one with the stonecarvers proper as it's own one"* -> *"yeag that's what i was thinking hahah, everything already there stays there."* **Shape: base + dependent expansion, additive only.** Terra Firma stays the base and KEEPS EVERYTHING IT HAS ALREADY SHIPPED; the stonecarver expansion is a second workshop item that only ADDS. No removals, ever, from the base -- that clause is the user's and it is the load-bearing one, because the Stonecarver calling is already live in subscribers' games and advertised on the page, so splitting it out would strip a subtype off characters mid-run. **The boundary is therefore the SHIPPED SET, and it was measured against the 0.1.2.2 artifact at the ruling, not guessed.** BASE KEEPS: all 7 mutations; the stone spine (`Rocks.xml` at 57KB, larger than every other XML combined, plus `Objects.xml`, `Merges.xml`, the Fissure tables in `Populations.xml`, the `IsAnyStone` tag, and `_Helpers.cs` which 41 of 56 scripts call into); the calling SHELL (stats, skills, gear -- the rep block is branch-side and has never shipped); etching (`Part_Etchable.cs`); the gated mill + cooking code; figurines and the maquette tables; the four inert creature wish-doors. EXPANSION GETS ONLY WHAT HAS NEVER SHIPPED: `Builder_StonecarverEpithet.cs` (the single unshipped script in the whole repo, zip-verified), the Quarriers' Guild faction and the calling's rep grant, the carver NPC roster and conversations, and all spawn wiring per the standing no-debut ruling. **KEY CONSEQUENCE FOUND AT THE RULING: the cross-mod C# dependency shrinks to one file, and it is not load-bearing.** Direction was checked both ways first -- no mutation script references anything carver-side (grep-clean), so the mutations were always the separable half. The reverse coupling was two files: `Part_Etchable.cs` reads `Cleo_TerraFirma_StoneshaperWallProperties` by type, and the epithet builder calls `new Cleo_TerraFirma_Fissure()` and three siblings. Etching stays in the base, so that one becomes intra-mod and disappears. The epithet builder's four `new` calls are the only survivors, and TWO string-based escape hatches already exist in this codebase: `Mutation_Stoneshaper.cs:667` uses the `AddMutation(string, int)` overload, and `<mutation Name="Cleo_TerraFirma_Fissure" Level="4" />` resolves by CLASS NAME from XML (the exact mechanism the 08-14 cobble baboon bug taught us). Either one dissolves the last hard type reference, so the expansion can be built needing zero mutation types. `[open]` Whether Qud supports cross-mod C# references at all is STILL UNTESTED -- no manifest on disk carries a dependency or load-order field (all 5 checked). It is no longer blocking, only nice to know. `[open]` Names for the two workshop items. `[open]` Timing relative to 0.1.3. `[open]` The straddle: shipped creature blueprints live in the base while their spawn table entries would live in the expansion. XML merges across mods far more readily than C#, so this is expected to be fine, but it is untested too. `[open]` `publish-beta.ps1` is single-mod; a second item needs its own id, preview, staging folder and publish path. *(Preview half softened 2026-08-27, USER LEAN: "TF2 workshop will probably just use a placeholder like the mutations do, honestly" — so the preview is a copy task, not owed art.)* *(Recorded on the `creatures` branch because that is where the carver work lives; belongs in the release section on master at merge.)*

**SPLIT SCOPE RE-RULED 2026-08-26 (user, verbatim: "scope is everything *not* mutations for the second mod. so all items, behaviors, chargen characters, pets, creatures, etc go in terra firma 2").** FULL MOVE — this SUPERSEDES the 08-16 shipped-set boundary ("everything already there stays there"): the base ends up mutations-only, and the shipped non-mutation pieces (Stonecarver calling, boar vessel, burrowing urchin, etching, figurines/maquette, the four creature wish-doors) eventually leave it too. Working name for the second item: **Terra Firma 2** (user's phrasing at the ruling; confirm as final at the workshop-names call). **TRANSITION RULED same sitting, from the offered menu ("full move, with a transition plan"), window shape settled in the follow-up (user weighed strip-at-beta-drop vs leave-until-beta-done, affirmed the latter: "that sounds fine"):** the base keeps everything it has shipped THROUGH the TF2 beta; the TF2 beta ships only never-shipped content additively on top (which keeps the 08-16 seam analysis valid for the beta period and avoids double-defined blueprints); the strip happens ONCE, at TF2 1.0, announced directly to subscribers and in the patch notes. Owed before the strip, all flagged CLAUDE at the ruling: (1) live-save handoff test — a save with a Stonecarver character must survive the calling's definition moving from the base to TF2; (2) double-load collision test during the beta window; (3) C# seam re-measure at the new boundary — the 08-16 grep-clean verdict assumed etching/figurines/calling stayed base-side, and with them moving, TF2 code calls base-side `_Helpers.cs` and reads `Cleo_TerraFirma_StoneshaperWallProperties` by type while cross-mod C# remains untested (string-based escape hatches on record). **SEAM RE-MEASURED 2026-08-27 (read-only recon, all 24 TF2-bound scripts scanned member-by-member): the seam is SOFT — no load-bearing cross-mod C# exists.** The `_Helpers` coupling (21 of 24 files) is `VerifyLog` instrumentation almost entirely (plus one `DebugLog`, one `SERIAL_SENTINEL`); TF2 ships its own tiny logging shim and that whole coupling vanishes — the 08-16 "41 of 56 scripts call _Helpers" scare does not apply to the TF2 set. Remaining hard type refs, each with a cheap out: `StoneshaperWallProperties` read by type in 4 files (Etchable / MoleCricket / StatueMaker / WarrenCarver) → string-based part lookup; mutation types in 3 (epithet builder — string hatches already on record; AnimationTable + StatueMaker call the statue-law statics `GetHP`/`GetAV` → duplicate the two one-line formulas TF2-side); `Fx_ChalkReveal` rides `Fx_StoneRise`'s pooled-sprite chassis → duplicate the small chassis classes it uses. One architectural check rides the existing double-load test: whether two mods may each define same-named classes in their own assemblies (per-mod compilation argues yes; rename TF2's copies if not). Verdict: the split needs a mechanical rewrite pass, not an engine gamble. Derived tracking view: the "Part 2 Docket" artifact (2026-08-26); the journal stays canon.

**CREATURES BRANCH OPENED 2026-08-15 (user: "set up a branch for the creatures, including stonecarvers, so i can generate them in game"):** git branch `creatures`, cut from `185a98c` right after the 0.1.2 rig. Contract lives in `design/creatures-branch-2026-08-15.md` **on the branch** — read it before working there. Short version: all spawn wiring and the stonecarver roster build happen on the branch, because master's no-debut guarantee currently holds *by construction* (every mod creature is `ExcludeFromDynamicEncounters` with zero table entries) and the first real table entry would end that. **Scope RULED at the sitting: staging wish first, wiring after** — the branch's first landing is `tfcreatures`, which lines the roster up frozen and pacified for a look (cobble baboon · rockhound · snapjaw miner · burrowing urchin · Husangeion), plus two SPRITE CHECKS painting `stonecarver.png` / `stonerat.png` onto plain human bodies at runtime, since no stonecarver NPC blueprint exists yet and the roster color map still has unruled slots. Nothing about the roster is committed by that. **Deploy discipline: one live dev copy.** `Mods\TerraFirma` currently runs the CREATURES BRANCH build (so the wish is in game next launch); `Mods\TerraFirmaBeta` holds the clean 0.1.2 upload source and is untouched by dev deploys, so the workshop push does not need master re-deployed. To get back to a patch build: `git checkout master`, re-mirror `TerraFirma\` into `Mods\TerraFirma`. **Bookkeeping catch from the same sitting: the derived tracker's "malformed cragmensch — built + deployed" line is STALE** — there is no cragmensch blueprint in the shipped XML at all; it lives in the quarantined v1 catalog. Not in the staging wish for that reason.

- **const → XML-settable public field** (no behavior change, defaults identical): `Part_ChalkLine.TrampleSmearChance` (5), `Part_GrinderCell.ChargePerPound` (50) + `TricklePerTurn` (10, both joined `SameAs` so differently-dialed cells refuse to merge), `Part_GlowsphereBreak.ShardBlueprint`, `Part_GlowstoneLantern.ShardBlueprint`, `Part_AnimationTable.WalkerBlueprint` (was a literal in two method bodies), `Part_ChalkBox.UsesMin`/`UsesMax` (8/12 creation roll, user number preserved), `Part_MossyWall.Adjective` ("{{green|mossy}}", part now reusable per growth wall), `Part_Cairn.ToppleSound` (a field, NOT the stock `ImpactSound` tag: the sound plays via `PlayWorldSound` in the topple handler, so the thrown-weapon tag seam would never fire; cairn `Owner` left alone, its deferral pending a warren faction is documented intent, not debt).

The four beats, new `Scripts/Fx_GravelWhorlFX.cs` + call sites in Effect_GravelWhorl.cs: **(1) DEVOUR — the user's own concept, their words: the projectile "dissolves into a mass of pixels, swirling in a vaguely circular mass around the tile of whoever has the mutation," rendering RULED brazier-style ("like how the fire on braziers does it's texture - a mass of pixels masquerading as the effect"), linger RULED 1-2 revolutions** — 12 pooled-sprite flecks at 0.10-0.18 scale (indistinct pixel clumps; a third wear the devoured projectile's OWN tile), palette-thieved from the live Render (TileColor-first per the carve-reveal lesson), fg/detail channels swapping on a 10Hz flicker (AnimatedMaterialFire's cycler translated to sprite channels), one coherent spin, 1.6 revolutions over 0.9s, ease-out dissolve in + shrink-to-nothing out; petrify-chunk lifecycle verbatim (turn-sweep declined, park-behind-map, pool-scale restore). **FEEL ROUND 1, same sitting (user sketch): the swirl is a SPHERE, not a ring ("instead of a circle, the whorl represents as a sphere") + axis TILT ("the top axis of the globe is tilted slightly to the left") — REBUILT + REDEPLOYED:** 16 flecks each riding their own latitude band (asin ladder = even surface coverage), band radius shrinking to the poles, depth faked by scale (back flecks 0.6x) and z-order within the chunk layer, whole frame rolled ~12° so the top pole leans left (TILT_RAD, CLAUDE dial). **Feel rounds 2-3 (user): fleck scale 0.10-0.18 → 0.18-0.30 → 0.22-0.34, round 3 "then that's good" — SIZE SETTLED. Gate = the effect's own `IsLightProjectile`. Reserve note: devoured lasers NEVER fed the reserve (+1 is IsAnyStone-gated) — the user's no-stone lean was already true mechanically, flagged to re-check if a live +1 is ever observed. Sampler beat now the split at 3NE. BUILT + DEPLOYED.** **Feel round 6 (user, first working look: "the laser line effect would be better if done with pixels like for our whorl"): split streams moved from tangent glyphs to FLECK SPRITES — new `Cleo_TerraFirma_JuiceWhorlArc`, a 5-fleck train per side traveling the arc (50ms stagger so it reads as a stream), bolt-color/white flicker, shrink-out over the last 30% of traversal, radius just outside the devour sphere (18/14px); both devour beats now speak the same pixel-fleck material. Entry flash stays a glyph. BUILT + DEPLOYED.** **Feel round 7 (user: "it's a bit elliptical, and the laser still visually hits the player rather than the devour effect intercepting it - how feasible is that?"): (1) arc radii equalized 18/14 → 16/16 — the parting is a circle now. (2) TRUE interception RULED OUT with reasons on record: the beam is a Unity prefab (`MissileWeaponsEffects/medium_laser`, 0.25s) drawn by the engine to the impact cell — the player's own cell, where the whorl lives; shortening the drawn endpoint needs a Harmony patch into the missile-VFX assembly, which collides with the standing de-Harmony direction. Sold by OVERDRAW instead: 4-fleck rim splash at the entry point facing the shooter, zero delay, timed against the beam's fade. (3) Color find riding the same blueprint read: the laser rifle bolt RENDERS cyan but its beam prefab draws #FF0000/#FF8000 — the split now parses beamColor0/1 from `ProjectileVFXConfiguration` and the streams flicker the beam's own red/orange (char-based theft stays the fallback for VFX-less projectiles). BUILT + DEPLOYED.** **Feel round 8 — the PIVOT TAKEN, user's own concept (after "worst case... rework the animation to not be the splitting ring thing", then: "what if instead we utilize the pixels produced by the end of the laser shot effect itself?"): the splitting ring is GONE, replaced by the EMBER-CATCH — flecks born AT the beam's terminus (the impact cell the engine insists on drawing to), in the beam prefab's own beamColor0/1, the instant the hit resolves, then spiraling OUTWARD with coherent whorl-spin and shrinking out (~126° over 0.5s, 8 embers, 30ms stagger). Perceptual continuity: the shot's own dying pixels seized and spun apart — no interception claim anywhere, so nothing for the engine's draw to contradict. (Literal hijacking of the prefab's particle instances was considered and rejected: live Unity scene surgery, the exact hackiness the user vetoed.) `JuiceWhorlArc` generalized with radius lerp (R0→R1; equal pairs = the old fixed arc). Rounds 5-7's ring machinery superseded; rim splash deleted. `PlayDevourSplit` renamed `PlayDevourLight`; sampler beat now the ember-catch at 3NE. Caster-only exemption stands.** Root cause decomp-verified: the engine's base PV of 4 is `RuleSettings.VISUAL_PENETRATION_BONUS` — DISPLAY-ONLY, appended in weapon name-lines and mutation stat text, never rolled (`MeleeWeapon.GetNormalPenetration` = PenBonus 0 + capped Str mod; Stinger/LightManipulation add the 4 only in text). Our ruled "PV 4 + Str, the melee-weapon grammar" fed display units to `RollDamagePenetrations`: at Str mod 3 the wave rolled +7 — a weapon reading ~↔11 — and +7 vs AV 2 cannot fail a pen die. Fix: `GetCreaturePV()` = Strength mod ALONE (true melee grammar; any future PV text adds the visual 4 back). Wall carve PV (6+3L flat) knowingly LEFT AS-IS: tuned as its own dial with verified outcomes, not claiming melee grammar. EB clean (rides engine weapon parts). Level-scaled bonus stays rejected; MaxStrengthBonus-style cap stays reserved. `[verify]` next boot: [FIS] hit lines show pens varying (0-pens now possible vs armored), chaff no longer guaranteed-erased by pulse 1.

## Fresh-air gear trio — COMMITTED 2026-08-20 (promoted from the 08-18 jam; user: "i think we can commit the chisel as an item worth building" + "let's sketch out the shape of 2 other items going in")

- [ ] `[design]` **Attendant chisel → REPLACED by the symbiotic mole cricket (user reflavor ruling 2026-08-22; built same day, see the New-item-descriptions block below). This entry retained as design history.** — tier 2 Floating Nearby, shape USER 2026-08-20 ("a chisel that randomly digs and sometimes attacks enemies based on adjacency *could* work. could be a tier 2 floating nearby, right in stonecarver range"). Ground truth: slot = `Armor WornOn="Floating Nearby"` (base Items.xml, glowsphere grammar); base's tier 1 AND tier 2 floating-nearby comment headers are EMPTY — the slot gap is real. CLAUDE build sketch, unruled details: one roll per turn — adjacent hostile → small peck (~1d2, stone sfx, damage ATTRIBUTED TO WEARER, the Quills-attribution idiom, so XP/aggro stay sane) else adjacent `Diggable` wall → chip damage, else idle; carries `Cleo_TerraFirma_EtchTool` tag (satisfies the chisel half of the etch gate — the item's rent). Poptable seed idea (USER MUSING same day, unruled): uncommon in stonecarver poptables, alongside seeding base structural scanning bracelets rare there (user: "we could have structural scanning bracelets be a rare/uncommon spawn in stonecarver poptables hmmm"). All player-facing text DRAFT/user.
- [ ] `[design]` **Unwritten armor** — body slot (USER CONCEPT 2026-08-18: "unwritten armor that hardens when bled on"; the mud+blood chestplate). Ground truth: `XRL.World.Effects.Bleeding` decompiled — per-turn damage effect, stacks by raising SaveTarget, gated on the `Bleeds` intproperty. CLAUDE sketch: low base AV; a part on the armor watches the wearer's bleed ticks and accrues hardening per tick, AV bonus stepping in tiers via StatShifter (Quills idiom, arrow reversed). OPEN FORKS (user's to rule): shed condition (full heal / timed / knocked off by big hits) · cap size · reads bleed damage vs bleed turns · NPC wearers.
  - **RENAMED + FULLY SHAPED 2026-08-22 (design sitting, in chat): "unwritten armor" is now the MUDSKIN CARAPACE (user: "renamed the unwritten armor"; the mudskin naming-adjacency flag, sibling to the recorded mudblood flag, was re-raised at the rename and rides along — naming reopenable, user's domain).** Paired at the same sitting with the **FLINT-SPIKED DOUBLET** (USER CONCEPT: "a flint-spiked doublet or something that makes you do a bleed save when attacked. couple that with the mudskin carapace ... you have a texture for fights, but also a backdrop for the culture itself"). Both C#-allowed by the user for the chest slot. Same slot BY DESIGN: the coupling expresses across bodies (one body can never wear both), which implicitly answers the NPC-wearers fork YES. Every fork RULED at the sitting:
    - **Carapace statline RULED: 1 AV / +1 DV bare, bleed cap +2 AV** (user: "i could see 1AV/1DV for the carapace even tbh, and then have +2 as the bleed cap so situationally it can give more AV while innately having more DV"). Hardened ceiling 3 AV / +1 DV; DV constant throughout (the earlier DV-stiffening micro-fork is retired by this shape).
    - **Shed RULED: timed decay; hardening persists "until the mud flakes off"; flake STEPS DOWN** (-1 per interval; user: "step down is fine"). **Legibility via a STATUS EFFECT** (user float "we could have it as a status for legibility?", affirmed: the accrual lives as an Effect the armor part applies — the mod's own effect-shelf idiom — giving status-column stack count, look text, StatShifter home, save/load). Effect name + all strings USER, DRAFT placeholders till supplied.
    - **Metric: per bleed TICK** (Claude lean; user weighed it: "per bleed tick encourages you to bleed more, which could be good or bad") — the incentive is self-taxing: riding a bleed costs HP per tick and base stacking raises the stop-save, so the risk-play is priced by base law.
    - **Doublet statline RULED: 2 AV / -1 DV** (user: "i lean 2AV/-1DV and 0AV/2DV" — the carapace half of that lean was superseded same sitting by the 1/1 shape above).
    - **Doublet trigger RULED: ANY ADJACENT MELEE** (user: "adjacent melee reads fine to me"), over Quills' stricter unarmed-only gate, chosen for the culture loop: armed carvers trigger it, so guild infights let doublet families feed carapace families. Ranged and thrown are excluded by the adjacency check — the mod's own rock-throwing playstyle is never punished by its own armor.
    - **Build shape (CLAUDE, delegated plumbing):** doublet = `TookDamageEvent` (Quills' decomp-verified retaliation hook) applying base `Effects.Bleeding` to the attacker, Amount `1d3` / SaveTarget `20 + 2×Tier` (ModSpiked's own decompiled dials — the part that was slot-illegal on a body returns as the number source); bloodless attackers immune via the `Bleeds` gate (free texture: stone doesn't cut on stone). Decomp owed at build, bookkeeping only: whether natural weapons carry the Unarmed damage attribute.
    - **NOT SCHEDULED:** shapes set, build docket unopened. Doublet name still sits near base "studded leather armor" — user's to settle.
    - **NAMES RE-RULED same sitting, superseding both working names above (user, arriving as the sitting logged: "lets have them be flint-spike pauldrons, and mudskin girder. this reduces overlap with basegame items i think"): FLINT-SPIKE PAULDRONS and MUDSKIN GIRDER.** The overlap instinct is right twice over: "doublet" sat next to base studded leather armor (the line above is settled by this), and "carapace" collided with the base Carapace mutation. Read every doublet/carapace mention in this entry as these two items. Slots UNCHANGED: both remain BODY per the sitting's coupling law, whatever the names now evoke. "Girder" was a typo, fixed on the flag same sitting (user: "we can fix that to girdle") — the name is **MUDSKIN GIRDLE**. The mudskin naming-adjacency flag rides along unchanged.

## New item descriptions — USER TEXT, FINAL 2026-08-22

**RULED 2026-08-22 (user: "okay, done, log these").** Seven locked description strings, user's own voice, delivered across a three-pass revision loop. These are FINAL shipped text: swap verbatim into blueprint `Description` `Short=` fields when each item is built. Do not polish, re-draft, or "improve" any of them per the standing user-voice rule. Mechanics for all seven are UNDESIGNED as of this entry — the user's own framing at delivery was that the descriptions were locked first and the "what each does" block comes separately. Nothing here rules a mechanic.

- **lantern (lit with glowstone shard)** — `a wrought metal cage encloses a flickering shard.`
- **glowstone shard** — `a perfect pearl of light was shattered, yet no life springs forth from it. its glow wanes in the harvest dawn, memories of wholeness reflected in the high salt sun.`
- **symbiotic mole cricket** — `a hopping, diminutive thing scrapes idly at the earth. at hindsun it hums, biological processes becoming musical.`
- **seismophone** — `a thin shim of wire encloses a whispering ear. when pressed against the earth, it speaks.`
- **grinder cell** — `a rumbling spiral milks out bursts of piezoelectric energy from chunks of earth.`
- **cairn** — `a waystone to those with eyes to the earth. it rests upon the path, watching each traveler passing by.`
- **disrupted cairn** — `a waystone once sat here. now, it rests from its watch, humble stones returned to the earth.`

**EVENING RE-DELIVERY 2026-08-22 (user; capitalized per the case-convention ruling): three of the strings above SUPERSEDED, one name SETTLED.**

- **glowsphere fragment** (the "glowstone shard" entry above, post-rename) — `A razor shard of a shattered phosphorescent opal-like weeps light that drifts away like smoke. Its glow is waning, but, struck by the high salt sun, it yet reflects memories of wholeness.` SWAPPED verbatim into the live blueprint (Objects.xml). **Flagged once, unanswered at logging: "opal-like" reads like it may be missing a word ("a shattered phosphorescent opal-like weeps light"); stands verbatim per the user-voice rule until said otherwise.** Lowercase "high salt sun" carries over from the original string, the user's standing call.
- **cairn** — `A waystone for those with eyes to the earth, this stack of rocks inhabits an unresolved superposition of endurance and fragility.` Journal-held; blueprint unbuilt.
- **toppled cairn** — `These scattered rocks once formed a waystone; now, only traces of their former order remain.` Journal-held. **The name "toppled cairn" SETTLES the "disrupted(?) cairn" naming thread below** (the query-mark note is answered; "disrupted cairn" retires).
- The recorded rests/watch echo between the old cairn pair is RETIRED by this re-delivery; the preserve-it note below is answered, not deleted (provenance).
- **box of chalk, same evening — USER TEXT FINAL, swapped into the live blueprint verbatim:** `Fragmented tubes of lime sketch out concepts, ideoforms, and diagrams, only to be smeared away by the passage of time.` Supersedes the lowercase "figments of lime" string; the box-of-chalk description debt from the original batch is retired.

### Ground truth checked against Base during the revision passes (Claude, 2026-08-22)

- **`hindsun` is stock Qud vocabulary**, not coinage — 5 uses in Base, incl. `LibraryCorpus.json` and `ObjectBlueprints/Items.xml`.
- **`high salt sun` is stock**, and the corpus capitalizes it as "the High Salt Sun" (`Books.xml`, `Conversations.xml`, `HistorySpice.json`, `LibraryCorpus.json`). The lowercase form in the shard text is the user's call and stands.
- **`harvest dawn` is stock**, and the base line it appears in reads "He'd spent from harvest dawn to the peaking of the high salt sun" — i.e. the shard's dawn→noon span reproduces the base game's own diurnal phrasing, in the same order. Arrived at independently by the user across two passes.
- **`waystone` is NOT in Base.** Clean coinage, contradicts nothing.
- **`aluminum` is the Base spelling** (16 uses, zero "aluminium"). Moot in the final grinder cell string, which dropped the walls clause, but recorded for future gear text.
- **`traveler` is the Base house spelling** — 123 American vs 9 British, and the 9 are conversation lines plus procedurally generated corpus, not authored item text. **The final string above uses `traveler`, matching Base.** An intermediate pass carried the British form; the user corrected it before lock.
- **`diminutive` matches Base** (8 uses). Two intermediate passes carried misspellings; the final string is correct.

### Grinder cell — precedent + thread collision

Base ships a whole `Cell` family in `ObjectBlueprints/Items.xml`: Antimatter, Biodynamic, Chem, Combustion, Lead-Acid, Nuclear, Solar, Thermoelectric, and **Fidget Cell**. Fidget Cell is the kinetic-harvest sibling and the closest mechanical neighbour to anything a grinder cell would do — `EquipCharge` with `NameForStatus="KineticConvertor"`, `ChargeRate="2"` rising to `CombatChargeRate="20"`, `Charge/MaxCharge 2500`, worn on Arm. Its description opens on the mechanism, not on the object ("Spinning the stannic cuboid about any of its axes of rotation generates a pinch of resistance"), and Thermoelectric Cell uses the family's house verb: "cracks the energy out of lava". The user's "milks out bursts of ... from chunks of earth" sits inside that register by construction.

**Thread collision to resolve at pickup:** a grinder cell already exists in this tracker as **Pending design budget → Fresh-air jam top 5, item 3** ("energy cell recharged by feeding it rubble", CLAUDE PROPOSAL, user-earmarked 2026-08-18). The locked description above attaches to that entry rather than opening a second one. Whether the final mechanic is feed-it-rubble (the parked proposal) or ambient harvest off the churning earth (what the description's "rumbling spiral" implies) is UNRULED and the user's to settle.

### Mechanics — USER 2026-08-22, shapes set (numbers and forks still open)

Delivered by the user in one block the same sitting the descriptions locked. Everything below marked USER is the user's own shape and is RULED as direction; everything marked CLAUDE is recon or a flagged fork, unruled. **Two of the five land on top of already-committed tracker entries; see the collision notes.**

**glowstone shard + lantern — USER:** "we hijack the existing glowspheres, add a break option on them or a condition that breaks them. the shards will slowly deplete in light over time and list their status in their name like luminous mote iirc. it could then be loaded into a lantern, which would increase its light radius and make it last slightly longer."

- **The remembered part is real and is exactly right.** `MoteProperties` (decompiled) is the depleting-light part: `Fuel = 5000` ticking down one per turn while `Lit`; radius stepping **6 / 5 / 4 / 3** at fuel brackets 3500 / 2000 / 500; `GetDisplayNameEvent` appends a parenthetical status tag from `getLitDescription()` — **`{{Y|blazing}}` / `{{W|bright}}` / `dim` / `{{K|faint}}`**; at zero it prints "your mote dissipates" and `Destroy()`s. A mod part copying this shape gets the whole depletion loop, the name tag, and the death message for free.
- Base `Glowsphere` (`Items.xml`): `Inherits="LightSource"`, `LightSource Lit="true" Radius="8"`, `GlowsphereProperties AllowedParts="Hand"`, `Commerce Value="40"`, `Gift *noinherit`. **No fuel of any kind — glowsphere light is permanent.**
- **CLAUDE, FLAGGED — the radius ceiling is a real problem.** Base portable light tops out at **8** and glowsphere is already there: `Floating Glowsphere` 8, `Quantum Mote` 8, all `BaseTierFloating3-8` 8, `Miner's Helmet` 6, `Torch` 4, `Headlamp` 3, `Symbiotic Firefly` 4, `Luminous Mote` 4. The only thing above it in the whole game is `BaseNephilimLightCircle` at 16. So "lantern increases its light radius" has **nowhere to go that is not above the base game's portable-light ceiling.** And the trade as described is currently strictly negative: the player breaks a permanent radius-8 light into a depleting one, and the lantern has to beat radius 8 forever just to get back to even. Either the lantern goes to 9+ (ceiling break, a real economy decision), or the payoff moves off radius entirely (the shard does something a glowsphere cannot). **User's call, unruled — but the shape as stated does not pay for itself.**
- **CLAUDE, open forks:** does the shard inherit `DestroyOnUnequip` like Mote and Firefly do (that fights being loadable into a lantern) · does the lantern burn the shard's own Fuel or hold its own · does a spent shard leave anything · break verb vs breaking condition (user offered both) · whether hijacking base Glowsphere means a merge onto the base blueprint or a mod-side variant.

**symbiotic mole cricket — USER:** "reflavoring the attendant chisel, a floating nearby item that randomly does digging attacks on walls or weak claw attacks on creatures (i'm thinking like, 1d2?), and *maybe* sometimes etches walls with a random phrase. behavior would work like symbiotic firefly."

- **COLLISION, RESOLVED BY THE USER: this REPLACES the Attendant chisel** in the committed **Fresh-air gear trio** section above. Same slot, same one-roll-per-turn shape, same 1d2, reflavored from tool to creature. The chisel entry's build sketch carries over; treat that entry as this item's history rather than a second item. **One thing does not carry over cleanly: the chisel held the `Cleo_TerraFirma_EtchTool` tag as "the item's rent" toward the two-tool etch gate.** A live cricket satisfying a chisel-and-mallet requirement is a flavor question the user has not ruled.
- Base `Symbiotic Firefly` (`Items.xml`) as the named register precedent: `Inherits="LightSource"`, `Armor AV="0" DV="0" WornOn="Floating Nearby"`, `Physics Organic="true"`, `Springy Factor="0.5"`, **`DestroyOnUnequip`**, `Tier 2`, `Commerce 400`, `AddsRep Faction="Insects" Value="400"`, `stag Floating`, `tag LiveAnimal`, and a `RulesDescription` that states the permanence plainly: "Extinguished permanently when unequipped."
- **CLAUDE, flagged:** `DestroyOnUnequip` is the load-bearing half of the firefly's register and it makes the item **one-use** — unequip it once and it is gone. If the cricket inherits that, it is a consumable, not gear. If it does not, it is not really working like a symbiotic firefly. Either answer is fine; the user should pick knowingly. Also: `AddsRep Faction="Insects"` is the base pattern for wearing a live animal, and **this is the first thing in the set that could pay off the unexplained "symbiotic" in the name** (a rep tie, or the hindsun hum in the description doing something).
- **BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-22 (rulings same sitting: "yes it inherits destroy on unequip - and yes my idea is that it sometimes digs a wall, *maybe* sometimes etches a wall, and sometimes attacks for a small dice roll (thinking something like 1d2)"; compiles green, XML strict-valid, hash-verified; user sprite `mole_cricket.png`):** `Part_MoleCricket.cs` + `Cleo_TerraFirma_MoleCricket` (Floating Nearby, firefly chassis: Organic, Springy, **`DestroyOnUnequip` BY RULING**, LiveAnimal, `AddsRep` **RULED 2026-08-22: `Insects,Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarriers` at 200 each** (user: "+200 rep with insects, +200 rep with stonecarvers"; base comma-list idiom, Birds/Winged-Mammals precedent) — the rep tie pays the "symbiotic" in the name toward both keepers, plus a firefly-style shimmer sequence in the display name). One roll per turn while worn: adjacent hostile → peck **1d2 (USER NUMBER)**, damage attributed to the wearer (Quills idiom); else adjacent NATURAL stone wall (`StoneshaperWallProperties Natural=true`, the carve gate's own marker — earth, not blast doors) → scrape. CLAUDE DIALS: peck 35 in100 · dig 25 in100 · scrape 2d4. **DIG IDLE-GATED (RULED same sitting, "idle gating works i think", after the pacing talk-through: at a flat 25%/turn a cave-walking wearer smeared shallow damage everywhere and a 100-turn rest breached a 150-HP wall by accident):** dig now requires the wearer STANDING STILL with NO TARGET (position tracked turn-over-turn in the part) — "scrapes IDLY at the earth", the locked description's own word. Walking = no wall damage; the campsite divot is chosen, not accidental. Peck untouched (self-limiting: only rolls in active melee). **Etch rider framing, logged for IF it gets ruled in (user's worry: "it's weird for a bug to be carving words into a wall, right?" — Claude assessment: rarity doesn't fix category-weirdness, fidelity does):** cricket etch = markov source obfuscated HARD (70-90% slip vs tier 0's 24%), so what lands is glyph-hash scratchings with the odd surviving word — a symbiote imitating its keepers and getting it nearly all wrong, not an animal writing. Still UNRULED; the maybe stands. **The *maybe* etch rider NOT BUILT** — still the user's maybe, twice running; it rides the idle-etch refactor below. **Etch-gate rent moot:** the gate is knives-and-hammers now, so the cricket carries no `EtchTool` duty. Rules text trimmed to firefly register, USER-APPROVED 2026-08-22 ("that's a fine edit to the line"): "Sometimes pecks at enemies and scrapes at stone. / Perishes when unequipped." Wish `tfcricket`. `[verify]` next boot: worn cricket pecks an adjacent hostile for 1d2 with XP to the wearer (`[TF-VERIFY] [CRICKET]`) · scrapes shale ONLY while standing still with no target (walk a corridor: zero `[CRICKET]` scrape lines; stand still: they appear) · dig target varies between sittings instead of always upper-left, and stays on one wall until it falls. **Live-catch, same sitting (user, testing the pre-idle-gate build: "it seems to always prefer diagonal left upper left tiles if it can choose, and continues digging on them until they're destroyed?"): diagnosed as first-found cell-iteration bias (`GetLocalAdjacentCells` hands upper-left first), not a preference. FIXED: initial pick now RANDOM among all adjacent natural walls, then LOYAL to that wall until destroyed or out of reach (`DigTarget` field) — the commit half was kept deliberately, it is what concentrates a campsite divot instead of smearing eight faces. Compiled green, redeployed; both this and the idle gate activate on next restart.** · refuses metal/constructed walls · unequip destroys it · rep +200 Insects AND +200 Quarriers’ Guild while worn. **USER-TESTED 2026-08-22 ("already tested the cricket") — behavior confirmed post-idle-gate; the rep split and rules-text trim ride the next restart.** **REP SPLIT VERIFIED 2026-08-23 (user: "the rep on cricket looks good"). Perish-message flag same sitting (user: "the perish message when removed might need to be changed") — FOUND + FIXED: `DestroyOnUnequip`'s DEFAULT message is "The mote of light is extinguished." (decomp; neither base firefly nor our blueprint overrode it), so the cricket died with a light's eulogy. Now `Message="Your symbiotic mole cricket perishes."` — DRAFT, verb from the user-approved rules line; user rewords at will. Deployed, XML strict-valid, text-rules green.** `[verify]` next boot: unequip the cricket, read the new line.
- The *maybe* etch-a-random-phrase rider is the **idle-etch brain part** thread (USER FLOAT 2026-08-11) arriving on an item instead of an NPC. The markov commit path and the `AttemptEtch`-refactor note recorded there apply directly; so does its recorded guard, that rarity paces but cannot cap, so a per-zone etch budget is the real ceiling.

**seismophone — USER:** "grants heightened hearing when you're adjacent to a wall and it's powered."

- **COLLISION: this is the committed Wall-ear entry** in the Fresh-air gear trio, now named and given a firing condition. The user's 2026-08-20 direction there ("a sort contact microphone or wall penetrating acoustic amplifier, a powered piece of machinery") is the same item; **seismophone is presumed to be the name replacing "wall-ear", but the user has not said so in words.** Treat the wall-ear entry as this item's design history.
- **What is genuinely new: the grant is now CONDITIONAL on wall adjacency.** The recorded build shape there was `MutationOnEquip ClassName="HeightenedHearing"` + `EnergyCellSocket` + `PowerSwitch`, which is a *static* grant that toggles only with power. Wall adjacency means the mutation has to be added and removed as the player moves, so the plumbing changes: a part watching movement, checking the eight neighbours for a solid/diggable cell, and adding/removing the grant. **CLAUDE note:** flickering a mutation on and off every step wants a message-spam guard and a check that HeightenedHearing's per-turn detect does not misbehave when the grant appears mid-turn. `HeightenedHearing.GetRadius = 3 + 2 x Level` and the engine's search being Combat-objects-only both still stand from the wall-ear recon; the liquid-ping part recorded there is still the answer for the aquifer half of the user's original pitch.
- The description ("when pressed against the earth, it speaks") now reads as a rules hint rather than pure flavor, which is a good sign the name and the mechanic are converging.
- **PRE-BUILD WALKTHROUGH 2026-08-23, all five open dials RULED (user, one message):** wall gate = **ANY wall** ("i think any wall is fine"); mutation **level 2 / radius 7** ("this is fine"); **tier 3** ("tier 3 probably"); **liquid-ping/aquifer half SKIPPED** ("fine to skip this, we don't need it" — the wall-ear pitch's aquifer clause is retired, not deferred); grant toggles **silently**, status legibility via the item's examine state ("sure, sounds fine").

**cairn / disrupted cairn — USER:** "decorative path objects like paths around the palladium reef. if you step on one, it turns into the disrupted state, and makes nearby stonecarvers hostile. it's patharound so autoexplore will avoid it, just a sort of introduction to the mechanic."

- **The mechanism exists, under a different name.** There is no `PathAround` tag in Base. The real hook is the **`NavigationWeight` intproperty**, read by `GetNavigationWeightEvent.HandleFor` — it walks the cell's objects, takes the highest `NavigationWeight` found, and **a weight of 100 or more terminates the walk, i.e. reads as do-not-path-here.** Base carriers are all hazards: `MiasmaticAsh`, `Frigid Mist`, `Space-Time Anomaly / Vortex / Rift`, `Wormhole`, `TrembleEntranceWormhole`, `TrembleExitWormhole`, `InteriorVoid`, plus terrain-scale use on `TerrainJungle` / `TerrainDeepJungle` / `TerrainFungalBase`. `AvoidMovingOnto` also exists (`CyberWall`, `MachineWallHotTubing`) as a second, blunter avoidance flag.
- **CLAUDE, flagged:** every base carrier of `NavigationWeight` is a thing that *hurts you*. A cairn using it is the first decorative one, and the semantics fit (autoexplore routing around a social tripwire is exactly right), but it means the avoidance is a pathing hint only — **the player can still walk onto it deliberately, which is precisely the intended trap.** Good fit, no engine work needed.
- **CLAUDE, naming correction for the reef reference:** the palladium reef's `Palladium Strut` is *not* a walkable decorative path — it is `Physics Takeable="false"`, `Occluding="true"`, 200 HP, tagged `NavigationStrut` / `Reef` / `HangingSupport`, i.e. a solid obstacle. The actual walkable decorative-path precedents are `DirtPath` / `DirtRoad` / `SaltPath` / `PaintedFlowers` in `ZoneTerrain.xml`, all inheriting `DirtFloor` at `RenderLayer="0"`. **The cairn is a takeable-false object standing ON a floor cell, not a floor object** — otherwise stepping on it is not a distinct event. Worth confirming which the user pictures, because it changes the blueprint root.
- **CLAUDE, open forks:** what counts as "nearby" for the aggro radius · whether the disrupted state is a blueprint swap or a part flipping render + name + description · whether a disrupted cairn can be restacked/repaired (the stonecarver-appeasement half writes itself and the user has not mentioned it) · whether the aggro is faction-wide or just the zone · whether cairns are painter-placed (they already appear in the **Cairn tableaus** and **trail** threads above, so this is a third entry point into the same object and should be reconciled before anything is built).

**Grinder cell — SHAPED 2026-08-22 (design sitting, user + Claude leans; resolves the feed-rubble vs ambient-harvest fork in FEED's favor):** USER: "a high capacity cousin of the fidget cell. where instead of being low max charge, easy to fill up, it's higher max charge, but harder to fill up. stones have weight, boulders especially, and grinding takes time." RULED same sitting: **grinds anywhere** (inventory; no installed-in-device requirement) · **trickle, not burst** (user lean over Claude's burst-on-completion pitch; the locked description's "bursts" reads per-crunch, no text conflict) · **mill law inapplicable** (user: "invalid as no armed stones made it in!" — correct, the armed-rock catalog rode the fissure-v1 quarantine). Build shape agreed: socket-style feed (the lantern part pattern, one stone ground down at a time), charge = weight x rate (geomancy weight-as-potency precedent), grind time weight-scaled, `Cleo_TerraFirma_IsAnyStone` as the diet gate, gems grind wastefully (comedy intact). CLAUDE DIALS, RATIFIED 2026-08-22 (user: "all this is fine, no worries"): MaxCharge 8000-10000 (fidget 2500, chem 10000 anchors) · charge = weight x 20 · trickle ~10/turn · NO size cap (a boulder's 100+ turn grind is its own tax). Description locked above. **BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-22 (numbers + no-cap confirmed in-chat: "numbers are fine, no need for boulder cap"; compiles green 0 errors, XML strict-valid, hash-verified; user sprite `grinder_cell.png`):** `Part_GrinderCell.cs` + `Cleo_TerraFirma_GrinderCell` blueprint (`Inherits="Energy Cell"`, **MaxCharge 50000 with `StartCharge="10000-25000"` (RE-RULED mid-build, user: "make the max charge something like 50k and have it usually spawn 1/5th to half full"; StartCharge = base EnergyCell dice field, rolled at creation, decomp-verified)**, Commerce 100, Tier 3, wish-only via `ExcludeFromDynamicEncounters`). Feed = inventory action with a picker (stones differ in worth and in regret, so the player chooses; NPCs take the heaviest); stone destroyed on feed, pool = weight x 20, trickle 10/turn into the cell's own `EnergyCell.AddCharge`; name wears `(grinding)` while working. **Claude calls, flagged:** no unload (a half-ground stone is not a thing) · grinding PAUSES while the cell is full, stone conserved · picker prompt + feed messages DRAFT. Dev door: wish `tfgrinder` (cell + 3 small stones = 600 charge, 60 turns). `[verify]` next boot: feed via picker · `(grinding)` tag · charge climbs 10/turn (`[TF-VERIFY] [GRINDER]` lines) · small stone = 200 over 20 turns · gem grinds wastefully · full cell pauses the grind · slotted in a device it discharges like any cell · save→load mid-grind rides. **Post-build round, same sitting (user): colors = `c` cyan main / `y` grey detail (tile + display name) · `StartCharge="2500-25000"` (re-ruled from 10k-25k) · charge = weight x 25 (from x20). BUG, user repro + FIXED same round: feeding the equipped sculpted pebble destroyed it IN the thrown slot, leaving a stale reference that made EarthenBarrage's pickup re-arm bail forever at its `GetFirstThrownWeapon() != null` guard (Mutation_EarthenBarrage.cs:512). Root fix: feed candidates are now LOOSE INVENTORY ONLY via the mill's own `GetInventoryDirect` idiom (equipped stones never offered), and `Temporary` (conjured) stones are excluded outright — they vanish on their own and were a free-energy loop besides. Compiled green, hash-verified, redeployed.** **Weight preservation, RULED + BUILT same day (user: "grinder cell should probably preserve the weight of what its grinding and steadily decrement that over time?"):** the cell now carries its stone's unground weight via `GetExtrinsicWeightEvent` (the waterskin/LiquidVolume idiom, decomp-verified `E.Weight +=`), derived straight from the fuel pool — 1 lb leaves per 25 charge ground out, `FlushWeightCaches` on feed and on each whole-pound step. A fed boulder is HEAVY until the spiral has actually eaten it; feeding is no longer a weight-teleport. Compiled green, deployed. `[verify]` re-check next boot: equipped pebble absent from the feed picker · fed stone's weight shows on the cell and ticks down as it grinds **ADJACENCY FEED RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-23 (user: "piezoelectric should pull stones from adjacency rather than just your inventory, so selecting feed a stone knows if a boulder is in the tile next to you"):** the picker now also offers tagged stones on the actor's own tile and the eight neighbors, marked "{{K|(nearby)}}" (DRAFT), ground down IN PLACE — a boulder never has to be lifted. Guards: walls refuse (tagged wall = terrain, not a meal), creatures refuse, OWNED stones skipped rather than silently vandalized (dial, reopen if theft-with-consequences is wanted), Temporary still excluded. **Weight conservation stands as ruled: a ground-fed boulder makes the carried cell HEAVY until the spiral eats it down** — drop the cell while it chews if the load is too much; that is the conservation ruling expressing itself, not a bug. `[verify]` next boot: boulder in an adjacent tile appears in the picker marked nearby · feeding it starts the grind without the boulder entering inventory · cell weight climbs by the boulder and ticks down · walls and owned stones never offered. **DIALS RE-RULED 2026-08-23 (user, after the ModHighCapacity recon: "lower the starting charge to 2500-10000, lower the cap to 25000, and raise the grind to *50 charge") + BUILT + DEPLOYED:** MaxCharge 50000 → 25000 · StartCharge 2500-25000 → 2500-10000 · CHARGE_PER_POUND 25 → 50 (weight decrement follows the const: 1 lb per 50 charge). Derived shape: a stone is worth double and grinds twice as long (5 turns per pound at the 10/turn trickle), a full fill from empty is 500 lbs of rock, and a tier-3 high-capacity roll now tops at 42,500 instead of 85,000. `[verify]` folds into the grinder list: cell reads max 25000, spawns 10-40% full, small stone = 50 charge over 5 turns. **STOP GRINDING RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-23 (user: "there should probably be a stop grinding option in case you cell fills up but its still grinding away at rocks and weighing a ton"):** "stop grinding" inventory action shown while a stone is in the spiral — pool zeroed, carried weight cleared, animation stilled, remaining charge FORFEITED (no reconstitution per the standing no-unload call; the stone was already paid, so dumping is pure weight relief, no exploit). Message "The spiral stills and dumps its unground grist." DRAFT; 1000 energy (dial). Feed and Stop are mutually exclusive actions by grind state. **Adjacency-scan diagnostic also live (`e6c96f7`): the boulder-not-offered report awaits one instrumented feed attempt — the `[GRINDER] feed scan` lines name the refusing gate.** **SCAN VERDICT IN + BOTH FIXES DEPLOYED 2026-08-23 (the instrumented attempt confessed two at once):** (1) `feed scan refused LargeBoulder: tagged=True, wall=True` — **`LargeBoulder` CARRIES the base `Wall` tag** (the earlier "boulders have no Wall tag" claim was read off a TRUNCATED blueprint print — lesson: head-limited greps lie by omission), so Claude's over-careful `IsWall` guard refused the exact target; the guard is DELETED — real walls never descend from `BaseStone`, so the stone tag alone is the diet gate. The user's diagonal hunch was innocent; the boulder they then LIFTED (500 lb, feature's whole purpose inverted, bless) fed at pool 25000 and the stop-dump returned 470 lb — both new verbs live-proven in the same log. (2) **`reach=9` then `reach=10` across two scans = my `Insert(0, origin)` was MUTATING THE ENGINE'S CACHED adjacency list** — `GetLocalAdjacentCells` returns `_LocalAdjacentCellCache` BY REFERENCE (decomp), so every feed attempt taught that cell it is its own neighbor, corrupting adjacency for anything else consulting it. Fixed: the scan copies the list; a restart flushes any already-corrupted cell (runtime cache, not serialized). `[verify]` next boot: adjacent (including diagonal) boulder appears in the picker marked nearby and feeds in place · repeated feed attempts hold reach=9 in the scan line. `[verify]` mid-grind cell shows Stop not Feed · stopping clears the weight and the churn animation, logs the dumped pounds · idle cell shows Feed again. **Finish message added same sitting (user: "when grinding finishes, it should put a message to chat, like starting and dumping"): "The spiral grinds its stone down to nothing and stills." (2) **Boxes COMBINE instead:** `MergeIntoCarried` in the EnsureGrant funnel (Taken/AddedToInventory/Equipped) pours an arriving box into the carried one and destroys the husk on the crumble idiom (Destroy + validate + Obliterate belt, `box merge:` VerifyLog); survivor = the grant-holding box, so the command registration never moves; merge line DRAFT ("You tip the new chalk into your box."). (3) **Legacy stacks in saves HEAL on sight** (`HealLegacyStack` at EnsureGrant + ConsumeUse): "(N) xK" becomes one box at N*K — the player has been reading N-per-box, so the heal grants the reading; over-credit for post-stack draws is unknowable, small, player-favorable, accepted. Texture flags for later rulings: merged boxes SELL as one (Commerce 10 flat, auto-merge trades pickup-value for convenience) · equipping a second box while carrying one absorbs the equipped box into the pack box (survivor stability won). ### 0.1.2 board answers (user question 2026-08-13, answered from code — ground truth, not memory)
- **Statue inventory = EXACT COPY, never a re-roll.** `_StatueHelpers.EntombPossessions` (shared by Gaze petrify-kills and Stoneshaper expiry swaps): statue becomes a real container (Chest recipe, Container+Inventory), every non-natural equipped item is unequipped, then the LITERAL GameObject instances move from victim inventory into the statue — stacks, mods, liquid fills, everything as-held at petrify moment (spawn-time table roll is the only roll that ever happened). Natural equipment stays with the body by design; `ReturnPossessions` unwinds it all if the death is prevented.
- [ ] `[bug]` **EQUIPPED GEAR IS DESTROYED BY PETRIFICATION, NOT ENTOMBED — USER REPORT 2026-08-15, live ("it makes sure that you can get their inventory, but it might not force unequip all their items before freezing them… no butchery is fine, but armor should be lootable"). DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same sitting (user: "let's do our one line fix for statues!"; compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified, active next restart).** The user's read was right and the damage is one step worse than "not lootable": the gear is **orphaned**, not merely left behind. Chain, all verified in `BodyPart.cs`: `_StatueHelpers.EntombPossessions` loops the body and calls **`part.Unequip()`**, which is the LOW-LEVEL SLOT CLEARER — its whole body is `FindPartsEquipping(equipped, …)` then `ClearEquipped()` on each. It fires no unequip command and hands the item to no inventory. Equipped objects do not live in `Inventory` (they live on the body part), so after that call the armor is referenced by nothing, and the sweep that follows moves only what was already in the pack. Hence exactly the reported symptom: statue opens, backpack is there, everything WORN or WIELDED is gone. **Scope is wider than the gaze:** the same helper runs on Stoneshaper's expiry swap (`Mutation_Stoneshaper.cs:720`), so statue-swap loot has the identical hole. **Fix shape (one line, not built): `part.ForceUnequip(Silent: true)`** — fires `CommandForceUnequipObject`, the engine's own forced path, which routes the item into the owner's inventory and handles multi-slot and cursed gear correctly; the existing inventory sweep then carries it to the statue unchanged. Considered alternative: capture `part.Equipped` and `Statue.ReceiveObject` it directly, fewer engine assumptions but it skips the unequip event that some items key off — ForceUnequip preferred. **RECORD CORRECTION, same sitting: the 08-13 board answer below claims "every non-natural equipped item is unequipped, then the LITERAL GameObject instances move from victim inventory into the statue." The first half is true, the second half never applied to equipped gear at all.** User's scope confirmed at report: corpse-path loss (butchery, pelts) stays as ruled, this is only about carried and worn goods. **BUILT AS DIAGNOSED:** `ForceUnequip(Silent: true)` replaces the slot-clear, with two hardenings the one-liner did not have — the parts list is SNAPSHOTTED before the loop (the unequip event can restructure the body underneath a live enumeration, which the old code was also exposed to) and a BELT catches a refusal: if `ForceUnequip` returns false and the item is still equipped, the slot is cleared the old way *and the item handed straight to the statue in the same breath*, so nothing can be orphaned again by any path. **Harness probe added, `feedback.entomb` (rides `tffeedback`/`tfbatch`):** equips armor + carries boots on a test Snapjaw, entombs, asserts BOTH land inside the statue; it distinguishes the three failure modes in its own report line (setup fail / worn lost / carried lost). **tfbatch expectation moves 24 → 25.** `[verify]` next boot: `tfbatch` green including `feedback.entomb`, then eyes on a real gaze kill — statue holds the victim's armor and weapon, not just the pack. **Knock-on flagged, not built:** `ReturnPossessions` (death-prevented unwind) hands everything back to INVENTORY and does not re-equip. Before the fix that path returned nothing at all for worn gear, so this is strictly an improvement, but a survivor now wakes up holding their armor rather than wearing it — auto-re-equip on return is a separate call, user's.
- **Chosen-not-discovered rule (Claude observation at the trace, ENDORSED lean, user veto open): petrify kills produce NO corpse, so corpse-path loot never rolls — gaze forecloses butchery.** A gaze-killed cobble baboon entombs his carried stones but never yields cobblefur; the rockhound's swallowed hoard DOES entomb (it's inventory), but pelts/butcherables/the stonefrog's ruled rock-corpse are corpse-path and lost to stone. Flavor read: "you can't skin stone." Recorded so the rule is deliberate rather than stumbled into at a bug report.

### Design calls
- [ ] `[design]` **Daze rider CUT — RULED 2026-08-06 (beta rebalance pass, item 3; user: "cutting the daze rider from earthen barrage") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Constants, `GetThrowDazeChanceIn100`, the `MakeStone` part attachment, the level-text line, the CollectStats stat, and the ability-card clause all removed. `Part_DazeOnThrowPens.cs` kept as an ORPHAN on purpose — earmarked for a wild rock-thrower variant (cragmensch note, TODO-archive benchmarks). Companion proposals from the same item (whorl stat gains, damage curve) tracked separately below, UNRULED at time of writing. `[verify]` thrown stones no longer daze; level text and ability card show no daze line.
- [ ] `[design]` **Whorl stat shifts CUT — RULED 2026-08-06 (user: "cut stat shift from whorl") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** The reserve-size AV (+1 at 3+, +2 at 9+) and the resonant DV swap (+2/+4, the 2026-07-23 compensation ruling — superseded with its parent) are gone; `Sync` now clears any lingering shift (old-save hygiene) and the whorl's defense is deflection alone (3%/stone +1%/level, outcomes 50/30/20). Level-text AV lines and the ability-card "grants DV in place of AV" clause removed; the parked whorl-DV display fix is MOOT. `[verify]` reserve at 9+: character sheet shows no whorl AV/DV; attune on/off flips deflect type only.
- [ ] `[design]` **Throw damage curve — PEN CUT RULED 2026-08-06 (user data point: "one shotting electrofuges at level 4"; from the simmed menu: "I lean penetration cut, yeag") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Pen bonus Lv/2 → **Lv/3** (+1/+2/+3 at 3/6/9); damage dice UNTOUCHED at (1+L/3)d6+(L%3). Sim ground truth (`design/eb_damage_sim.py` + electrofuge one-shot runs, Toughness HP modifier included per user reminder — Leveler.cs applies TouMod×(Lv−1)): P(one-shot electrofuge | hit) at L4 drops 19-37% → 8-21%; L10 avg vs AV 3 drops 44 → 29, landing at Light Manipulation lase parity (28) — defensible as "EB per-hit ≈ lase, paying the to-hit/range tax with a bigger magazine." Dice-cut and both-cut variants REJECTED (both-cut undershot lase). Rider supersession: resonant pens (trained/5, "+1 at Lv10" per the 2026-07-22 ruling) is now 0 at every level — resonant stones ride pure vibro; noted in MakeStone. Every-level lattice intact (damage bonus/dice tick all ten levels). `[verify]` L4 stones read 2d6+1 pv+1 on the sheet; electrofuges survive most single hits at L4; resonant stones show no pen bonus.
- [ ] `[design]` **Damage still reads too high — RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-15 (ladder F; opened by the user note "note: tune earthen barrage damage lower").** Successor to the pen cut above: that pass took penetration only and left the dice untouched at (1+L/3)d6+(L%3), on the reasoning that L10 avg vs AV 3 landed at Light Manipulation lase parity (29 vs 28). This note says the result still hits harder than wanted — which puts the dice back on the table, since pen is the half already spent. The dice-cut variant REJECTED on 2026-08-06 is the obvious first thing to re-sim, but its rejection was relative to lase parity, so settle the target first: is parity-with-lase still the yardstick, or does EB now aim below it? Re-run `design/eb_damage_sim.py` (Toughness HP modifier on) once the target is set; the electrofuge one-shot runs are the comparable. **Open question for the user: which lever, and what number does it aim at.**
  - **Friend's lever menu, relayed by the user 2026-08-15 (verbatim):** *"i think its fun that u can use it a lot so maybe just less damage to start? / or maybe it Should just come back slower / or fewer in the chamber? / or have a decent amount of weight / but then that makes it even worse lategame when it probably falls off quite a bit anyway"* — four levers, thinking out loud, nothing ruled. Claude's read (PROPOSAL, unruled), with the code checked at time of writing: **(a) less damage to start = the live one.** It is the only lever pointed at the actual complaint (`GetThrowDamage` = `(1+L/3)d6+(L%3)`, L4 = 2d6+1, the electrofuge one-shot level) and it is the only one that respects the friend's own stated value, that using it a lot is the fun part. **(b) slower regen — misses.** `TURNS_PER_CHARGE = 15`, Willpower-scaled; slowing it changes how OFTEN you one-shot an electrofuge, never WHETHER you do, and it flattens the mod's second Willpower payoff (the "earth rewards the disciplined" theme it shares with Gorgon's Gaze DC). **(c) fewer in the chamber — double-dip trap, recommend against.** Reserve size IS the whorl: `GetDeflectChance() = (3 * Charges) + Level`, and `MaxCharges = 3 + (Lv-1)` runs 3 at L1 to 12 at L10, so cutting the magazine nerfs offense and defense on one dial. The whorl already paid at the 2026-08-06 stat-shift cut. **(d) weight — near-dead already.** The sculpted stone ships `Weight="1"` in `Objects.xml` and engine weight is an int, so the only moves up are whole points carried per stone in reserve, worst exactly at L10 with 12 of them. The friend talked themself out of this one in the same breath and they were right. *(Do not confuse this with the 2026-07-24 "pebble weight 0" ratification, which was a rock-catalog item, now quarantined.)*
  - **The friend's closing worry is the most useful line in the note, and it cuts against the last pass.** They flag lategame falloff. The 2026-08-06 pen cut (Lv/2 → Lv/3) took **1 pen at L4 and 2 at L10** — it was steeper LATE, i.e. it already pushed hardest where the falloff worry lives. So the shape to sim is not a flat damage cut but an **early-weighted** one: move where the dice ladder ticks (`diceSize` off `L/3`) so L1-6 comes down and L10 lands near where it is now. **Still blocked on the same open question: parity-with-lase as the target, or below it.**
  - **SIM RUN 2026-08-15 (user: "sure, we can sim it") — `design/eb_damage_sim2.py`, results DATA not ruling; no code touched.** Engine validated against the 2026-08-06 record before anything new was read: it reproduces that pass's L10-vs-AV3 averages (pre-cut 44.2 vs recorded 44, post-cut 29.2 vs recorded 29) and its electrofuge one-shot band (pre 19-37%, post 9-21% vs recorded 19-37% / 8-21%) to the digit. **Electrofuge HP band 18-22 was BACK-SOLVED from those recorded percentages, not re-derived** — flagged in the script docstring, because reading `BaseSpider` Toughness (10+1d3 = 11-13, modifier NEGATIVE) off the blueprint suggests effective HP 6-10 instead, which would roughly triple every one-shot number. Unresolvable this session, no decomp on disk. It moves all rows together and does not reorder the candidates, so the ranking below holds either way; settle it before any number ships.
    - **The complaint is a CEILING problem, not an average problem** — this is the finding that picks the lever. Damage is rolled once *per penetration*, so the dice count multiplies. At L4 shipped (2d6+1) a two-pen throw tops out at 26; the electrofuge dies at 18-22. Trading dice for flat bonus at the same average pulls the ceiling down without gutting the curve, which is why a **flat average cut is the wrong shape** and the every-level lattice can survive intact.
    - **Ladder F — slow start, L10 identical. RECOMMENDED (Claude proposal, unruled).** `1d6+0 / 1d6+1 / 1d6+2 / 1d6+3 / 2d6+0 / 2d6+1 / 3d6+0 / 3d6+1 / 4d6+0 / 4d6+1` at L1-10, pen ladder untouched at L/3. L4 one-shot **9-21% → 2-8%**. L7 avg vs AV3 18.0 → 16.4. **L10 avg vs AV3 stays 29.2 — the exact shipped number, still Light Manipulation lase parity (28).** Answers "less damage to start" while touching the lategame falloff not at all. Ticks every level.
    - **Ladder G — F plus a lategame trim.** Same start, one notch off the top: L10 = `4d6+0`, avg vs AV3 27.2, a hair under lase. Take this one only if the answer to the target question is "below parity."
    - **Ladder E — the mild version.** Whole ladder shifted one level left (L4 `2d6+0`, L10 `4d6+0`). Cuts everything ~6-7% uniformly: L4 one-shot 4-13%, L10 avg 27.3. Cheapest to implement (two off-by-one edits, no table) but it is the flat-cut shape the ceiling finding argues against.
    - **The 2026-08-06 dice-cut rejection is CONFIRMED, with a new reason.** d4 dice put L10 at **21.4 vs AV3** — not just under lase, but hollowing out exactly the lategame the friend's closing line worries about. It buys the best early number (L4 one-shot 1-5%) at the worst possible price. Stays rejected.
  - **LADDER F RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "we can go with F") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same sitting.** `GetThrowDamage` in `Mutation_EarthenBarrage.cs` is now a hand-authored table for L1-10 (`LADDER_DICE`/`LADDER_FLAT`) with L11+ falling through to the pre-existing `(1+L/3)d6+(L%3)` curve; the two meet exactly at L10 (`4d6+1`), so there is no seam and wish-level scaling is untouched. Shipped ladder: `1d6 / 1d6+1 / 1d6+2 / 1d6+3 / 2d6 / 2d6+1 / 3d6 / 3d6+1 / 4d6 / 4d6+1`. Penetration NOT touched (`GetPenetrationCap` stays `1 + Lv/3`). Compiles green, 0 errors; deployed to `Mods\TerraFirma` and `Mods\TerraFirmaBeta`, both hash-verified; active next game restart. **No text debt:** the level text and `CollectStats` both read `GetThrowDamage(Level)` live, and no dice are hardcoded in any XML, so the ability card and level lines re-derive themselves.
  - `[verify]` next boot: L4 sheet reads **1d6+3** (was 2d6+1) · L10 reads **4d6+1**, unchanged from the current build · every level 1-10 shows a different damage string as you wish up · L11 reads 4d6+2 · resonant stones still show no pen bonus.
  - `[open]` **Electrofuge effective-HP derivation still unsettled — carried forward from the sim, does NOT block the ladder.** The 18-22 band was back-solved from the 2026-08-06 percentages; the blueprint reading (`BaseSpider` Toughness 10+1d3, negative modifier) argues for 6-10 instead. It scales every one-shot figure together without reordering the candidates, which is why F shipped anyway. Settle against `Leveler.cs` whenever a decomp is on disk again (none this session, old scratchpad dumps are gone) — it matters for the NEXT balance question, not this one.

### Whorl outcome scale — **BUILT 2026-07-22 (re-ruled same day; supersedes the 2026-07-21 spec below in three places)**
The three-stage deflection landed (`Effect_GravelWhorl.cs`: `Devour`/`Swat`/`Ricochet`, dispatched on a successful DeflectChance roll). Today's rulings vs the old spec:
- **Split RULED: even thirds (1d3)** — supersedes the old 50/30/20. **RE-RULED same day (third round): back to 50/30/20** — user asked which works better; Claude's case: rarity graded to drama (devour common = the mass hungers and feeds the identity; swat mundane middle; ricochet at 20% stays a story instead of noise). Named consequence, accepted: grenade stones now get torn apart on the defender in HALF of deflects rather than a third — the mind-game sharpens.
- **Devoured:** ephemera obliterate; **plain stones (`IsAnyStone`) feed the reserve +1 charge, capped at max** (as originally specced — enemy rock-throwers feed the barrager; "The whorl thickens." DRAFT message; `Charges++` + `SyncWhorl`, plumbing verified public). **DANGEROUS stones don't join the mass — RULED 2026-07-22 (second ruling round): they're TORN APART in the gravelly mass and their effects TRIGGER.** Boundary = the mill's own hazard class (`IGrenade` part OR `GrindSpawn` tag): grenade stones detonate **at the defender's cell** (a quarrelquartz eaten by your own whorl goes off on you — the mind game vs whorl-users is intended; blame attribution = the thrower via `E.Attacker`, so their kill not your suicide), grind-special stones release their contents around the defender (gyrestone → vortices in your lap, the mill's scatter shape). No charge gained either way. **DELTA — RULED KEPT 2026-07-23: non-stone real items are OBLITERATED** (user: "throwable items are almost always disposable… if you were throwing expensive items at an enemy's whorl to see what happens, you deserve to find out"). Rider from the same ruling, BUILT + DEPLOYED: the torn-apart branch (grenades detonate at the defender, blame to the thrower) now applies to **ALL devoured grenades, not just mod stones** — "as long as when a grenade hits the whorl, it explodes"; a base-game grenade is no longer silently defused. `[verify]` if an enemy-thrown base-game grenade can even reach the outcome scale (grenades usually target cells, not creatures).
- **Swatted:** the original behavior verbatim (munitions fizzle; real items to a random open adjacent cell).
- **Ricochet RULED (after checking the Light Manipulation precedent at user request — base reflect is ANGLE-based, `Direction = Angle + 180`, never target-picked): fully random angle line-trace** — from the defender's cell, range 5 (DRAFT), first creature on the line takes flat d20 vs DV (no marksmanship) and **the projectile's own damage** (ThrownWeapon die / Projectile BaseDamage; supersedes the old −2 PV `RollDamagePenetrations` mini-attack — simpler, no pen math); carom ends at the first body hit or flinched; walls stop it; real items land where it ends, munitions spend themselves; kill attribution = whorl's owner; **payload channels deliberately don't fire** (the whorl redirects mass, not triggers). Applies identically to resonant-mode deflects (light munitions careen too).
- [ ] `[design]` **Aegischist thrown = force BUBBLE around the victim, impassable (user ruling flag, 2026-07-23):** hitting an enemy should cage them in a ring of force shards **they cannot move through** — a thrown prison — replacing the current 3-cell wall line on hits (the line doesn't enclose anything; live-fire showed victims just walking away). Build shape: the thrown-hit path reuses the existing step channel's bubble recipe (ring of 8 around the target); keep the wall line for misses (terrain denial at the landing cell). Build-time check: base `Forcefield` pass semantics — ensure the VICTIM can't slip through (owner-pass rules; shards may need to be ownerless or thrower-owned), and decide whether the ring skipping occupied/solid cells leaves exploitable gaps (wall-adjacent enemies get partial cages — acceptable or fill-with-what).
- [ ] `[verify]` outcome scale: deflects split visibly three ways over a fight; devoured plain stone → charge count ticks up (capped) + message; devoured quarrelquartz → detonates ON the defender (and the death message blames the thrower); devoured gyrestone → vortices around the defender; devoured arrow simply gone; ricochet line now renders a staggered gravel-blip trace + endpoint dust puff (RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-07-23; "we could mess around with animations later on" — richer VFX parked as a future pass) — verify the trace reads as travel and hits/flinches/landings resolve, including friendly fire and return-to-sender geometry; whorl's effect description shows the new outcome text; resonant whorl still only touches Light projectiles.
- **Resonant `RefractLight` upgrade — BUILT 2026-07-22** (the 2026-07-21 decision, closed): the effect registers the `"RefractLight"` string event (BlinkingTicSickness idiom: `RegisterEffectEvent` in Apply, unregister in Remove — registered always, handler gates on Resonant since Sync flips attunement at runtime); on success (same DeflectChance) it stamps By + Verb "scatter" (DRAFT) and returns false. **Resonant whorls now scatter Lase-type beams** — the "direct beams pass through" gap closes for resonant mode (normal mode keeps it; effect description texts updated both branches, DRAFT). Two decompile findings shaped it: **(1) beams-only gating** — laser missile SHOTS fire the same event with a `Projectile` param set (MissileWeapon.cs:1426) and are excluded by the handler, because they already roll the missile-hit outcome scale (no double-dip; without the gate resonant would get two bites at every laser bolt, ~1−(1−c)²); Lase beams pass `Projectile=null` (LightManipulation.cs:159). **(2) Fully random scatter is engine-native** — both beam paths pre-seed `Direction = Stat.Random(0,359)` and the handler deliberately does NOT override it (the old spec's `RetroVariance`-style angle math turned out unnecessary — an unset Direction IS the gravel scatter). Sound = engine default `sfx_light_refract`.
