### Design calls
- [ ] `[polish]` **"Rough" statue look-text claims "intricately depicts" (Discord-feedback thread 2026-08-07, a friend's catch relayed by the user; user's "might be fixed now" hunch checked — NOT fixed, the 08-06 true-copy pass only touched WALL descriptions):** "intricate" appears nowhere in our files or git history — it's hard-coded in the base `RandomStatue.SetCreature` template ("This sculpture worked from {material} intricately depicts {creature}: ..."), stamped on every carve at `MakeStatueOfCreatureFromWall`'s `SetCreature(Muse)` call. Scope = Stoneshaper carves only (Gorgon statues never enter this path; sole `SetCreature` caller checked). DeanimateStatue copies `_Short` to the inert statue, so a carve-time fix rides through expiry. **RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-07 (compiles green, hash-verified — active next restart): TIER-1-ONLY fix** — after the six-tier lookbox mockup sitting (full draft ladder rendered: crudely/finely/faithfully/intricately-rehomed/masterfully/flawlessly), the user chose the minimal cut ("we could go even simpler and *just* do tier 1"; Claude concurred — rough is the only tier that CONTRADICTS the base sentence, the others merely restate the name's tier signal). Rough carves now read **"crudely depicts" (user's own word, from "rough statues depicted crudely" — no draft ceremony)** via a `NamePrefix == "rough"` string replace after `SetCreature` in `MakeStatueOfCreatureFromWall`; every other tier keeps the untouched base template (maximal base-voice adherence). Name ladder untouched — display names still climb rough→immaculate. The descriptor switch is the standing hook if the full ladder ever seems worth it (draft words above are the bench). Rides into the inert statue via DeanimateStatue's `_Short` copy. `[verify]` next boot: Lv1-2 carve look-text reads "crudely depicts"; a Lv3+ carve still reads "intricately depicts"; expired rough statue keeps "crudely".
- [ ] `[design]` `[verify]` Ensure it feels balanced and actually copies the creature's anatomy (`Body.Rebuild(Creature.Body.Anatomy)` — API verified real; test in-game against weird body plans: Slog, swarms, no-handed creatures).
- [ ] `[design]` **Statue-of-a-statue recursion — USER CONCEPT 2026-08-12 (friend report relayed: "with base cooldown, you can make a rough stone sculpture of a rough stone sculpture of a creature"; user's shape, their words: "rather than bar off this behavior entirely, what if we just gave it a random stat malus, so it was like 'each copy of a copy you make degrades'"). TIMING RULED same sitting (user: "i don't think it should happen the *first* time you make a copy, but every time you copy the statue after that, the stats should get dinged") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart).** Recon that shaped it (code, verified): a living statue passes the muse picker's `IsCreature`+`Body` gates, so the loop is real; the actual stakes are STATLINE IMMORTALITY — `ApplyStatueBuffs` copies Str/Agi/Tou from the muse, so re-carving your statue from itself before expiry preserves a dead/departed muse's statline forever, dodging the recorded temporary-by-design intent (Lv1-9). HP/AV are formula-driven and don't ride the loop. Build: every statue gets int property `Cleo_TerraFirma_StatueCopyGen` = muse's gen + 1 (real creatures read 0 → their statue is gen 1; the counter exists ONLY for the first-copy-free gate — the malus itself compounds through the Str/Agi/Tou inheritance with no math, gen 4 copying gen 3's already-dinged values); muse gen ≥ 2 → the new statue loses 1d3 from ONE random stat of Str/Agi/Tou (random-single shape proposed + go-ahead'd at the sitting; 1d3 size + floor-at-1 clamp = CLAUDE dials, flag if the decay rate feels off), `[SHAPE]` "copy decay" log line, player-only message "Some of the likeness is lost in the copying." (DRAFT, rides the release text pass). Inert expired statues already fail the muse picker — only living statues feed the loop, no code needed. `[verify]` next boot: carve creature → statue (gen 1, clean) → copy it (gen 2, clean, stats identical) → copy again (gen 3: one stat visibly down 1-3 on the statue's sheet, decay message in the log, [SHAPE] line names stat + roll); lineage stats never fall below 1. ("stoneshaper snaps to a target creature in sight, but the second part of the cast doesn't snap to walls, requiring manual targeting"). BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (polish sitting; compiles green 0 errors, hash-verified — active next restart).** Recon findings (decomp `IComponent.PickDestinationCell` + `PickTarget.ShowPicker`): the old call started the cursor at the CASTER's cell (Snap only hunts hostiles, useless for walls), and ShowPicker's `UseTarget=true` default silently discards any start coords in favor of the sidebar target — a creature, never a wall — so no PickDestinationCell arguments could fix it. Build: the NPC path's `FindCarvableWallCellFor` scan generalized to `FindCarvableWallCell(origin, radius, RequireVisible)` (identical eligibility walk — solid/no-holo-walltrap-forcefield/no-refusal-tag/audited-props/type-within-level — one predicate now serves both paths); player picker seeds at the nearest player-visible carveable wall to the MUSE (`PLAYER_WALL_SEED_RADIUS = 20`, CLAUDE dial) via direct `PickTarget.ShowPicker` with `UseTarget: false`, caster-cell fallback when no eligible wall is in range (old behavior exactly). `[TF-VERIFY] [SHAPE]` logs the seed cell. `[verify]` next boot: cast Stoneshaper near walls — after picking the muse, the wall cursor starts ON a legal wall near it; cast in open ground — cursor starts on the caster as before; the sidebar-target hijack gone (cursor no longer starts on your combat target).
- **Balance rationale (user, 2026-07-21):** statues are deliberately weaker than Beguiling — temporary by design, copies without inventory; "a survivable buddy who helps with a couple fights." Recorded as the design intent.
- [x] `[design]` **Lv10 permanence — RULED 2026-07-21: kept as capstone.** Rewards investment; duration ceasing to matter past 10 is accepted.

## Geomancy

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Small fixes / De-Harmony investigation)*

- [ ] `[bug]` **Wall HP no longer visible when throwing a rock — USER FLAG 2026-08-12 (their words: "at some point, the harmony patch we used for geomancy stopped letting us see wall HP when a rock is thrown"). SNIFF-OUT RUN same day (user: "we should sniff out the geomancy wall health thing"; context supplied: ava's patch existed because "the 'structural scanning' part had to attach to a player to work" — confirmed, see below). STATIC ANALYSIS EXONERATES EVERY LAYER; DIAGNOSTIC PROBE BUILT + DEPLOYED (`tfgeoscan`, compiles green, hash-verified); root cause NOT yet pinned.** The flag-time theory (readout died with the deleted throw patch) is DEAD — git shows the throw patch was already native by the revival baseline, and the SCANNING patch (`Scanning.HasScanningFor(GameObject, GameObject)` postfix, force-true for objects carrying `Cleo_TerraFirma_PartGeomancyScan`) is STILL IN THE TREE, byte-identical since the ava-era 1.1 backup. Everything checks out statically: patch target signature alive in the current decomp · the HP readout (`XRL.Rules.Strings.WoundLevel/WoundLevelN` — the Look/Sidebar/tooltip surface) calls EXACTLY the patched overload · walls carry no `NoDamageReadout` (WorldTerrain only) · Gemstone merge + `IsGemstone` tag intact, base `Gemstone` root alive · engine auto-applies mod Harmony patches (`ModInfo.ApplyHarmonyPatches`, failures = MODERROR) and the log shows none. Ava's design constraint confirmed from decomp: scanning is player-side int properties (`StructureScannerEquipped` etc.), and the new `GetScanTypeEvent` only picks scan TYPE — there is STILL no native object-side grant, so the patch remains the only per-object shape. **Prime suspect, untestable statically: JIT INLINING** — `HasScanningFor(who, obj)` is a one-line delegator (now routed through `GetScanTypeEvent.GetFor`), prime inlining bait; an inlined call site never sees a Harmony detour, and an engine/Unity update flipping that decision produces exactly "stopped at some point" with zero mod changes. Also noted in passing: `doScan` is GEMSTONE-gated in BOTH eras (plain rocks never scanned — if the remembered behavior was on plain rocks, the memory is of gemstone throws). **`tfgeoscan` (run by hand, not tfbatch): attaches the scan part to the nearest wall (Duration 50 for leisurely eyeballing) and asks the question three ways — compiled call / REFLECTION call (never inlined; answers "did the patch apply at all") / `Strings.WoundLevel` output (the actual display string). Matrix in the wish doc: reflect FALSE = patch never applied · reflect TRUE + bare WoundLevel = INLINING CONFIRMED · reflect TRUE + numeric WoundLevel = engine chain fine, bug is upstream (part not attaching on live gemstone throws — next probe is a real throw).** Fix shapes on deck once the probe names the channel (both CLAUDE, unruled): (a) re-aim the postfix at `Strings.WoundLevelN`/`WoundLevel` themselves (big methods, won't inline; still Harmony); (b) NATIVE de-Harmony shape — while any DoScan reveal is live, Geomancy grants the caster the engine's own `StructureScannerEquipped` (+Bio for creatures) property and revokes at expiry; kills a Harmony patch per the standing direction, cost = scan readouts apply to ALL visible walls/creatures during the window, not just the revealed radius (scope-widening = design call, user's). ~~`[verify]` next boot: `tfgeoscan` beside a wall, read the verdict line + look at the wall.~~ **PROBE RAN 2026-08-12 (user, same session): compiled=True, reflected=True, WoundLevel NUMERIC on a Shale wall, and the user eyeballed EXACT HP in the look UI — ENGINE CHAIN FINE. Inlining hypothesis DEAD; patch applies and is consulted; display renders. The bug is cornered in the ATTACH PATH on real throws: relay not firing vs doScan rolling false vs the wall-attach loop missing.** **LIVE THROWS SAME SESSION (watcher-confirmed): EB sculpted stones land with `scan=False` — BY DESIGN (the `doScan` gemstone gate is verbatim-identical in the ava-era 1.1 backup; plain-rock throws have NEVER attached the scan part in any era) — and `Rough Agate` lands with `scan=True`, relay firing on every throw. VERDICT: NOT A BUG — a DESIGN GAP.** The user's play loop (EB stones at walls) never had a scan in it; the remembered rock-throw wall HP was either gemstone throws or a pre-gate engine behavior (old assembly unavailable, immaterial — fix is the same). Remaining eyeball: user confirms exact HP visible on a wall inside the agate's reveal radius (log half green; tfgeoscan already proved the display chain, so this is expected-yes). **RULING OWED (user's), options on record: (1) keep as-is — gemstones are the scanner, matches shipped text, zero cost · (2) CLAUDE LEAN — rocks scan WALLS only (structural readout on any stone throw; creatures stay gemstone-only; preserves the gemstone premium, restores the readout to the actual play loop; small `OnStoneThrown` change splitting doScan walls-vs-creatures at the attach loop; text consequence: description's gemstone line needs a user rewrite) · (3) all stones scan everything — flattens the gemstone niche, not advocated.**

## Calcify

*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md: Small fixes)*

### Design calls
- [ ] `[design]` **Balance target — RULED: par with Force Wall, a bit stronger for the investment.** Force Wall ground truth (decompiled): mental, 3 MP, 9 forcefields fixed at all levels, duration 14+2×Lv (34 at 10), cooldown 100, owner can shoot missiles OUT through it. Calcify: physical, 2 MP, 3+Lv/2 walls (3→8), **permanent**, 500×ceil(Lv/2) HP, cooldown 90, opaque (blocks own missiles too), adjacent-start placement only, Stoneshaper can carve its walls. Assessment: Calcify is count-weaker early, decisively stronger late (permanence + HP scale). **RULED + implemented: `BASE_WALLS_CREATED` 3 → 4** (4 at Lv1, exactly 9 at Lv10 — matching Force Wall's count at full investment, in permanent stone). Otherwise numbers stand.
*(resolved -> TODO-archive.md, 2026-08-22 prune: whorling stones cut / DV-text mismatch mooted by true-copy)*
- [ ] `[design]` **Fugue-clone Calcify walls outlive the clone (user live-fire flag 2026-07-24, verify session 2: "temporal fugue calcify walls remain after the fugue clone dissipates") — RECON DONE, BUILD SHAPE READY, awaiting ruling.** As-is, a temporary clone leaves PERMANENT walls (free wall economy from a duration ability; also fires via the blurring rock's fugue clone). Decomp-verified build shape: fugue clones carry the `FugueCopy` string property (`TemporalFugue.CreateFugueCopyOf`, line 299); Calcify's wall placement checks `ParentObject.HasStringProperty("FugueCopy")` and, when true, attaches the engine's own `ExistenceSupport` part (`SupportedBy` = the casting clone, `ValidateEveryTurn` = true) to each wall — walls dissipate the moment their maker does, normal casts untouched. Options if ruled differently: Temporary-with-duration instead (walls linger a fixed time after the clone), or keep persistence as-is (Qud-weird freebie). Claude lean: ExistenceSupport — "walls as temporal as their maker." **RULED the lean 2026-08-05 (user, beta-schedule review: "sounds fine") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day (compiles clean, hash-verified — active next restart):** `PlaceWall` in Mutation_Calcify.cs checks the caster for the engine's `FugueCopy` string property and attaches `ExistenceSupport` (SupportedBy = the clone, ValidateEveryTurn) to each wall it raises — clone dissipates, walls follow; normal casts untouched; the blurring rock's fugue clone rides the same property for free. CALC cast log gains "fugue-tethered" when it fires. `[verify]` Friday boot: fugue-clone a Calcify caster, have the clone wall off, watch the walls vanish when the clone expires; a normal cast's walls persist as ever.
- [x] `[design]` **NPC-caster gap - RULED + BUILT 2026-07-23**; NPC calcify casting verified under the 2026-08-06 advertisement pass. Full record -> TODO-archive.md (2026-08-29 prune).
- [ ] `[design]` **True-copy walls — RULED 2026-08-06 (beta rebalance pass, item 1; user: "calcify should fully copy the wall it's adjacent to if possible, including description and stats. it should no longer gain HP scaling per level") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** Walls are now full copies of their source blueprint: native HP, AV, DV, and description all stand; level buys count only (4 → 9). Supersedes: the 500×⌈Lv/2⌉ HP formula (`GetWallHp` retired), the 2026-07-25 0-AV/-10-DV ratification (walls keep real armor now), and the HP clause of the par-with-Force-Wall ruling above (count parity at Lv10 stands; the defense dial moved from level to geology — sandstone 150 HP/AV 8, shale 200/10, limestone 1000/15, slate 3000/25, granite 26000/40). `Cleo_TerraFirma_CalcifyWallPart` reduced to a marker (the "calcified" name prefix + the fugue-tether anchor); its shift fields survive for pre-rebalance saves. Fallback blueprint (no natural stone anywhere) rides its Sandstone inherit, 150 HP/AV 8, and carries the old bespoke description in XML now — CLAUDE DIAL, flag if the floor feels wrong. New strings DRAFT for signoff: level-text line "Walls are true copies of the local stone" and ability-card sentence "Creates N walls, true copies of the local stone." `[verify]` cast beside shale and beside limestone: look shows the source stone's description + real stats under the "calcified" prefix; fallback zone shows gray stone at 150 HP.
- [ ] `[design]` **Every-level lattice + scaling cooldown — RULED 2026-08-06 (user flag: "I need to make sure every level up increases something on the mutations, including on calcify"; on the cooldown proposal: "we can try out cool down") + BUILT + DEPLOYED same day.** True-copy left Calcify's odd levels dead (walls tick on evens only; the retired HP formula had covered odds), and the audit confirmed it was the ONLY leveled mutation with dead levels (Gorgon/EB/Stoneshaper/Fissure all move something every level). Fix: cooldown now `max(100 − 10×⌈Lv/2⌉, 50)` — 90 at Lv1-2 (Lv1 unchanged), then 80/70/60/50 stepping on each odd level; evens keep buying the wall. Base precedent for declining cooldowns, decomp-verified: Burgeoning 115−10L, Teleport Other 115−10L, Belcher 210−10L, Sticky Tongue 22−L, Burrowing 15−L. The old "permanent product ⇒ flat cooldown" line was a CLAUDE recommendation (redoubt-numbers-draft) premised on level buying wall HP — premise dissolved with true-copy, provenance checked before superseding. Level text cooldown line now rules-wrapped (it scales). Framed as TRY-OUT — feel-session revisit expected. **Companion ruling, general (user): "1 cost and defect mutations don't level" — Geomancy and Lithovore exempt from the every-level rule by design.** `[verify]` level a calcifier 1→10: ability card cooldown reads 90/90/80/80/70/70/60/60/50/50 while wall count reads 4/5/5/6/6/7/7/8/8/9.

### Stonefrog — NEW unique creature (user concept 2026-07-22; recon DONE, numbers drafted, SETTLE NEXT SESSION — nothing built)
**Concept (user):** not a statue of an existing creature — an original stonefrog. Frog's anatomy, looks like a rock, jumps to attack enemies. **Ruled so far:** role = **BOTH** fall-table ally (DropRock brain-branch, joins ape/statuette) AND wild cave hazard (dynamic encounter tables); **tongue = fully OUT** (ceiling-tongue outside engine without abstraction — user's own call; Sticky Tongue mutation + tongue lash exist but are "Very invasive" for a monster like this — user hesitant, skipped).
**Recon (decompiled/grepped, all verified):** `Frog` anatomy exists (Bodies.xml:349); `BaseFrog` (Str 12/Agi 16/Tou 12, DarkVision, ColdResistance −25, Frogs faction, full frog VO) with family anchors — ice frog L12/HP30/AV3 (+1d10 bite), svardym (jump+charge kit), Frog Golem (constructed-frog precedent). **Jump-attacks are native AI:** `Acrobatics_Jump` skill answers AIGetMovement/Offensive/RetreatAbilityList — two XML lines buy leap-at/leap-in/leap-away; svardym adds `Tactics_Charge` for the pounce. **Disguise depths:** (a) flavor-only render, XML; (b) **stone camouflage — Claude recommends**: `FoliageCamouflage`/`ICamouflage` pattern re-keyed to the mod's `IsAnyStone` tag → +DV while among rocks (serves BOTH roles: ally hides in your fissure fallout, wild one lurks in cave rubble), C#-small (one effect + trigger); (c) true ambush mimic (`Hidden`/`HiddenRender`, renders as an inert rock until it strikes) — most work, serves only the wild role, needs deeper recon. **DISGUISE DEPTH UNRULED.**
**Numbers draft (ALL Claude, unratified):** band 2 weight 5 (statuette's neighborhood); L8 / HP 25 / AV 5 (frog ~3 + stone-body +2); stone rules applied per standing ruling (AcidResistance −50, NoBleed); **cold weakness removed** (BaseFrog −25 is flesh-frog; stone doesn't freeze — flag); kit = Acrobatics_Jump + Tactics_Charge; stone bite 1d6 (stone-mimicry rule); camo +2 DV (foliage-camo's own level); **no MentalShield** (living stone animal, not animated statue — beguilable/dominatable, flagged for veto); **corpse = 1 plain fallen rock** (stalagmite Corpse-drop pattern — kill the stonefrog, throw the stonefrog); wild spawn via cave-tier dynamic tables (exact table names to verify at build); VO mix = frog idle/attack + rock hurt/death (croaks like a frog, chips like a stone).
**To settle next session:** disguise depth (menu RESHAPED by the 2026-08-11 mimic recon below — a+/basilisk/Hidden, mix-and-match) · numbers sign-off · MentalShield call · corpse-rock call · cold-weakness call · which dynamic tables + wild rarity.
**Disguise-depth signal (user, 2026-08-11 roster sitting — NOT a ruling):** "still considering rock frog that disguises itself as a stone before breaking camouflage and jumping" — reads as the true ambush mimic. Cost note: Hidden's "needs deeper recon" caveat is now mostly PAID — the sprouting-orb decomp (same sitting, trapdoor-spider recon in the roster section) covered Hidden's discovery mechanics (Difficulty check, found-on-step, walkable cell, navigation weights).
**MIMIC RECON 2026-08-11 (user-directed: "check out lithofexes ... and how they do their disguises"; base-game ground truth, decomp + base XML, all verified):** the base game ships TWO stone-mimic patterns, and the user's remembered "spawns with husks and hides among them" is the **Molting Basilisk** (base Creatures.xml:6216, Bethesda Susa lurker), not the lithofex.
- **Lithofex pattern — the render pun, zero code:** no disguise part exists anywhere (grep-verified: only StoneGaze.cs + a saving-throw label mention it). `RandomStatue.SetCreature` copies the depicted creature's Tile/RenderString onto every statue and paints it gray, so "gray creature tile" MEANS statue on a Qud screen — and the lithofex renders `&y`/`Y`, statue-gray, among the real statues its own StoneGaze kills accrete (victims become `Random Stone Statue` objects seeded with a DeepCopy; 50/50 pray-at/desecrate Shrine — the garden lures you close). Glance lies, examine is honest ("lithofex" on look).
- **Molting Basilisk pattern — full identity theft + self-seeded decoys (`MoltingBasilisk.cs`):** (1) on first EnteredCell during zone build it scatters **7–11 `Molting Basilisk Husk` items** at random empty cells — solid, occluding, RenderLayer 6, same tile/colors as the live creature; (2) `SyncState()` keyed on Target==null flips its ENTIRE presentation — DisplayName becomes "molting basilisk husk", Description becomes the husk item's text verbatim, `HideCon=1` suppresses the con readout (the one UI tell of a live creature) — so it lies at glance AND examine until it acquires a target, then everything snaps back (state flip, not a spawn); (3) brain = `Hostile=false Wanders=false Hibernating=true` kill radius 1/1 — motionless sleeper, adjacency killer; (4) bite = 3d3 vibro `StoneOnHit` (the quartz-fake petrifies you into its own aesthetic). Resync hooks: AfterObjectCreated / BeginTakeAction-until-created / AICombatStart.
- **Reshaped depth menu for the frog (CLAUDE framing, options not rivals — mix-and-match):** **(a+)** rigorous palette costume — old depth (a) done exactly: frog wears a real mod rock's tile + colors, ambush-at-a-glance for zero C# (the lithofex lesson: the palette IS the mechanic) · **(basilisk)** SyncState-style identity theft — name/desc/HideCon flip until a target exists; ~40 lines of part code, base-game-proven; frog's structural gift over the basilisk: its decoy is a GENERIC ROCK and the mod already carpets floors with those (fissure fallout, spent EB ammo, the ruled corpse-rock — the player seeds the ambush themselves); optional basilisk-style zone-build rock scatter guarantees cover in clean caves · **(Hidden)** the sprouting-orb machinery — object absent until found-on-step; recon paid but returns to being the spider's alone if unused here.
- **STONEFROG PARKED 2026-08-15 (user: "let's not worry about stonefrog yet") — the settle sitting is not in this pass; everything below stays a lean until it happens.** **CLAUDE LEAN, UNRULED (supersedes the earlier "(a+) plus Hidden" lean from the same conversation): (a+) + basilisk flip, no Hidden.** The user's own signal phrase — "disguises itself as a stone before BREAKING camouflage" — is literally the SyncState flip; and a rock you can SEE but can't trust beats an object you can't see (Hidden's whole effect) for this creature's fantasy. Pounce = `Acrobatics_Jump` (native AI leap-at, already in the numbers draft) replacing the basilisk's wait-for-adjacency. Ecology note worth protecting at the ruling: EB players carpet fight scenes with exactly the litter a stonefrog hides in — the mimic depth and the ruled corpse-rock reinforce each other ("dead stonefrogs become the cover for live ones").

## Throw-augmenting items — THREAD RETIRED 2026-08-13 (user ruling at the cobblefur reconciliation; sole heir: the chalk pouch, promoted below)

*(the four dispositions — sling CUT / chalk container SURVIVES as the chalk pouch (own section below) / stabilizer bands CUT + XML scrubbed / visor CUT -> TODO-archive.md, 2026-08-22 prune)*

**Standing facts that outlive the thread (numbers-sitting REQUIRED READING):**
- **Deft-shadowing:** Deft Throwing's skill-line close-accuracy = 50; engine takes max(item-line, skill-line) → the bracer's +25 (even paired +50) is INVISIBLE to Deft holders. Bracer as drafted = early-game pre-skill item; the final Bonus pick must know this.
- **Cap constraint:** item-line close-accuracy sources SUM; totals past 100 flip the aim-variance multiplier negative. Any future granter (the chalk pouch is first in line) MUST use the cap-aware lend/return idiom — `Part_CobblefurBracer.cs` is the reference.

**DIALOGUE OPENED 2026-08-16 (user-written text sitting; all lines are the USER'S, verbatim, no Claude drafts accepted).** Four conversations wired in `XML/Conversations.xml`, each `ConversationScript` sitting on the ROLE so the mole variant inherits it by descent:

| speaker | ID | lines | target |
|---|---|---|---|
| snapjaw miner | `Cleo_TerraFirma_SnapjawMiner` | 2 | ~8 |
| outrider | `Cleo_TerraFirma_Outrider` | 2 | ~8 |
| mason | `Cleo_TerraFirma_Mason` | 4 | ~8 |
| hewer | `Cleo_TerraFirma_Hewer` | 2 | ~8 |

**Bank-size target measured, not guessed (user: "pull me up how many lines a stilt merchant has, i'll aim for that many"):** `BaseMerchant` points at `GenericMerchant` (16 lines), but every named Stilt trade overrides it with its own bank — bookbinder/scribe/hatter/apple merchant 7, vintner 8, beekeeper/kipper/shoemaker 9, apothecary 13. **So 7-9 is the real per-trade weight.** Every bank above is a stub until it reaches that.

Findings from the sitting, all verified against base or against our own files:
- **The guild was MUTE.** `BaseHumanoid` carries no `ConversationScript`, so the whole roster was unclickable. The outrider is the first quarrier voice.
- **The snapjaw miner was NOT.** He inherits `Snapjaw`, which carries `ConversationID="Snapjaw"`, so he had been saying the generic `ehehehehe` barks all along. His entry OVERRIDES rather than adds. Merging into base `Snapjaw` was rejected: it would put pick-and-tunnel lines in every snapjaw in Qud.
- **Water ritual, trade and join-party arrive FREE.** Base `Conversations.xml` opens with `BaseConversation`, "inherited by all conversations by default," carrying `WaterRitualChoice` and its subtree. This is what the Quarriers' `WaterRitualJoin`-defaults-true finding of 2026-08-15 was waiting on: a written bank was the whole remaining cost of recruitability.
- **Faction `DefaultAddress` has NO conversation token.** Checked every token base uses; they are all `=player.formalAddressTerm=` and kin, i.e. the player's own term, never the faction's word for the player. Issachari hardcode "lack-salt" into node text, and the user's "wayfarer" is hardcoded the same way. `Live and drink, wayfarer.` is also already a line in `GenericMerchant`, which settles it as courteous-commercial register rather than the wary one.
- **`HaveItem` + `TakeItem` are stock conversation parts** (Argyve's 200 feet of wire runs on both). The hewer's "got any witchwood?" could become a real handover gated on the player carrying bark, zero C#. `Cleo_StartingGear_Stonecarver` hands the player 2-4 Witchwood Bark, so a stonecarver arrives holding exactly what he asks for. NOT BUILT, flagged in the file.
- **The hewer's aquifer line is the exception to a rule the warren generator encodes.** `warren-lab.html` seals one brine pocket per warren at `wetChance` 0.45, and `wetAvoid` exists so tunnels visibly bend around it (hover text: "diggers don't breach aquifers"). Text arriving AHEAD of its terrain: the pocket is lab-side only, `ZonePainter.cs` has no wet code yet, and TODO already floats a pre-breached variant.

- [ ] `[bug]` **ROCKHOUND THROWS ITS OWN GUT — USER LIVE-FIRE 2026-08-27 ("once it ate it, it immediately equipped and threw it?"), DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same sitting (compile green, hash-verified; ACTIVE NEXT RESTART, not blueprint reload — C#):** swallow-into-inventory + the brain's reequip pass = the loot becomes gear (the beetlebum-wears-the-hat behavior; stones are thrown weapons, so the hound's version is slot-and-throw, leaking kill-spills-the-hoard). Fix is the engine's own knob: every AI equip branch skips items with `NoAIEquip` (Brain decomp 4025/4102/4127, thrown-fill included; players exempt in the same checks) — Part_RockSweeper now stamps `NoAIEquip` per-instance on each swallowed stone. Floor stones stay armable by snapjaws; accepted wart on record: the mark rides spilled stones after death, so NPC scavengers won't arm gut-stones. **First live `[HOUND]` swallow line also on record this sitting (Small Stone, gut 1) — log-half of the sweeper verify. Eat-variety verify friction answered: the manners guard only blocks player-CONTROLLED hounds, not player-dropped stones, so drop any IsAnyStone item under 100 lbs in its path (sculpted pebble, ephemeral stone).** `[verify]` after restart: swallowed stone stays in the gut through combat (no equip, no throw) · kill still spills the hoard · a snapjaw can still arm a never-swallowed floor stone.

Deployed strict-valid, hash-verified. `[verify]` next boot: ~1 in 7 outriders wears a bracelet or neck-ring.
