Two committed concepts (user, ruled): **(1)** quarrier packs in the canyons carrying 3x3 lumps of rock they've carved free from the walls; **(2)** a Stilt bazaar merchant selling non-expiring fissure stones as a random tent spawn — cheap, one-use utility grenades to stock up on.

**Lore direction (user, ruled):** the stonecarvers came from outside Qud (why they're a mod, not basegame). No established holds — at most a small temporary camp they stop at on pilgrimages through Qud, drawn by its rich canyons and deep caves full of varied stone. They bring their culture, their mutations (the mod's seven), and a new lens on the game. Consequence: no procedural-village surface; everything is zone-build-time content that reaches existing saves in unvisited zones. *(2026-08-23: the zone-build-time half of this consequence is SUPERSEDED IN PART for warren placement — the user's lean at the world-builder recon picks the world-build hook, new saves only, on the two-mod-split rationale; see WARREN v2 § SPAWN RATE, "FORK ANSWERED". Zone contents still build lazily; only site placement moves to embark.)*

### WARREN v2 — GOAL STATEMENT + BRANCH CHARTER (branch opened 2026-08-17)

**Why a branch (user, 2026-08-17):** *"i feel like there's a lot of rulings and things that have piled up that are leading us towards an inevitable result in game"* + the art-class rule the user's sister passed on — *"after you finish something, even if you liked the first result, do it again. the second result will at least teach you something, and you might like it more."* Second pass, drawn against a stated brief this time. **v1 is NOT superseded and NOT deleted** — same treatment the legacy camp chain got at the 2026-08-08 fresh-slate reset: v1 stays rollable side by side, v2 forks the lab with its own localStorage namespace.

**The gap that motivated it, confirmed by grep 2026-08-17: the warren record had NO goal statement.** Eleven drawings, six calibration rounds, three dress passes, size classes, floor budgets — and the only sentence anywhere saying what the space is FOR was a Claude line in `design/cavegen-survey-2026-08-07.md:272` ("the warren, whose whole bet is that the geometry itself reads as made"), tagged unruled and never ratified. Closest the user ever came was mid-course on 2026-08-08 (*"i might be diluting the thing that makes it feel like a stonecarver's quarry"*), and that got answered with a mechanism (no-tunnel-without-a-reason) rather than with the goal it was reaching for.

#### GOAL STATEMENT — USER RULED 2026-08-17. The user's words; every v2 decision argues from this.

> "the quarry warrens are a sort of halfway point between procgen villages and goatfolk villages. they are part settlement, part camp, part work area. they sometimes might contain a recipe you haven't had before, that you can only eat in this one camp. or a legendary stonecarver and their entourage, that you can encourage to join you. or a few fragments of history or gossip from faraway lands and those close to their home. sometimes, they've cultivated a provisioner in the walls - a jilted lover and consortium representative that will trade with you and restock periodically. they don't assign quests, nor are they immediately hostile to the player. learning the location of a quarry warren should be an 'oh neat!' moment earlygame, while goatfolk villages turn to groans extremely quickly."

**The bar is a CONTRAST bar, so the named failure mode is part of the spec.** Goatfolk villages groan because every instance is the same instance. Ground truth already on record (`VillageMaker`/`VillageBase` decomp 2026-08-11): goatfolk identity is exactly three things — `ThatchedWall`, `RoundBuildings`, and the population table — one of which is a wall material. Procgen villages get theirs from a generated history, i.e. a DATA layer sitting above the geometry. **"Halfway between the two" therefore requires an identity layer above the carve, and v1 has none** — v1's entire identity is its carve grammar, which is fewer things than goatfolk carry.

**COLLISION NAMED, and it is the reason the branch exists:** v1's own judging criterion was *"does the language hold over thirty rolls — the one i actually judge on"* (user, warren-lab handoff 2026-08-11). That is a SAMENESS test, and sameness is the goatfolk groan mechanism. The first pass was tuned toward the failure mode the goal statement is defined against.

#### THE STAGE AXIS — USER RULED 2026-08-17 (answering "is a warren one register, or all three at once")

> "the more large/dense a warren is, the most it expands outwards into settlement territory. the roots of it being a work area are always visible, and as they progress it becomes more of a camp. and then finally it fleshes out to have more decorative areas devoted to making people happy rather than producing more stone - the sign of a successful dig"

One axis governs size, register, payload count and dress: **work area → camp → settlement-with-leisure.** Two consequences the ruling carries on its face:
- **Accretion, not selection.** "the roots of it being a work area are always visible" makes the stages STRATA. The work core survives into the finished site and later stages grow onto it. A young dig is a finished site, not a failed one — the generator grows in passes and stops at a rolled stage.
- **Leisure is the success tell.** Decorative space devoted to people rather than to production is how the player reads that the dig succeeded. This is the axis's payoff and it is the layer with the least content behind it (see costs).

#### PAYLOADS — USER RULED 2026-08-17: 0–2 per warren, riding the stage axis

> "0-2 payloads - an abandoned warren could have nothing, while a flourishing large warren could have a legendary overseer *and* a merchant"

Prosperity buys population. The four named payloads, audited against what actually stands behind each (2026-08-17):

| payload | state |
|---|---|
| legendary overseer + entourage, recruitable | **the most-built thing in the workstream.** Name-drives-kit RULED 08-15, the epithet bank IS the pool manifest, water ritual arrives free off `BaseConversation`, faction entry ruled. Waiting on a builder. |
| a provisioner cultivated in the walls | **fully designed 2026-08-11** as the vine merchant — `Jilted Lover` chassis (wall-living, non-hostile, holds wares, natively spawns in desert canyons), `Consortium-100` guild-first off Yurl's sheet, `GenericInventoryRestocker`, max ONE, mid-to-large only. The user's "jilted lover and consortium representative" restates a standing ruling. |
| history/gossip from faraway lands and near home | direction ruled (outside Qud; Sunderlies / Athenreach / Oth), four conversation banks wired 08-16 — **all stubs at 2–4 lines against the measured 7–9 target.** |
| a recipe you can only eat in this one camp | **NOTHING UNDER IT.** Cooking effects QUARANTINED 2026-08-11; the vine's own wares ruling parks a hook reading "if we add a food item to the mod, this can slot in" — and no food item exists. Newest idea in the brief, least support. Scope flag, not a blocker. |

**Ruled by omission and it costs something: no quests.** Base-game villages draw partly on quest hooks; taking the halfway point without them means every "oh neat" has to be discoverable inside the visit. Nothing arrives by being sent somewhere.

**Distribution is NOT the lever — USER, 2026-08-17:** *"there's a decent amount of canyons so i expect more than a few warrens to end up rolling, though i'm aware that number is tunable."* Rarity will not carry the anti-groan bar. Per-instance difference has to.

#### OPEN — the one fork the user has not ruled

- [ ] `[design]` **Is ABANDONED a stage on the axis, or a second axis?** **CLAUDE READ, UNRULED:** second axis. **Built extent** (how far the dig got) and **occupancy** (is anyone still here) separate cleanly, and separating them buys a large abandoned warren — settlement geometry, leisure hall, nobody in it. Same carve, population stripped, decay dressed over the top. Doubles the instance vocabulary at every extent, which matters given the user expects many warrens to roll.

#### CLAUDE PROPOSALS, UNRULED — do not cite these back as rules

- **Spatial law: age radiates from the face.** A quarry's face advances AWAY from the mouth, and nobody lives at the face — you live in the ground already taken. So settlement occupies worked-out rock near the entrance and active work sits deepest. Two gradients running opposite: **social payoff at the front, material payoff (fresh stone, the wet pocket, geology) at the back.** Inverted from dungeon logic on purpose. Red Rock's depth-ranked-region law ("far from the entrance is where the good thing goes" — flagged in `cavegen-survey-2026-08-07.md` as a steal we have never taken) still applies; there are simply two good things at opposite ends.
- **Costs, so the branch is not priced wrong.** (1) **Staged growth is harder to keep sound than one-pass growth** — every later pass must attach without breaching earlier ones, and each existing guard (wet-pocket avoid, protected shell, `sealDiag`, the articulation guard) would need to know which pass it is in. Real engineering, not a refactor. (2) **The leisure stratum is content that does not exist.** Dress tables cover work evidence, the gallery, and communal blocks; "spaces devoted to making people happy" is a table never written, it carries the most culture of any layer, and by the standing text rule its voice is the user's. **The flourishing stage is expensive in the USER's time, not Claude's.**

#### WHAT CARRIES OVER — USER DIRECTION 2026-08-17

> "the thing i'm thinking is about not relying on the engineering from the first part - the seals, the primitives, we can always dial back and re-examine what a quarry should look like"

Three buckets. The operative word for bucket 3 is **EMPTY, not pruned** — port-and-prune keeps everything, because whatever already sits in the file wins every argument by being there.

1. **ENGINE GROUND TRUTH — CARRIES.** Not design opinion, cost real archaeology, re-deriving it is waste. `PaintWall` mask order (N NE E SE S SW W NW, off-map neighbours read 0); all 256 `wall_rock` masks stocked; the Drillbot's `AddNoise` being a FLAT-98 blob field rather than a smooth cost field; Redrock seeding from doors with `emptyBase` 200 against a wall's 80; `DirtPath` on every village hut interior; the re-decompiled `RiverBuilder`/`CellularGrid`.
2. **THE RIG — CARRIES.** Knob panel, roll history carrying per-roll seed AND knobs, contact sheet, the real extracted wall sprites, gallery view. A microscope is not a specimen.
3. **CARVE LANGUAGE + GUARD STACK — STARTS EMPTY.** Primitives, seals, part tables, fit rules. A piece returns only by being argued from the goal statement above.

**Flagged at the ruling so it is not a quiet loss: bucket 3 contains eleven of the user's own drawings** (comb, wraps, ranks, rake, suite, trunks, hooks). Those are the user's hand transcribed, not Claude engineering. The argument for dialing them back is the user's own and it is a good one — every drawing was made before a brief existed, answering "what does a stonecarver's warren look like" with nothing on the other side of the question, and **a drawing made under no brief does not automatically survive the brief arriving.** Same move as the 2026-08-08 fresh-slate reset, which worked.

**Sorting rule for the re-earn pass (CLAUDE, offered): legibility rulings travel across a re-brief, taste rulings do not.** `sealDiag` came from a player-experience fact — a room enterable by corner-kiss that you would walk past and never notice — and that fact is still true in v2, so expect it back, but by re-argument rather than inheritance. The shard sweep was a taste call about how deliberate the rock reads; a new brief is allowed to overturn taste.

**Branch mechanics:** fork `warren-lab.html` to a v2 lab with its own localStorage namespace (the `tfwarren*` precedent). v1 stays rollable side by side. Nothing deleted, nothing inherited by default.

#### DRAWING PROTOCOL v2 — CLAUDE PROPOSAL 2026-08-17, awaiting the user's OK

Answering the user's question, *"what makes a good drawing from me?"* Evidence base is the eleven-drawing corpus and its recorded misreads.

- **SIZE — whole-site is the default this pass; zoom only for a piece with no precedent.** The v2 questions are compositional (what a young dig looks like beside a flourishing one; where settlement sits relative to the face), and composition only exists at site scale. **But every recorded misread happened at piece scale, and all of them were resolution failures** — drawing 2's dots were posts drawn light-on-black, drawing 3's identical mark in inverted contrast got read as a sealed chamber, and the user's correction ("just a pillar there or something, but it could be anything in the center") is what produced the wrap-centre-as-slot ruling. The rule that kills the whole class: **draw so the smallest thing you MEAN is at least 3 marks across.** If one mark carries a whole primitive, Claude is guessing — and the record shows Claude guesses, then builds the guess. Roughly true proportion helps: the zone is 80×25, so a site drawing wants to be wide, about 3:1.
- **AMOUNT — 3 to 5, drawn as a SET, in one sitting, before any code.** Not the v1 cadence. The first corpus arrived one drawing at a time across sessions, each triggering its own implement-and-verify round, which means **the differences between drawing 3 and drawing 7 were accidents of when they were drawn, and those accidents were read as design.** A set drawn against itself makes the variance deliberate — exactly what a stage axis needs, since the axis IS the variance. Natural set: one per stage (work-only dig / camp / flourishing), plus one anti-target, plus an optional zoom. Anti-targets pay — drawing 5 was self-flagged *"a bit too symmetrical but was fun to make"* and still yielded the flanked threshold — **but only because it was labelled.** Unlabelled, its symmetry would have been read as target.
- **COMMUNICATION — four cheap things and one loop.**
  1. **A legend.** Four to six marks down the side with what each means. Kills the dot-vs-pillar class outright.
  2. **Stable polarity across the set**, or state it per drawing: which value is rock, which is space. The worst misread in the corpus was one mark in inverted contrast.
  3. **One line of intent** — not a description, just "the thing i'm showing here is X." Given five ideas in a drawing, Claude weights them by visual loudness rather than by importance.
  4. **Label target vs anti-target.** One word.
  5. **THE LOOP, highest-value item on the list: Claude reads the drawing back as a numbered list BEFORE building anything, and the user strikes the invented entries.** The sealed-vault reading — demoted to a parked float, "it was never in the drawing" — is content Claude invented from a mark and then implemented. A readback is the only thing that catches invention, because from the inside invention looks exactly like a finding.
- **Highest-bandwidth mode on the record: a drawing that ANSWERS A STATED QUESTION.** Drawing 11 (the pillared gallery) transferred cleanly and immediately because the user drew it against a gap Claude had just named ("no deliberate large space" → *"something like this?"*). Claude cannot invent intent when the drawing is an answer to a posed question. **Offered: Claude names the v2 gaps as questions first, the user draws the answers.**

#### JUDGING BAR — USER RULED 2026-08-18 (replaces v1's criterion; user: "i totally agree with that metric, actually. it's something i've internally been trying to do on the last one")

**Roll two sites. Can the user tell them apart and say what each one IS?** — "a young dig with one worked pocket" / "a mature one, pillared hall with a loop through it." If two rolls both read as *just a warren*, the identity layer failed and we know inside twenty rolls.

This is the anti-groan test made falsifiable, and it is the deliberate inverse of v1's bar (*"does the language hold over thirty rolls"*), which was a SAMENESS test. **Stated before the first roll, on purpose** — v1's rubric was never written down, so every judgement drifted toward it by default.

#### PALETTE + LEGEND — SETTLED 2026-08-17/18 (drawing exchange)

**white = solid · grey = floor · black = not part of this drawing.** One-cell black margin around every canvas, so "continues off-frame" is carried by the drawing rather than by the crop.

Rationale, and it is ground truth rather than taste: **the engine has no wall object.** A wall IS a rock cell — `wall_rock` autotiles off its neighbours, all 256 masks stocked. The earlier white-wall/black-rock split implied a distinction the target cannot represent. Collapsing it also converts black from an absence into a positive mark: floor touching black now means *the site continues past the frame*, one reading instead of two.

Measured on the corpus: artery v1 carried 121 black cells (36% of canvas) and **4 floor-cells touching raw black — every ambiguity in the readback came from those 4.** Artery v2 carries 25 black (9%), **0 ambiguous cells, 16 floor cells running off-frame.** The class was eliminated, not reduced.

#### THE TEN RULES — extracted from the user's three v2 drawings, 2026-08-17/18

Corpus at `design/drawings/v2/` (`organblock.png` 11×13, `artery.png` + `artery2.png` 24×14 / 22×12, `pillaredroom.png` 18×18). All 1:1, decoded cell-exact rather than eyeballed.

**Global**
1. **The artery IS the haulage way — USER 2026-08-18:** *"i imagine the artery is used for hauling - that direct path makes sense to me to connect to the main entrance of the quarry and trace through most of the compound."* Two hard constraints on the generator (one end anchored at the zone entrance; coverage of most of the compound) plus a free dressing rule for stage 3 — **you do not clutter a haul road.**
2. **Corridors always deliver into a room. Rooms are allowed to dead-end.** The asymmetry is deliberate — the user kept exactly one sealed dead-end room through both artery revisions.
3. **Rooms sit ON the route, not off it.** The artery passes *through* rooms; v1's halls delivered destinations at their ends. Different circulation model, and the one that serves "a site you walk through."

**Regional**
4. **Crosscuts make circuits.** Parallel drifts plus occasional connections between them; `pillaredroom` has two N–S drifts (x=8, x=14) and two E–W runs (rows 4, 9), yielding two genuine closed loops. Loops are the highest-value navigation feature because they kill backtracking.
5. **Loops beat trees.** `organblock`'s internal circulation is a circuit around the partition mass, not a branch. v1's suite branched.

**Local**
6. **One cell, everywhere.** Every partition and every doorway across all four drawings is exactly 1 cell. No exceptions, no wide openings.
7. **Partitions are PARTIAL.** They stop short rather than sealing; `organblock`'s long vertical partition is open at both ends. Rooms are implied by division, never closed by it.
8. **PILLARS ARE PLACED BY SPAN, NOT BY PATTERN — USER 2026-08-18:** *"i tried to keep them 1 pixel away from the wall except in situations where the wall itself would likely provide sufficient support."* **Measured and the invariant holds with zero exceptions:** every free-standing pillar sits at Chebyshev ≥2 from its room boundary (i.e. never less than one clear floor cell). Counts scale with span — **4w room → 0 pillars · 7w → 2 · 12w → 9.** Where mass would land inside the clearance margin it MERGES into the wall as a **spur** rather than standing free (two in the lower room: a 3-tall off the north wall, a 2-tall off the south). *Claude correction on record: these were first read as "irregular, hand-worked" aesthetics. Wrong — the irregularity is the OUTPUT of a support rule, not a style choice.*
9. **A plain room is a SMALL room, and its plainness is information.** `pillaredroom`'s upper-left is 4×5 with zero pillars because a 4-wide span needs no support. This answers the user's own standing worry ("a lot of these are just going to end up being plain rooms") — the vocabulary does not need to prevent plain rooms, it needs plain rooms to MEAN something, and these already do.
10. **The haul lane stays clear even crossing a worked space.** Row 14 of `pillaredroom` runs clean across all twelve cells of the pillar field while every other row carries solid. **CLAUDE READ, unruled:** if pillars exist to break spans, a clear lane is a deliberate structural exception — the quarry trading roof safety for haulage.

#### ARCHITECTURE — three layers, CLAUDE PROPOSAL accepted in direction 2026-08-18

The size question resolved: **scale in a drawing is resolution on the RULE, not the size of the stamp.** Big fixed footprints fail twice — they repeat, and they fail to place (v1's suite, the one demanding footprint, fired in roughly **one warren in three**: *"fits where the network left space"*). But rules alone is how v1 got mush, because every v1 rule was LOCAL — roll a part, attach it, repeat — and sixteen dice always land on the mean. The missing ingredient is **altitude**, not size.

1. **GLOBAL** — lay the artery. One decision about the whole site, before any local decision exists.
2. **REGIONAL** — the artery partitions remaining mass into pockets. Each pocket gets a **character**, not a footprint.
3. **LOCAL** — inside a pocket the constraints resolve it (rules 6-10).

Variance lives at all three layers, so two sites differ *structurally* rather than statistically. The same rule set stopped at different stages delivers the ruled growth axis for free.

**Two questions this dissolved rather than answered:** stamp-vs-grammar (different layers, not competing table entries) · pillar irregularity (a rule, not a texture).

**And one it did NOT — USER RULED 2026-08-18: "organ blocks *do* need entrances also."** Corrects a Claude claim made the same day that the question was dissolved by construction (the reasoning being that a pocket carved off the artery inherits an opening wherever the artery grazes it). **It is not dissolved: the entrance is part of the block's own definition, not a side effect of attachment.** So `organblock.png` being fully sealed — no floor cell touching its edge, none touching black, verified across all 143 cells — is an incomplete drawing rather than a design statement, and the earlier "sealed by design?" reading is dead. Open at the build, and the user's plural is deliberately preserved: **how many entrances, and whether they are deliberate thresholds or simply where the artery arrives.**

**User's three drawings map one per layer:** `organblock` is a resolved pocket · `artery2` is the global layer with pockets roughed in · `pillaredroom` is the two meeting.

#### CANYON HOST + THE SIZE CENSUS — BUILT AND MEASURED 2026-08-18 (`design/warren-lab-v2.html`)

The `CanyonBuilder` port and the Qud tile rendering carried over from v1 as bucket-1 ground truth (see the parked-track entry). Integration law, re-earned rather than inherited: **the artery stops at canyon floor and never eats it** — the mouth is the one deliberate breach, sited where the band's skin meets the canyon. Verified across 400 rolls: **zero breaches, zero splits, zero doorless rooms.**

**TILES INLINED.** All 256 `wall_rock` masks + 4 `sw_ground_dots` are baked into the lab as base64 data URIs (52KB of PNG). Relative `<img>` paths cannot resolve when the page runs from `file://` inside an embedded preview (served as a `data:` URL with no base directory), so a disk-backed tile bank silently degraded to grey blocks. **Licensing question raised and CLOSED BY THE USER 2026-08-18:** *"the sim is purely for our use and will not be shipped or sold - everything in our mod that's related to it will not be in the finished version, except the carving that is actually in the game once were done."* The gitignore on `design/tiles/walls/` is about the shipped mod, not the design rig.

**THE SIZE CENSUS — the user's question answered with numbers.** User: *"a warren has to fit into a canyon, so it can only be so big, right?"* Right, and harder than expected. First census, 300 rolls, stage rolled uniformly at ~25% each: **built 79.1% young dig / 18.1% working / 2.4% camp / 0.3% flourishing**, 66% of sites degraded. Bare-rock control came out 22/28/25/25, so the dice were fine — the canyon was doing all of it.

**Diagnosis: the constraint was CLAUDE'S, not the canyon's.** Measured over 120 canyons, the best rectangle reachable behind a face ran **width min 16 / median 48 / max 70** against **height min 6 / median 13 / max 23** — bands are long and thin because canyons cut across the zone. Camp wanted 19 tall and flourishing 23, against a median of 13 and a ceiling of 23 in the single best canyon seen. But band MASS runs 543–1992 cells while the best rectangle cut from it is about 624, **so the rectangular footprint was discarding half the band and the stage axis took the blame.** A quarry follows the seam; it should grow wider, not taller.

**FIX: the footprint follows the band.** `growRegion` floods band cells outward from the mouth to an area budget — any shape, naturally long and thin where the band is. Waypoints are drawn from the flood's own depth ordering, so the artery is pulled along the seam instead of across a box. Result, same 300 seeds: **24.0 / 27.3 / 33.0 / 15.7**, zero zones with no warren, degradation 66% → 11.7%.

Two things broke with the rectangle gone, both fixed: **45 of 300 rolls came back disconnected** because `repairConnectivity`'s straight drills can't reach around a snaking region (it now BFSes through carveable rock instead of boring straight); and pocket boxes are rectangles one level down, so they now give ground as placement attempts pile up.

#### YOUNG DIG CUT + THE WORTH-THE-TRIP BAR — USER RULED 2026-08-18

> "if we're going to have these be rumored information (like lairs or farms in the canyons) they are better off making sure each has something to do in them, if that makes sense. and a young dig is the least likely to be worth the player getting notified of in that way, which makes me wonder if we should cut it and just do working/camp/flourishing?"

**New design fact this puts on the record: warrens are RUMOURED locations, the way lairs and farms are.** That makes a rumour a promise, and it converts generation quality into a content guarantee — a site the player is *sent to* has to be worth the walk. This is the anti-groan bar arriving through the discovery layer rather than the geometry one, and it hooks straight into the four conversation banks that are still stubs at 2-4 lines.

**Stage axis is now three: working → camp → flourishing.** The young dig is cut and deliberately NOT replaced — a band too thin to feed the smallest real stage yields **no warren at all**, which is the honest outcome (not every canyon has a quarry in it) and strictly better for a rumoured-location system than a disappointing one.

**Claude refinement, accepted in direction: the bar is about SIZE, not emptiness.** The user's 0–2 payload ruling stands untouched — an abandoned *camp* is a leisure hall with nobody in it and is absolutely worth walking through; an abandoned young dig is a hole. Built extent is what has to clear the bar; occupancy stays free.

**Two guard attempts, and the first one was wrong — recorded so it isn't re-proposed.** Guarding the *region's* reachable rock did not bite: bands run 543–1992 cells so nearly every face clears any input threshold, and a 400-cell region still produced 39-cell warrens when the band was too thin for pockets to place. **The bar belongs on what actually got BUILT**, not on what was available: `minSite` (knob, default 70 floor cells) plus a hard floor of 2 rooms, checked after the carve.

**FINAL CENSUS, 400 zones, defaults:**

| | share of all zones | share of warrens | floor cells | rooms | loops |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| *no warren* | **7.8%** (31) | — | — | — | — |
| working | 38.3% | 41.5% | 108 (min 70) | 3.1 | 0.6 |
| camp | 35.0% | 37.9% | 173 (min 72) | 4.3 | 1.1 |
| flourishing | 19.0% | 20.6% | 250 (min 78) | 6.2 | 1.8 |

Zero splits, zero canyon breaches, 2 doorless rooms in 369 sites (0.5%). Smallest site that exists anywhere: 70 floor cells, 2 rooms — the bar, exactly.

**Open at the sitting:** whether 7.8% empty zones is the right spawn rate (it is a knob, and it is a design call about how common warrens should be, not a generation fact) · whether the vine merchant's ruled *mid-to-large only* now maps onto camp+flourishing (55% of warrens, which is a live shop rather than the 2.7% it was under the rectangle) · loops thinned to 0.6–1.8 because a snaking band has fewer places to close a circuit, which may be inherent to the shape rather than tunable.

#### ENGINE TUNNELING PORTED + FOUR CORRECTIONS — 2026-08-18

User asked whether the decompiled builders had anything for us; they had the answer to both live problems. `ZoneBuilderSandbox.TunnelTo` and `EnsureAllVoidsConnected` decompiled the same day and ported, **replacing three pieces of hand-rolled machinery**: the straight-line door drift, the straight-line crosscut, and the BFS repair. Straight drifts could only leave the way they set out, which is why 28 rooms came out doorless the moment the region got tight; a weighted path walks around.

**What the engine's weight function actually says (ground truth, not proposal):** border 2000 · wall `80 + noise` · open floor `noise + emptyBase` (Redrock passes **200**, so threading an existing room costs 2.5 walls and hauls bore FRESH passage) · `InfluenceMapBlocker` `80 + noise` — **a cost, never a ban** · noise `|simplex(x/3, y)| * 160`. **Amplitude 160 against a wall's 80 means the noise outweighs the terrain 2:1** — the wander IS the cost field, not a knob bolted onto a walk — and the `x/3` stretches it horizontally, which is what a canyon band wants. `CardinalDirectionsOnly` + **`ShuffleDirections`**: random tie-breaking is where the organic look comes from. `EnsureAllVoidsConnected` regionalizes, then joins each region to its **nearest already-connected** region using **random cells at both ends** — closest-pair endpoints would make every connector meet at the same obvious pinch.

**Where we needed a stronger rule than the engine has.** At the engine's flat 80, tunnels leaked through our rind and cost us one mouth in two. The engine does not care how many ways into a region there are; the single-mouth ruling does. So the **canyon skin specifically is priced at 900** — finite, so a boxed-in room can still buy its way out, but dear enough that a tunnel detours most of the zone first. Ordinary rock beyond the region keeps the engine's 80.

**CORRECTION 1 — the slack trimmer was SEALING THE MOUTH in 4.7% of warrens.** The entrance is a dead-end leaf, so removing it disconnects nothing and the connectivity guard waved it through: a warren you cannot enter, passing every test. Exempting the whole artery from trimming put slack back along the entire haul road, so the exemption is exactly one cell — the mouth.

**CORRECTION 2 — the missing cost term, and the biggest single finding of the session.** Loops read 0.7 per site and the crosscuts looked broken. Instrumenting the MIDDLE of the pipeline instead of its ends showed what was actually happening on a flourishing site: **109 loops before trimming, 81 cells demolished by the trimmer, 0.68 loops left.** The carve was braiding into a sponge and the trimmer was tearing a third of it back out; surviving circuits were whatever happened to be left standing. **`emptyBase` prices going THROUGH existing floor; nothing priced running BESIDE it.** One term — a cost per side touching existing floor (`hugCost`, 260) — and:

| flourishing site | before | after |
|---|---|---|
| loops before trimming | 109 | 16.0 |
| cells trimmed | 81.2 | 6.5 |
| **loops in the finished site** | **0.68** | **6.32** |

**The loop gradient became real for the first time: working 1.32 / camp 4.47 / flourishing 6.32** — the "navigational richness IS the growth curve" proposal finally demonstrated rather than asserted. **Lesson for the record: every headline number looked fine while a third of the carve was being demolished. Two passes were fighting and it was invisible from outside — instrument the middle, not the ends.**

**CORRECTION 3 — "every site has exactly one mouth" was true of one geometry only.** Reported confidently at 235/235; that count measured **orthogonal** adjacency. Adding the diagonal check found **82 diagonal slips into the canyon across 164 sites** — roughly half of all warrens had a second, unseen way in. Cause was the same N4/N8 blindness: the region excluded rock orthogonally touching the canyon and kept rock touching it at a corner. `onSkin` is now N8. **7 slips across 194 sites**, orthogonal count still 100%.

**CORRECTION 4 — DIAGONAL PINCHES, user flag 2026-08-18** (*"the only thing i'm not crazy about is diagonal breaks - it means that players can walk through those breaks"*). This is v1's `sealDiag` ruling re-earned by the user hitting the same fact independently, which is the sorting rule from this branch's charter behaving as predicted: **legibility rulings travel across a re-brief, taste rulings do not.** Measured first: 2.2 per site, scaling with stage (working 1.3 / camp 2.6 / flourishing 4.2), about half touching a room.

Both known fixes carry v1 receipts and neither is free — **filling** is the user's own ruling (*"if we just fill that from inside the room, then you can still walk down the hallway without even noticing it was there"*) but it is what produced the 08-16 "walls in front of doors" flag, and its thickened variant was rejected on sight; **opening** a wall cell adds connectivity, which is how v1's pinch police breached the protected shell. **USER PICKED PREVENTION 2026-08-18 ("cost term sounds good")** — same move as the hug term, price it in `tunnelCost` rather than repairing afterwards. Result: **flourishing 4.2 → 1.25, camp 2.6 → 0.97, working 1.3 → 0.26.** The guarded fill for the ~1/site remainder is drafted but NOT built, and it is now tidy work rather than surgery, which matters because the walls-in-front-of-doors complaint scales with how much filling happens.

#### RETRY PER ZONE — USER CONCEPT 2026-08-18, BUILT AND MEASURED

> "what if we implemented a like, 'try again' system for warrens that fail to generate?"

**Measured before building.** Of 88 failures in 400 zones: **73 were "too little got built"** and **50 of those landed within 20 cells of the 70-cell bar**, median 56. Two of the near-misses had plenty of floor and failed on the 2-room minimum instead. That is not a zone that cannot host a warren — it is an attempt that picked a poor face. **v1 already ruled this exact shape for camps** (2026-08-07: each mouth a complete attempt with grid-snapshot rollback, next mouth tried, up to 6, best home-only pocket kept as a waystop).

**Two guards, both deliberate.** The canyon is regenerated **identically** every attempt — same seed, `genCanyon` runs first, only the face rotation differs — so we retry the SITE and never reroll the ZONE; otherwise this is rolling dice until the terrain cooperates. And it must still be able to fail, or the empty canyons that make a rumour honest disappear. **Takes the FIRST attempt that clears the bar, not the best of N** — best-of-N would push every warren toward the ceiling of what its band can do and flatten exactly the variance the stage axis lives on.

| tries | warrens / 300 zones | no warren | won on attempt | avg floor | ms/zone |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | 234 | 22.0% | all first | 165 | 3.0 |
| 2 | 269 | 10.3% | 234 / 35 | 159 | 2.9 |
| **4 (default)** | **283** | **5.7%** | 234 / 35 / 10 / 4 | 158 | 3.3 |
| 6 | 285 | 5.0% | +2 | 157 | 3.4 |

**Knee is at 3-4 tries**; past that it is nearly free of gains. What retries recover is the *marginal* sites, so the stage mix flattens slightly toward the bottom (flourishing 18% → 15%, working 48% → 52%) and average floor drops 165 → 158 — retrying makes warrens slightly smaller on average, because you are rescuing the ones that barely made it. Residual 5.7% is close to the genuine floor: "no face opens onto enough rock" alone accounted for ~3.75% before retries existed.

**Still open and still the user's:** the empty-zone rate is a spawn-density call (how common should warrens be), not a generation fact · the guarded diagonal fill for the ~1/site remainder · whether the vine merchant's ruled *mid-to-large only* maps onto camp+flourishing (~48% of warrens at 4 tries).

#### SPAWN RATE — USER RULED 2026-08-18: 25 WARRENS PER WORLD (+ a Claude correction that took three passes)

> "i think 25 warrens makes sense as a good amount to aim for"

**How base game actually sites its canyon landmarks — `JoppaWorldBuilder.BuildForts`, decompiled 2026-08-18.** Not a per-zone roll anywhere. Three calls to `AddMutableEncounterToTerrain("DesertCanyon", 25, …)`, each popping 25 mutable canyon **zones** (240×75 grid, level 10), stamping a zone builder on each and removing them from the mutable pool so nothing else claims them:

| site | builder | count per world |
|---|---|---|
| pig farm | `PigFarmMaker` | **25** |
| starapple farm | `StarappleFarmMaker` | **25** |
| snapjaw fort | `SnapjawStockadeMaker` | **25** |
| Snapjaw Hero Stopsvaalinn | (object + secret) | 1 |

**And the rumour hook is literal, not analogous.** Every one of them calls `AddSecret(zoneID, "<name>", ["…","settlement","humanoid"], "Settlements")` followed by `AddLocationFinder(zoneID, secretID)`. That `AddSecret` under the **Settlements** category IS the system that makes gossip point at a place and puts it on the player's map when someone tells them about it. **The user's 08-18 framing — warrens are rumoured "like lairs or farms in the canyons" — turns out to name a specific pair of engine calls, and it is what the four stub conversation banks should eventually feed.**

**Denominator, counted rather than estimated: `QudWorldMap.rpm` is AUTHORED, not generated.** An 80×25 parasang map, 2001 terrain cells, of which **139 are DesertCanyon** (`TerrainDesertCanyon` 71 + `…2` 13 + `…3` 1 + `…4` 54). A parasang is 3×3 zones, so **1,251 desert-canyon surface zones per world.** 25 sites in 1,251 zones = **2.0%**, which is exactly the frequency a pig farm sits at.

**CLAUDE CORRECTION, recorded because it took three wrong answers to get here.** Asked for a spawn analogue, Claude first read `DesertCanyonZoneGlobals` / `DesertCanyonPerSector` (zone *contents* — trees, fauna, a 15%-per-region snapjaw party) and reported a 1–5% "notable place" band off `StandardSurfaceGlobals`. Corrected by the user ("compare it to farm and lair spawns"), Claude then found the `Lairs` weight-5-of-61 inside `DesertCanyonCavePopulation` (8.2%, but that is the **subterranean** zone population) and `BuildLairs`'s flat `num = 125` over 18,000 surface zones, and generalised that to "a rumoured place in Qud is a once-per-144-zones thing." **The user refuted this from play — "in under 5 minutes in the desert canyons i've run into 2 ranches, 1 snapjaw fort, and 1 ruin" — and was right.** `BuildForts` and `AddMutableEncounterToTerrain` were never looked at. Lesson: the world builder places landmarks by TERRAIN-TARGETED FIXED COUNT, and that mechanism is invisible from the population tables, which is where Claude kept looking. Observed play beat three readings of the source.

**MODEL CHANGED IN THE LAB.** The spawn knob was a per-zone percentage; it is now **`warrens / world`** (default 25), with the per-zone rate derived from `CANYON_ZONES=1251` and the measured share of canyon zones whose terrain can host one (`VIABLE≈0.85`). This matches how the port will actually work — a world-build-time placement pass, not a zone roll.

**Measured over a full simulated world (1,251 canyon zones):** target 25 → **22 warrens** (1.76% of canyon zones; binomial σ≈5, so on target), of which **10 working / 10 camp / 2 flourishing**. Target 50 → 53. So a run carries roughly **a dozen payload-bearing warrens and one or two flourishing ones** — which is the "oh neat" cadence the goal statement asked for, arrived at from the base game's own numbers rather than from taste.

**Owed at port time:** register each warren through `AddSecret`/`AddLocationFinder` under the Settlements category so the gossip banks have something to point at · confirm whether warrens should share the mutable pool with farms and forts (base game removes a claimed location, so the three classes never collide) · the vine merchant's ruled *mid-to-large only* now maps onto camp+flourishing, which is ~12 of the 22.

**ENGINE RECON 2026-08-23 (CLAUDE, decompile ground truth; full report with citations in `design/worldbuilder-recon-2026-08-23.md`): the engine is cooperative, and there are two viable doors — with a fork the 08-18 model quietly assumed away.** Door A, WORLD-BUILD: `[JoppaWorldBuilderExtension]` hooks are real, mod-facing and unconditionally invoked; a hook receives the live `JoppaWorldBuilder` and can call the exact farm/fort toolkit (`AddMutableEncounterToTerrain("DesertCanyon", 25, …)` + `AddSecret`/`AddLocationFinder`) — exact count, native mutable-pool dedup, but it fires ONCE AT EMBARK, so **new worlds only**. Door B, ZONE-BUILD: `Worlds.xml` is mod-overlayable (cells merge by Name, `Inherits="DesertCanyon"` resolves against base; the trap is that a `<zone>` node replaces the whole slot, so the stock builder chain must be restated, and all four `DesertCanyon2/3/4` variants need their own override), the builder rolls per-zone on RNG seeded `"ZONESEED"+ZoneID` (deterministic per world seed, visit-order-immune), and it **reaches existing saves in unvisited zones** — zone build is lazy-on-first-visit, confirmed in `ZoneManager.GetZone`. The rumor hook survives either door: `AddSecret` is a thin wrapper over `Qud.API.JournalAPI.AddMapNote` with zero world-build dependency, callable lazily at first zone build. Door B's dedup cost has an answer: world-build POI builders are stamped into the per-ZoneID `ZoneBuilders` dictionary, so a warren builder can check for `SnapjawStockadeMaker`/farm makers and decline the zone. **THE FORK, UNRULED: exact-25 + new-worlds-only (A) vs binomial-around-25 + existing-save reach (B).** The lab's own five-world measurement (22/21/25/23/24 on target 25) is already the Door-B distribution, and the "reaches existing saves in unvisited zones" consequence recorded under the Quarriers lore direction favors B. CLAUDE LEAN: Door B. The 08-18 sentence "a world-build-time placement pass, not a zone roll" describes Door A and should not be cited as settling this. Either way the carve port is unblocked — both doors trigger the same builder class; only the trigger differs.

**FORK ANSWERED 2026-08-23 — USER LEAN, one hedge shy of ruled (verbatim: "new saves only probably? since this will be in a new mod you should start a new save for that"): DOOR A, the world-build hook.** The rationale is the two-mod split: part 2 ships as its own workshop item, and installing a new mod means starting a new save anyway — so the existing-saves argument that favored Door B applies to the BASE mod's history, not to the expansion. Supersedes the Claude Door-B lean above. Consequences carried by the pick: warrens, their secrets and their gossip targets exist only in worlds embarked with the expansion installed; in exchange the count is exactly 25, farm/fort collision-avoidance is native (mutable pool removes claimed zones), `AddSecret`/`AddLocationFinder` run at embark so rumors point at warrens from turn one, and the 08-18 lab sentence "a world-build-time placement pass, not a zone roll" turns out to describe the chosen door after all. The zone-build-time consequence under the Quarriers lore direction is SUPERSEDED IN PART (see the note there). Build shape when the port lands: `[JoppaWorldBuilderExtension]` subclass calling `AddMutableEncounterToTerrain("DesertCanyon", 25, …)`, stamping the warren builder per claimed zone — no `Worlds.xml` overlay needed, which also retires the restate-four-cells trap from the recon. Stamp RULED when the user drops the "probably"; nothing engine-side changes either way.

#### OCCUPANCY — USER RULED 2026-08-18: three whole-site states. Closes the fork open since the charter.

> "i do think wholly abandoned sites make more sense for the time being - some that are in use, some abandoned, some reclaimed adds an even mix of variety to that axis, while our other elements of reward create variety on a different axis"

**IN USE / ABANDONED / RECLAIMED**, applied to the whole site. Two supporting rulings the same sitting:

- **A rumour that does not pay off is fine — USER, citing precedent:** *"in qud you can find villages that are just abandoned, so a rumor leading to something that doesn't pay off is fine."* This amends the worth-the-trip bar: the bar is about built EXTENT, not about someone being home.
- **RECLAIMED = snapjaws, miner-weighted — USER:** *"an abandoned warren becoming a pseudo snapjaw fort could be interesting - specifically with our own special poptable of snapjaws that includes a fair bit more miners."*

**RETREAT GRADIENT — HELD, NOT REJECTED (user: "the main thing i don't wanna lock down is the retreat gradient yet, because i'm not totally sure about it").** Claude had pitched partial abandonment — a retreat line along the depth gradient, the site dying inward as it grew outward. It is a GENERALISATION of the ruled binary (line at either end), so holding it is free **on one condition, which is built:** every pocket carries its OWN `live` flag even though the whole site shares one value today. Dressing must ask per-space, not per-site. That keeps the gradient a change to one function rather than a rewrite of every table written between now and then.

**Depth evidence banked as measurement, not design** (so it is not re-derived when the gradient reopens): across 75 canyon sites, **a later pocket sits further from the mouth than the one before it 79% of the time** (50% would be no ordering at all), mean distance running **9.5 cells for the first pocket to 26.6 for the last**. The accretion order is already in the geometry — nothing records it.

**Why the reclaimed idea lands harder than it looks, and it is all pre-existing.** The **snapjaw miner is already built** (`Cleo_TerraFirma_Snapjaw Miner`, user sprite, wired 2026-08-13) with user leans from that day: *"can spawn in ANY snapjaw group"*, *"also sometimes canyon-wild"*, *"likes digging walls as much as attacking."* The same entry carries an unruled Claude float — snapjaws robbing quarried-stone sites as **the temptation loop from the other direction** — which this ruling closes by giving it somewhere to happen. Base game also sites **25 snapjaw forts in canyon terrain** (`SnapjawStockadeMaker`), so a snapjaw-held quarry is diegetically pre-seated the way the jilted lover was.

**And the digging is 100% stock — the pickaxe IS the AI** (08-13 decomp, restated because it changes the design): `Pickaxe` = `Drill` + `DiggingTool`; `DiggingTool` installs `Digging` on the wielder, which answers `ShouldAttackToReachTargetEvent` so he swings at diggable walls between him and a target; and `Drill` answers `PathAsBurrowerEvent`, so the route planner treats walls as passable for him even in ordinary travel (Worm of the Earth honeycombs zones on exactly this, with no mutation). **So a reclaimed warren does not merely contain snapjaws — it gets chewed, in play, for free.**

**CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED: generate the snapjaw extension as ANTI-CARVE.** Ragged, crooked, ignoring every law the branch enforces — no 1-wide corridors, no pillar clearance, pinches everywhere, holes punched where doors belong. The quarriers' deliberate right angles with something visibly worse gnawed off the back of it: two hands in one site, tellable apart at a glance.

**Open on this thread:** contested (snapjaws deep, quarriers still in front) vs taken outright — **Claude lean: taken first**, simpler and it is the version that makes an abandoned warren still worth the trip · bespoke table vs a miner-weighted variant of `SnapjawParty1` · share of abandoned sites that get reclaimed.

**MEASURED, 5 simulated worlds at the ruled 25/world (knobs abandoned 25% / reclaimed 20%):** 24, 21, 25, 23, 24 warrens — mean **23.4**, on target. Occupancy came out **59% in use / 27% abandoned / 14% reclaimed**. Full grid over 117 warrens:

| | in use | abandoned | reclaimed |
|---|---|---|---|
| working | 37 | 20 | 11 |
| camp | 25 | 9 | 5 |
| flourishing | 7 | 3 | **0** |

**Structural consequence worth a decision later, flagged not solved: 25 warrens cannot populate a 3x3 grid.** Five of the nine cells land under one per world, and *flourishing / reclaimed* — arguably the most dramatic combination in the concept, a whole settlement chewed through by miners — occurred **zero times in five worlds**. The bottleneck is the stage distribution, not occupancy: flourishing is only ~9% of warrens. Either the flourishing share rises, or occupancy is deliberately biased so the rare stage draws the interesting state, or the corners stay effectively unreachable and that is accepted.

#### DRESS PASS PORTED — USER GREEN LIGHT 2026-08-23 ("this is our first honest to goodness good patch that feels like the sim, so i feel like we can do dressing"); BUILT + DEPLOYED same sitting (compiles green FIRST TRY, XML strict-valid, hash-verified 2/2) — **log-confirmed pending, awaiting in-game verification**

The full sim `dressSite` into `WarrenCarver.cs`, tables/laws/weights verbatim: socket rolls on faces (one per ~30 floor) · gallery pillar/bay/floor tables on the commons articulation · the typed-dwelling grammar whole (5 room types, hearth at its own 0.42 density, light-first + signature-first, one-oven-per-site, one-chest-per-room, hookah-circle and kiln-brick orbit clusters) · plain-room human traces · the FIVE-STATE chalk yard on the mod's own directional chalkline blueprint (grades ascend from the bench; robbed yards tear gaps and drop loose stones) · warren-floor top-up (Nothing-tail cut) · corridor traces with wall-facing chalk marks · natural scatter · the LIGHTING LAW (sconces flank commons entrances, den doors stay dark, the mouth post you steer by) · TENSE (dead spaces go cold per-pocket: campfires to remains, torchposts to Unlit Torchpost, stray torches gone; reclaimed = taken, whole abandoned base + 1-2 deep-biased snapjaw fires; dead sites stop sweeping the road, 4x trash) · v1's articulation guard + threshold rule + SOLIDITY set.

**Blueprint translation, every name verified real before use** (an unverified name is a MODERROR at spawn): base Torch/Pickaxe/Garbage/Chest/Random Stone Statue/Village Monument Stele/Brazier/Campfire/Campfire Remains/Woven Basket/Floor Cushion/Canvas Folding Chair/Bed/Bedroll/Folding Cot/Table/Workbench/Wine Pitcher/Anvil/Oven/Hookah/Bench/Kline/Stool/Kiln/Torchpost/Unlit Torchpost/Random Figurine/LargeBoulder; mod FireDish/QuarriedBlock/Random Stone Figurine/Rock_Generic/Rock_Pebbles/ChalkLine (the chalk system's own blueprint - its "painter pass" comment finally cashed). **NEW XML: `Cleo_TerraFirma_QuarryStones1/2/3`** on the USER'S quarrystones sprites (found at the user's mid-build pointer, "look at quarry*stones*") - the yard's three grades with RandomTile a/b variants; DisplayName "stack of quarried stone" DRAFT, descriptions deliberately absent for the release pass.

**Deliberately deferred, flagged loud:** commons pre-etched walls (Part_Etchable markov integration, own sitting) · basket contents poptables (baskets place empty; basket_stones/basket_food are Woven Basket for now) · the cider pitcher blueprint (maps to Wine Pitcher) · catch basin (no confirmed base name; dropped from the liquid pool) · cold brazier/firedish ART (light + campfire parts stripped so they are functionally dark; sprite may still read lit).

`[verify]` next boot, fresh warren: `dress:` log line with item counts · a dwelling reads as a typed room (lit corner, walls furnished, no doorway blockers) · the yard shows chalk ring + graded stacks matching its logged state · commons sconces + the mouth post · an abandoned site is cold and trashy · no MODERROR on any dress blueprint.

**TWO DRESS RULINGS, SAME DAY (user docket) — BUILT + DEPLOYED (compiled green against clean HEAD in a throwaway worktree; the dev tree was transiently red on the PARALLEL session's in-flight Part_FissureStone.cs, not ours):**
- **Chrome braziers CUT (user: "let's remove chrome braziers with the blue flames from the roll"):** base `Brazier` is chrome + blue flame, wrong hands entirely. Out of the light pool, flip map and blueprint map. **Fire dish substituted into the light pool - CLAUDE dial, strikeable** (the mod's own light furniture; without it the pool was stray-torch-only).
- **THE YARD LEDGER (user: "a chalkboard as a potential/likely roll in the quarry yards - chalked with a date of arrival, and if they've left, a date of departure, styled in the games own vernacular. as well as potentially saying how much stone was quarried"):** 70% yard roll (CLAUDE dial) places a base `Chalkboard` carrying `Cleo_TerraFirma_ChalkWriting` text - the chalk system's own storage, so the board reads/rewrites like any chalked board. Dates ride `Calendar.GetDay/GetMonth` (the engine's marginalia vernacular, ground-truthed); arrival 45-320 days back, departure 8-40 days back on non-in-use sites (dials CLAUDE); the stone tally (60% roll) is the site's TRUE carved floor count, so the board never lies about its own dig. **LINE WORDING DRAFT ("arrived the X of Y" / "departed the X of Y" / "N blocks raised") - user voice owed at the release pass.** `[verify]` next boot: a yard board reads with dates in game vernacular; an abandoned yard shows a departure line; no negative-date weirdness on young worlds (tick wrap guarded).

**LIGHTING REBALANCE + FACE FLOOR (user in game, first dressed-site feedback: "galleries are spawning pretty overlit, while other rooms spawn relatively bare and without light") — BUILT + DEPLOYED same sitting, compiles green (dev tree clean again, the parallel session moved to its own branch at the user's direction).** Diagnosis: v2 commons STRADDLE the haul road (rule 3), so they carry far more openings than v1's galleries, and the ported law double-flanked every doorway cluster - floodlight by construction. Fix, dials CLAUDE + sim catch-up flagged: **one flank per doorway** ("a lone flank is fine" was already the law's own text), post spacing 2 -> 3, **hard cap 4 posts per commons**. Bare end: **A FACE IS NEVER STERILE** - its first socket roll skips the Nothing row (work stopped there; something is always left; echoes the plain-rooms ruling's "it just isn't sterile"). Dens stay dark - the bud-door law is ruled and untouched. `[verify]` next fresh site: commons reads lit-but-chosen (max 4 posts), every face shows at least one trace, dwellings unchanged.

**FIRE DISH VFX (user, two flags now that the dish rolls the light pool: "the firedish shouldn't flash when lit" + "the fire inside the firedish should be positioned higher") — BUILT + DEPLOYED (XML-only, strict-valid, hash-verified).** Base precedent read per the VFX law: base `Campfire` = `AnimatedMaterialFire` (the sprite strobe) + `PrefabImposter Prefab="Prefabs/Particles/CampfireFlames"` (the flame overlay) + LightSource; the dish inherited all three wholesale. Fix: `<removepart Name="AnimatedMaterialFire" />` (dressed stone does not flicker as a sprite; the light itself stays) + the imposter re-declared with **Y="5"** to lift the flames into the basin. **CALIBRATION FLAG: X/Y/Z on `BasePrefabImposter` are ints, NO base blueprint sets them, so sign and scale are unverified - Y=5 is a first guess and the eyeball may flip it to negative or rescale. That is engine-iteration 1 of the VFX law's 2.** `[verify]` next boot: lit dish holds a steady sprite; flames sit in the basin not below it; extinguish path (Campfire part -> Campfire Remains) unchanged.

#### DEEP WARRENS (multilevel) — USER CONCEPT 2026-08-23, LOGGED OPEN; decoupled from the first port

> "what about multilevel warrens? stairs can randomly generate inside them, right? what if we leaned into that and some warrens could go deep instead of wide?"

Ground truth already on record: canyon-zone stairs are real zone connections and `CanyonBuilder` paths to every Stairs connection as an interior endpoint (the stair-spur law the sim already ports). The multilevel precedent is Red Rock itself: strata 11-14 run the dedicated `Redrock` builder with stairs as forced nodes, each level carving to meet its connections (`design/worldbuilder-recon-2026-08-23.md` §5). The door-A placement extension can stamp the warren builder on zones below a claimed surface zone; the connection system keeps the shafts aligned — no new engine machinery.

Design fit (CLAUDE read, offered at the sitting): the accretion axis goes vertical — settlement on top, active face at the bottom, the two-gradients-opposite-ends law told in literal strata.

Two costs named, neither resolved: **(1)** lower cave strata build their own carve + population first, so a deep warren either lands ON TOP of living caves (breach-as-feature, house doctrine) or replaces the lower build outright (negative-priority override mode exists; Red Rock replaces via its cell blueprints). Different flavors, choose deliberately. **(2)** Sim leads, ruled — deep needs a lab pass before any port: stacked-strata view, per-level roles (no commons at depth 3), the judging bar applied to a whole shaft.

**SEQUENCING ACCEPTED 2026-08-23 (user: "yeag log it and then we can go for port!"): the first port ships wide-only; deep is a future lab pass.** CLAUDE floats banked, unruled: deep warrens rare the way flourishing is rare, so finding one stays an event · minimal shape = 2 strata · license rule = a warren goes deep only where a pre-existing stair falls inside the band (the world provided the way down).

#### FIRST PORT PASS — BUILT + DEPLOYED 2026-08-23 (branch `warren-port`, cut at the user's ask; compiles green 0 errors, deploy hash-verified 3/3) — **log-confirmed pending, awaiting in-game verification**

Three new files, the sim ported line-faithful (sim leads by ruling; every dial is the sim's DEFAULTS frozen at port time):

- **`Scripts/WarrenCarver.cs`** — the carve engine: mulberry32 RNG bit-exact (a sim seed and a port seed are comparable), band/region growth with the N8 rind, seam-shell artery with jogs and the mouth run, box-layout pockets with the commons guarantee (3000-try seat, front-40% lean), pillars/spurs/partitions per the ten rules, engine-idiom tunneling (noise 160 over wall 80, hug 260, pinch 400, emptyBase 200), crosscuts, infill, aquifers + veins + breached headings (Redrock water idiom, `DeepBrackishPool`), repair + trimSlack + navigational-loop measure, worth-the-trip licensing + stage demotion, the FULL registration contract (roles, depth BFS, yard rect + state), and the moss port (K/g colors, 35% graded, guaranteed tell) applied as render recolors on real walls. **The real zone replaces genCanyon:** host/water/tree masks read from the built zone; non-natural walls (house classifier `IsNaturalStone`, the harmonized stone call) are marked untouchable.
- **`Scripts/WarrenMaker.cs`** — `XRL.World.ZoneBuilders.Cleo_TerraFirma_WarrenMaker`, stamped per zone at priority 6000 (SnapjawStockadeMaker's slot). Seed = FNV-1a of the ZoneID, so rebuilds reproduce. **Always returns true:** a refusing band yields no warren and logs, per the rumor-that-does-not-pay-off ruling. Plus dev wish **`tfwarrenv2`** (random seed per call, current zone; rides the VERIFY_WATCH strip).
- **`Scripts/WarrenWorldBuilder.cs`** — door A: `[JoppaWorldBuilderExtension]`, `OnAfterMutableInit` (fires before BuildForts, same mutable pool, collision-free by construction), `AddMutableEncounterToTerrain("DesertCanyon", 25, …)` + `AddSecret`/`AddLocationFinder` under Settlements + `friendlySettlements` (CLAUDE call; forts use enemy) + `SetMutable 0`. Overland glyph Q/y (CLAUDE dial). **Secret name "a quarry warren" is DRAFT** — player-facing, rides the release voice pass.

**Deliberately NOT in this pass, so the gaps stay loud:** blockMode (sim's non-default A/B lane) · dressSite + the v1 tables (registration logs roles so dress lands on the contract later) · reclaimed snapjaw population (fork still open: bespoke table vs miner-weighted `SnapjawParty1` variant) · deep warrens (own entry above) · any XML (zero blueprints touched; moss is render-recolor on existing walls).

Still open: moss/pool eyeball (the site has 35 pool cells + 2 mossy walls somewhere in it), river-zone carve, rebuild-loop watch over more zones.**

**REFUSAL RATE, live tally after the fix (this world, small n): 2 carves / 5 refusals across 7 warren zones (~70%), including one refusal in a zone with 1099 rock cells.** That last one points past honest geology at a PORT-vs-REAL-TERRAIN gap: the rind law excludes region cells N8-adjacent to ANY host cell, and real zones scatter isolated open bubbles through the rock mass (the sim's canyon host has none) - every interior bubble poisons its 3x3 ring, shredding big bands under the 400-cell floor. **CLAUDE PROPOSAL, UNRULED (round 4): the rind should stand only against EDGE-CONNECTED host (the canyon network the mouth law protects); isolated interior bubbles become carvable-through pre-carved air (they cannot be a mouth to anywhere - round 3 already bars them as mouth targets). Expected effect: refusal rate falls toward sim levels without touching carve grammar. SIM CATCH-UP flagged (sim-first ruling): add scattered interior clearings to the lab's canyon host and re-verify the rind there - the port found a terrain class the host model lacks.**

**PROPOSAL UPGRADED SAME DAY (user posted a full refused zone: "i wanted to post this quarry warren that refused so you could see a zone from the game"; screenshot on file).** The zone is an ARCHIPELAGO - mesas and rock snakes, all coastline, open floor threading everywhere. Two mechanisms, both port-vs-real-terrain, bubbles now the lesser one: **(1)** the rind excludes every rock cell within one step of open floor, which in 4-6-thick masses is MOST of the mass (the sim's host had fat interiors; real rock is shore-heavy); **(2)** at pinch points 1-2 cells thick the rind leaves zero passable core, so region growth cannot reach the far lobe at all - the sim CONFLATES "not carvable" with "not traversable." Round-4 shape, three parts, all port-side: interior bubbles carvable-through · rind stands only against edge-connected host · rind cells become PASS-THROUGH-BUT-UNCLAIMABLE for region growth (the flood crosses a pinch; the carve still never opens the shell, so the one-mouth law holds untouched). Each part carries a sim catch-up item. **RULED + BUILT + DEPLOYED SAME DAY (user: "i could see it being a valid quarry spot, we just have to get smarter about how we're placing them"):** archipelago zones are valid quarry country; the three-part mechanism landed in `GrowRegion` (rind measured against edge-connected host only · isolated bubbles pass-through · rind cells pass-through-but-unclaimable, so the flood crosses pinches while the carve never opens the shell and the one-mouth law holds by construction). Compiles green, hash-verified deploy. `[verify]` next boot: refusal rate over fresh warren zones drops materially (was 5 of 7); an archipelago-class zone carves; mouth forensics stay green; no extra openings into the canyon by eye.

**ROUND 6 — THE REBUILD-LOOP DIALOG (user screenshot: "This zone isn't building properly. Do you want to force it to stop and build immediately?" on a warren zone - the worst case, called in the port-pass verify list). DIAGNOSED + FIXED + DEPLOYED same session.** Root cause was a JS-parity bug armed by round 5: the waypoint sampler computes `hi = -1` on a tiny mouth-lobe, and the sim SURVIVED the negative index because JS `cs[-1]` is `undefined` and its `if(c)` guard swallowed it - the guard WAS the behavior, and the port dropped it as unneeded. In C# that is an ArgumentOutOfRangeException, and because the carve is zone-seeded on purpose, the engine's rebuild replayed the identical throw forever. Two fixes: **(1)** the index guard restored (a bad waypoint is SKIPPED, JS-faithful); **(2)** THE BELT - `WarrenMaker.BuildZone` now try/catches the whole carve, logs `[WARREN] EXCEPTION` + `MetricsManager.LogException`, and returns true, so any future throw degrades to an honest no-warren instead of a loop. Compiles green, deploy hash-verified. Lesson for the port record: **every silent-index-miss the sim shrugs off is a hard throw here; parity includes the language's failure modes.** `[verify]` the crashed parasang was never cached, so next boot it builds fresh with the guard - visit it: clean carve or clean refusal, no dialog; grep for `[WARREN] EXCEPTION` staying absent across further zones.
