With the constraint lifted, that choice rests on nothing but flavour, and whether hewers should carry pickaxes is live again. **Species TAG deliberately left inherited** — building the body does not claim the species string, and what these people ARE stays the user's lore call. Display names "mole hewer / mole mason / mole outrider" are CLAUDE DRAFT, existing only so the two bodies are tellable apart in a log; collapse them to the bare profession on a word. *(Original float, preserved:)*  the stonecarvers themselves partly mole *people* — Barathrumite-idiom mixed faction, "though not uniformly." Mole variants carry BurrowingClaws naturals; human variants keep pickaxes (camps still littered with them). Professions remain the face — species is a second axis under the ruled six-role roster (snapjaw variant-blueprint idiom, cheap). **~~RESHAPED same session — species is FIXED PER PROFESSION, not rolled per individual: each of the six roles commits to one species, one sprite~~ — **STRUCK 2026-08-16 (user: "well the tracker is wrong on this point").** What the user actually said was **"I think we could live with one sprite for each stonecarver"**, a remark about SPRITE BUDGET. It got written up as a design ruling about species assignment, and a four-item consequence list was built on top of the gloss rather than on the quote. Textbook case of the provenance discipline this tracker exists for, caught by the user nine days later. **The quote stands; the reading does not, and what it actually meant is the user's to say.** **Knock-on: the 2026-08-15 "both bodies, all roles" ruling is NOT in conflict with anything** — Claude had opened the mole conversation by apologising for building against a ruling that turns out never to have existed. The struck consequence list, preserved only so nobody rebuilds from it: sprite debt stays at six; look-descriptions single-body; pack tableau keeps six-silhouette legibility; the mixed faction displays in every pack by construction. NEW OPEN (user's, concepts theirs): ~~**which roles are mole-folk**~~ — considerations floated (Claude, unruled): claws pull toward digging trades (hewer; drover has burrower-tends-burrower logic); provisioner-as-human has an outsider-facing-face argument; overseer species = loudest choice (water-ritual face); mason species reads as theology. Claude proposals riding on it, unruled: **hands are cultural, not anatomical** — claw or pick, a mason cuts square (preserves the two-voice carve legibility; the claws' showcase = the soft round spaces: divots, satellites); PathAsBurrower=false on camp residents so claws don't eat the map. **QUESTION COLLAPSED 2026-08-11 (user, sprite sitting): "the 'which job is a mole vs human' collapses with the color distinction — since we have both tiles, we can just do random tile parts on them, and the detail color already communicates the role." Species is ROLLED PER INDIVIDUAL again (un-does the fixed-per-profession reshape now that its six-sprite premise is gone); role stays on DetailColor.** Recon (Claude, same exchange, verified): stock `RandomTile` BUILDER is exactly this — base farmers roll among six tiles (base Creatures.xml:880), so `<builder Name="RandomTile" Tiles="human,mole" />` per role blueprint, zero code. Consequences threaded: the 08-07 "mixed faction displays by construction" guarantee reverts to STATISTICAL (an all-human pack can roll; per-role tile weighting is the dial if it ever grates) · look-descriptions must go BODY-AGNOSTIC (text-pass consequence — role descs can't name the body; the writing-sittings sheet inherits this constraint) · ~~open dial: cosmetic-only vs anatomy-follows-tile~~ **SETTLED 2026-08-11, CONVERGED LEAN (user: "i lean more towards only mole tile blueprints getting burrowing claws personally" + bank OK'd "that sounds fine yeag"; Claude concurring on the merits): ANATOMICAL — mole-tile spawns carry `BurrowingClaws`.** Grounds on record: qud bodies are load-bearing (examine must corroborate the sprite — claw naturals in the weapon list; cosmetic-only would be the mod's one painted-on body) · mopango precedent (base game's only civilized burrowers are anatomically clawed on all four blueprints — species-is-anatomical is how qud writes burrowing peoples). **Implementation correction (Claude, same exchange): `RandomTile` builder can't attach mutations, so the randomness moves to the VARIANT-BLUEPRINT idiom** — each role gets a thin mole child (inherit role base, override tile, add claws, ~6 lines), pack tables roll between variants (snapjaw pattern, zero C#); the builder shape from the collapse note is superseded. Riders unchanged: `PathAsBurrower=false` on camp residents (claws fight as melee naturals — the NPCs-use-their-mutations ruling is satisfied without map-eating) · body-agnostic descs (species still rolls, now at table level). Texture note banked: mole carvers are always-armed (naturals never drop), humans leave pickaxes as loot — the species die differently.
  - **Mopango symmetry (lore note, user insight 2026-08-07 — mopango are PANGOLINS, no mole collision):** mopango = cave-faring pilgrim folk with Light Manipulation who naturally light up wherever they go; the stonecarvers are cave-faring pilgrims whose passage leaves *shape* — carving, sockets, cairns; the beast's trail (bore-mouth + pebble dung) joins the same ladder. Two cultures marking the dark, each in its own medium. The escalation ladder (cairn → musing spot → camp) is the mechanism that delivers this read.
- **Tile-condensing consideration (user 2026-08-11, CONSIDERING — not ruled):** "just using one or two tiles and condensing the faction. and just having things go off color instead of unique sprite, for the time being. that way we can be sure every NPC we're adding carries their own weight." If ruled, it supersedes-for-now the sprite consequences of the 2026-08-07 species reshape ("one sprite for each stonecarver" — six-sprite debt) and re-points the asset-debt tracker's six TO DRAW rows at one/two bases + color variants; "for the time being" = the six bespoke sprites stay the eventual bar, this is the interim. Note the tension to resolve at ruling: the six-silhouette pack-tableau legibility argument (profession-first ruling) would lean on color alone.
  - **COLOR MAP BANKED 2026-08-11 (compression jam, second sitting — USER DIRECTION, floated not ruled; "here's what i'm thinking" / "we Could have"):** **overseer = pink, legendary, same blueprint** (user; rides base language — and VERIFIED ENGINE-DEFAULT: decomp `HeroMaker.cs:33-34` resolves `HeroColorString`/`HeroTileColor` to `&M` unless overridden, `HeroIconColor` stamps the map icon `&M` — base carver blueprint + hero builder gets the pink for FREE, zero bespoke art) · **outrider = warden blue** (user; spot-checked: warden of the Sanctum renders `&B`; mechanical rhyme noted — EB's whorl kit is protective, the warden promise gets kept in play) · **hewer = rust-red DetailColor, mason = grey-or-brown DetailColor** (user's pair, replacing Claude's earlier shared-look lean) — **mason = grey — RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "mason grey is fine"), promoted from CLAUDE LEAN; the product-rhyme argument that carried it:** each carver wears the color of the thing they make — hewer rust ↔ fissure fabric carves sandstone red/yellow (the carve-reveal bug's own palette), mason grey ↔ statues are grey in Qud's render grammar (RandomStatue) — so the role color doubles as a forecast of what the caster adds to the room, learnable from either direction. ~~Detail-color-only note: body/main color stays the shared base, so a grey-DETAIL mason does NOT hit the grey-creature-tile-means-statue register~~ — **THE FALSIFICATION IS ITSELF WITHDRAWN, same day. The note below almost certainly stood all along.** Claude claimed this reasoning was dead on the strength of a pixel count plus an ASSUMPTION about which channel those pixels map to. The user's eyes then settled it ("colors are recoloring the wrong bits, flip them on everything but legendary"): the role color was landing on the minority, i.e. **ColorString paints the majority and DetailColor is the accent, exactly as the original note assumed**. The count was a measurement; the mapping was a guess presented next to it, and the pairing made the guess look measured. **Flag on the consequence: the user's "keep mason grey, accept the overlap" ruling was taken on this withdrawn evidence.** The outcome likely survives anyway (mason is grey on both channels either way, so it reads grey regardless), but the ruling deserves a second look now that the argument under it is gone. Retained below for the record: That note assumed DetailColor is a minority accent. It is not on this sprite: `stonecarver.png` is **66 black pixels to 28 white**, and Qud's two-color convention draws the BLACK channel in DetailColor. **DetailColor is ~70% of the body**, so the role color IS the creature's read and the shared base is the trim, exactly inverted from the note. Consequences, both real: (1) **a grey-DETAIL mason WILL read as a grey creature**, so the statue-pun collision the note believed it had dodged is live and the stonefrog's exclusive rights are NOT safe — reopen at the roster build (keep grey and accept the overlap, or move the mason). (2) The upside, unlooked for: role legibility is far stronger than planned, since a blue outrider and a rust hewer differ across most of their pixels rather than in trim. The other two sprites split differently (`stonerat.png` 44/48 near even, `rockhound.png` 74/21), so this is per-sprite and worth measuring before assigning any future color. **BASE COLORS RULED FINE AS-SHIPPED, same sitting (user: "these are fine for base colors"): grey `&y` body, dark grey `K` detail STAYS.** Claude misread the report — the user's actual point was that the LEGENDARIES were rendering in base colors with only a pink NAME, when the ruled overseer pink should be on the body: "i expected the legendaries to be pink and dark grey." An interim brown/white base pass lived and died in between and was reverted at the user's instruction. **Fixed properly:** `StonecarverEpithet.MakeLegendary` now paints `Render.ColorString`/`TileColor` `&M` and leaves DetailColor alone, so a legendary is pink over the blueprint's dark grey. It has to be painted by hand because the builder deliberately skips `HeroMaker.MakeHero` (which would roll its own honorific/epithet/title over our byname) — and the free hero tile color rode along with the machinery that got skipped. **Channel note here is SUPERSEDED by the flip (see above): with ColorString the majority, a pink-MAIN legendary reads as a genuinely pink creature, which is what the overseer wanted all along.** **Legendaries were deliberately NOT flipped** (user: "flip them on everything but legendary") — their pink stays where the base-game hero convention puts it, and after the flip that is also where it renders best. **~~CLAUDE FLOAT, unruled: drover = brown~~ — OUT OF THIS PASS, RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "drover we don't need to include in this pass"):** the drover is not in the roster build, so its color question does not arise; the float keeps its reasoning on file for whenever the role returns (bounded by the centipede design anyway). (freed if mason takes grey; brown = Qud's beast/caravan tone via dromads) — would complete a one-tile-plus-palette-row faction: pink leader / blue guard / rust breaker / grey shaper / brown tender. Shape implication ~~if the whole map rules: ONE base tile + DetailColor per role (the "one or two tiles" fork resolves to one)~~ **SUPERSEDED same day by the sprite sitting: TWO bodies (human = calling tile reused, mole = user sprite), species ROLLED PER INDIVIDUAL via stock `RandomTile` builder, DetailColor carries the role — tile answers "who," color answers "what they do" (see the species-axis entry for the collapse + consequences)**; the six bespoke sprites remain the eventual bar; keep role colors STABLE across that upgrade so players aren't re-taught. **Provisioner formal cut still rides this open ruling** (weight-test framing from the jam, Claude: weight = a verb the player can see — cast/tend/lead/sell; the provisioner lost his verb to the vine, his carried-goods function migrates to the beast's cargo). **SPRITES DONE (user, same day, after the color-map bank): "the two sprites for them are done and can be thrown into the game whenever"** — the two-base shape has ART IN HAND; asset-debt-tracker §1 updated (six NEW rows superseded as operative debt). **Mapping CONFIRMED same exchange: the two bodies are the species split — human base = the player Stonecarver calling tile REUSED (user: "we'll just use the player icon that's already made!"), mole-folk base = user sprite — LANDED same evening as `Textures/stonerat.png` (ratlike drift, user-blessed; zero text consequence per the body-agnostic desc ruling). Net new art owed: ZERO.** Gear direction rode in with it (user): calling-face implies calling-kit — NPC carver inventories seed from the Stonecarver chargen gear, **kept LIGHT for now (user's words)**; Claude note: chisel+mallet in the kit means pack carvers pass the etch tool gate by construction. **User's remaining-path read, same exchange: sprites droppable anytime → "then it's just text passes, and iterating on the warren until they're about ready to ship" — a major break point on the road to 1.0** (user's own correction: mutations = the 0.1 beta, quarriers ride to 1.0 per the standing 2026-07-26 scope ruling — no scope change). **Gorgon flag riding the compression (Claude, unruled): two Gorgon verifies (dead-gorgon trap, stunned-caster) have waited since 2026-07-24 on "an NPC gorgon existing" — if the compressed roster never places Gorgon and no wild creature takes it, that debt has no landing strip; cheapest home = the overseer's chance-for-3 slot (Gorgon NPC AI already paid per the 2026-08-06 advertisement pass), else re-tag the verifies unreachable.**

  - **Profession-first structure — RULED 2026-08-15 (user: "profession first is fine, it's a qud idiom"; the user reached the idiom argument independently, which was the argument), promoted from Claude lean 2026-07-22 — user had floated provisioner/mason as example roles:** distinguish types by profession noun — the verified basegame idiom for civilized packs (Goatfolk Hornblower/Sower/Yurtwarden/Shaman vs snapjaws' combat nouns; Yurtwarden = identity-is-tending-the-camp precedent). Three layers: profession (face — name/gear/description/camp presence), mutation (engine each profession works with), and `Role` — **NOT an "AI layer": that description was wrong and is corrected here.** **ROLE RECON, decomp ground truth 2026-08-15 (user: "we should investigate creature AI roles more thoroughly? just in case"). Nothing under `XRL.World.Parts` or `XRL.World.AI` reads the tag at all.** It feeds four unrelated systems:
    1. **Difficulty / con rating** (`DifficultyEvaluation.GetRoleLevelOffset`): Minion −5 · Skirmisher −3 · Leader +3 · Summoner +4 · Hero +5 · **everything else 0**. Brute, Artillery, Tank, Controller and Lurker are cosmetic on this path.
    2. **Dynamic-table spawn weight** (`GameObjectFactory.RoleWeightMultipliers`): Common 4.0 · Minion 4.0 · Skirmisher 1.0 · Artillery/Uncommon/Brute/Tank 0.25 · Specialist/Leader/Hero 0.1 · Rare/Epic 0.01. Controller, Lurker, NPC, Breeder and Summoner are absent from the table entirely. **Only bites DYNAMIC tables** — an explicit population entry like our pack table is unaffected.
    3. **XP value** (the `*XP` stat): Minion ×20 · Leader ×100 · Hero ×200 · default ×50, per half level.
    4. **A Minion-only stat penalty**: `Role == "Minion"` decrements `Boost` on ALL SIX attributes. Minion also excludes a blueprint from a `ZoneManager` selection and gets its level QUARTERED in an `IComponent` threat check.
    **Consequence for our roster, caught the same sitting: `mason = Minion` (a Claude draft from that morning) was doing real damage** — statted down across the board, reading five levels weaker than it is, paying 20x XP, and quartered in threat maths, for a profession that is a peer and not a mook. **Changed to `Summoner`, which is mechanically honest AND literally true** (Stoneshaper summons statue companions) and rates +4, so the con warning matches what the fight actually becomes. hewer `Brute` and outrider `Artillery` stand: both are 0-offset flavor, and their 0.25 dynamic weight never applies to explicit tables. Overseer takes `Hero` from the legendary builder (+5, ×200 XP), which is correct. **If real behavioural difference between professions is ever wanted, `Role` is the wrong lever entirely** — that lives in Brain properties and AI goal parts (the `AIThrowAndScoot` the outrider already inherits is the example on file). Why: profession identity holds even where combat AI isn't built yet (mason reads as mason without casting); "provisioner" maps directly onto `GenericInventoryRestocker` (dromad-trader machinery — makes the pack itself the trading stop, cashing in the reception ruling's "at least they can trade"); work-division makes the pack tableau legible at a glance. Roster ruling = name the professions + which mutation each carries.
  - **Per-type cost map (recon):** *Fissure* — free, NPC-complete since the rebuild (AI handler in). *EB* — **RULED 2026-07-22: real EB mutation on the thrower type** (Boulderer-mimic fork closed); cost = AI advertisement handler + NPC toggle handling (moderate). *Stoneshaper* — ~~free if identity-only~~ **identity-only option closed by the NPCs-use-their-mutations ruling**; **cost re-estimated DOWN after full code read (2026-07-22): ~40-60 lines, nothing novel.** The complexity was all player-UI (two-stage PickDestinationCell dialogue + goto retry loops); NPC side collapses: muse := `E.Target` (AI commands carry the combat target; statue copies attacker's Str/Agi/Tou — self-balancing), wall := nearest-valid-cell scan reusing the existing validation gauntlet factored into a predicate (~15 lines; canyon habitat is wall-rich by definition), AI handler advertises only when a valid wall is in radius. Heavy machinery (MakeStatueOfCreatureFromWall/ApplyStatueBuffs/DeanimateStatue) already static + actor-agnostic; allegiance via generic `BecomeCompanionOf`; swap prompt + Lovesick popup already IsPlayer-gated. Needs a small refactor extracting the inline commit path (creation→cooldown bookkeeping) for both branches to share. Side-findings: player wall-pick is ~~unbounded~~ **sight-gated (verified 2026-07-22 vs decompiled `IComponent.PickDestinationCell`: `Range = 9999` but `VisLevel = AllowVis.OnlyVisible` by default, mod doesn't override)** — standard "range: sight" semantics, nothing to fix; Lv10+ NPC would auto-swap statues (prompt is player-only) — moot if pack stonecarvers stay below Lv10 (roster-spec number). *Random* — `RandomMutations` part precedented (esper hero rolls 4-5 mentals); restricting the pool to our seven needs a decompile check, else bespoke ~20-line part; **randomness doesn't dodge AI-readiness** — non-ready rolls yield NPCs that never cast. *Structure note:* types-by-gear/stats IS the snapjaw idiom (shared base + variant blueprints); the two ideas compose.

### Lore session 2026-07-24 (chat leanings — nothing ruled unless marked)
- **Stonecarvers are MUTANTS, not True Kin — user, firm. NO CALLING WRINKLE (corrected 2026-07-24):** callings ARE the mutant chargen category (`Mutated Human Subtypes="Callings"` / `True Kin Subtypes="Castes"`, Genotypes.xml) — the shipped Stonecarver calling was mutant-side all along. The "True Kin chargen" claim was an error in the old merge note (line ~35, now fixed) that Claude propagated despite a standing memory saying the opposite. Nothing to reconcile; the floated apprentice/rename options are void.
- **Homeland: no hard canon assignment — user ("touchy," not strictly opposed).** Claude pitch, awaiting ruling: the carvers never name their home (diaspora identity — ask a carver where home is and they tap the nearest wall). Dial open: full mystery vs speculation-allowed (unreliable in-world scholars may guess — Sunderlies among the guesses, framed as hearsay per the *Across Moghra'yi* idiom) vs invented homeland.
- **Creed draft (pitched, user "mostly fine"):** stone remembers; carving is waking, not making; Qud's stone is *awake* (the pilgrimage reason); the mutations are the stone answering. Spent-stone rites unify `DullAfter` + the "You return the stone to the earth" reabsorb text as one belief.
- **Geode lottery — user likes (had considered it independently).** Sealed geodes as mystery grab-bags in Stilt-merchant stock; crack → population table roll (mundane rock / brine / gemstone / rare **viewing stone** jackpot).
- **Camp — leaning commit, built manually.** User: base procgen too flimsy to hook; consistent with the ruled "no procedural-village surface." Tech: static chunk skeleton + population tables + placement hints (SixDayTents idiom) — fixed bones, rolled furnishings. Strange-mood tableau (one mason stood "listening" at a raw block, camp working around them) floated as the set-piece.
  - [x] `[design]` **Painter architecture RULED 2026-07-24 (user: "genius — solves a problem I've had since their inception"):** recursive recipe primitive. A recipe = footprint requirement + fixed placements + weighted blanks; a blank resolves to an object, a table roll, or another recipe (nesting dolls). Hard depth cap 3 (camp → room → cluster). Fit rule: try smaller variant, else skip — NO backtracking/solver/adjacency logic ever (that's the flimsy procgen being avoided). Carve-vs-drape is a per-recipe attribute, not two systems. Every recipe logs placed/shrunk/dropped in the TF-VERIFY idiom. Payoff: recipes reuse across contexts (grave = lone corridor cairn AND camp blank — recombination reads as unauthored narrative); strange-mood mason = rare roll in one workshop blank, not a special skeleton. Two independent dials: spawn weight (camp rarity across canyons) vs recipe tables (variety within a camp). Middle ground achieved: authored texture at procedural density — neither one overdesigned static zone nor canyon-spam. Same primitive later serves the Unfinished One, lithovore restaurant, Stilt dressing.

Context: Claude asserted a wrong basic fact (calling=True Kin) the same session it pitched writing an anchor book; the user's standing all-text-is-draft rule exists for exactly this. Anchor book stays unwritten unless the user asks for a raw-material draft or writes it themselves.

Scale by adding nodes, never by inflating rooms — every chamber stays den-sized (coziness preserved structurally; first slice at 4/3 radii read as a hall, confirmed the failure mode). Comb-cave resonance via pure architecture, no homeland named. Painter primitive set: **blob** (node), **satellite** (1-cell alcove, needs back-wall puncture guard), **spine** (1-wide tunnel, straight or one L-bend, NO pathfinding — hand-dug reads as the obvious line). Divot tunnel = spine + satellites, composition not new code. Sleeping: small camp = 2–3 perimeter alcoves (tfpaint v3, next build, zero assets — bedroll + Random Copper Figurine effects roll echoes the calling's own maquette); large camp = divot hall/tunnels, size-gated by recipe table.

Phase 1 carves greedily (primitives accumulate where wall supports them, "looking right" is the only criterion — iterate bare geometry via tf-wishes with zero dressing noise); output is an inventory of spaces with metadata (shape class, size, dead-end?, mouth-distance). Fiction: the comb = the dense deep residential quarter (the warren ruling's honeycomb resonance, finally structural). Maze budget 70% of lattice (sparse, not full); 1-3 rooms per comb; posts protected from the shard police via keep-set; attach = perimeter probe ≤4 cells to open floor, unattached combs revert via snapshot. Rolled at 35% on full camps + standalone Comb button. **UPGRADED same session (user: "not seeing that diagram reflected — very few branching paths with rooms"):** roll 35%→60%, lattice lengthened along-seam (4-6), maze budget 0.7→0.85, **branch-stub pass** (40% per lattice cell: dead-end side-pokes — backtracker-with-budget alone yields a snake, stubs are the diagram's branching AND future niches), rooms 2-3, placement spot-list covers both camp flanks with a small-comb fallback ladder. 11/30 zones place a comb (was 1/30 after the size-up broke the old offsets).
    - **MEANDER REWRITE same session (user's second reference drawing + clarification: "don't want them static — line up the procgen with something like these shapes"; the drawings are a CALIBRATION CORPUS, not stamps — user will draw more as style targets):** the backtracker walk replaced with a hand-styled meander — **momentum** (straight preferred over turning), **handedness** (curl one way, 15%/step flip — the drawings' hooks and spirals), and **LOOP CLOSURES** (≤3 per comb, 30% when the line meets its own earlier run: carve through and reconnect, enclosing a wall island — the drawings' signature feature, structurally impossible for a backtracker, which only makes trees). Rooms now anchor to points ALONG the walk (swellings on the line, not free-floating blocks); stubs eased 40%→25%. Naming of the reference pieces OPEN (slate offered: chalk [Claude lean] / tracery / meander / glyph [lore-expensive]); user hasn't picked. Verified: loops fire (~1 per comb); comb placement rate dipped 11→6 per 30 (walk gets boxed in more than a backtracker — dial candidates: stuck-hop cap, budget, perimeter-attach probe reach) — TUNE AGAINST THE USER'S NEXT DRAWINGS, that's the workflow now.
    - **CALIBRATION ROUND 2 same session (user's third drawing, "something like this maybe?"):** three new reads tuned in — (a) momentum reweighted straight-dominant (3/2/1 straight/curl/off; drawing 3 favors long runs over hooks); (b) **stubs extend to 2-cell T-arms** 40% of the time; (c) **the WRAP move** — the drawing's top-right motif: an 8-cell ring corridor encircling a wall block (1-2 per comb when lattice ≥3x3). **CENTER CORRECTED same session (user challenged Claude's "sealed chamber" read — the dot was "just a pillar there or something, but it could be anything in the center"): the wrap center is a SLOT** — 60% the wrapped 3x3 mass stands solid (the drawn pillar), 40% it hollows into a doored chamber; the dressing phase inherits the center as a natural feature frame (a blank, in the recipe sense — the user's "could be anything" is literally the weighted-blank philosophy surfacing in carve geometry). Claude's sealed-vault reading DEMOTED to a parked float (dig-to-reach treasure — revisit only if the user ever wants it; it was never in the drawing). Lesson banked per the proposal-vs-ruling discipline: don't take sketch marks literally — drawing-2's dots were posts drawn light-on-black, drawing-3's dot was the same mark in opposite contrast. (d) **KEY FIX: maze budget 0.85→0.55** — the drawings are a LINE through mass (~55% coverage), not a filled lattice; the high budget consumed the very blocks wraps enclose and stubs poke into (0 loops/wraps/stubs at 0.85 → 10 loops/9 wraps/18 stubs across 15 combs at 0.55, 15/16 attach). Standalone Comb button dims raised to R(3,6)xR(3,4).
