    - **LIGHTING PASS v1 — TORCHPOSTS, USER RULED 2026-08-14 (Claude shape proposed after decomp recon, user: "i'm fine with your shape here"). BUILT + VERIFIED same day.** Ground truth first: `Torchpost` is a wall sconce (`MountedFurniture`, `LightSource` Radius=6, Furniture.xml:4075) and is literally the DEFAULT VILLAGE LIGHT (`Villages_LightSourceType_*Default`); the engine's placement grammar (VillageBase decomp) is strict — one light type per village, placed in PAIRS FLANKING THE TOWN SQUARE'S ENTRANCES, paths unlit, hut doors unlit. **The ruled shape steals it whole: our town square is the gallery — torchpost pairs flank each entrance from the INSIDE (glow spills out the door); one sanctioned extension, a single wall-hugging post inside the canyon mouth (pilgrim-road front door, stage-2 only). Trunks, halls, dens, back door: DARK — underground the glow is choreography, blanket light would spend it.** Build (dress-gated like all dressing): gallery entrances = open non-gallery cells 4-touching gallery floor, clustered so a wide breach lights once, bud doors excluded by their far side opening into a sealed den; flanks = the two interior cells perpendicular to the door axis (a pinched doorway takes one — honest). Glow sketch for judging at reroll speed: BFS radius 6 through open cells (stacks pass — waist-high; true wall blocks but the reached face lights: lit rock, no bleed-through), rendered as a SEPARATE OVERLAY LAYER of grid-placed divs — NOT a cell background, because the recolor-filter cells must keep transparent backgrounds (the 2026-08-12 slab lesson's second dividend; first attempt as cell bg also died on cellStyle's early returns). Recomputed per render, hand-painted posts glow too. **Verified: stage-1 30 rolls — 14/14 placed galleries lit, 70 posts / 44 entrances (1.6 per — pairs where geometry allows); stage-2 15/15 mouth posts + 15/15 tripwires clean; glow confinement checked cell-by-cell: 0 lit cells beyond r6, rock shadows real (5 in-range cells dark behind walls on the probe roll), glow spilling into the canyon at the mouth as intended; 0 console errors.** CLAUDE dials: glow tint rgba(255,150,50,.16) · flank-pair-from-inside geometry · mouth-post search depth 2. **CORRECTION same day (user field report "generation 6 totally broke"): the overlay layer as first shipped BROKE THE MAP on every lit roll — glow divs were placed as explicit GRID items, and CSS grid lays those out FIRST with auto-flow cells then SKIPPING occupied tracks: each glow div evicted one map cell, shifting the whole zone around a naked glow-disc hole. Fixed same hour (overlays now position:absolute, out of grid flow; lesson comments at the CSS rule + the render loop; re-verified on the user's exact roll — cwarren seed 6, identical part log, coherent render, 0 errors). The verification failure is the record-worthy half: the original "verified" batches checked cells BY CHILD INDEX (which stayed valid) and colors by style value — all DOM-truth, zero pixel-truth, so a fully broken layout measured as 100% clean. Standing lesson: a RENDER-layer change is only verified by pixels (screenshot or layout-position probe), not by DOM state.** **GLOW SKETCH CUT same sitting (user: "don't bother rendering the glow of the lights - we'll see it all in game"): all glow machinery deleted — the sim PLACES posts and logs them, the game's own `LightSource` renders the light. Placement rules, verification of placement, and the generation-6 lesson all stand; verified post-cut on the same seed-6 roll: 2000 cells, 0 overlays, posts placing, 0 errors.** Banked: **`Unlit Torchpost` exists in base** — ready-made prop for the abandoned-tense flip. Port notes: posts are the stock blueprint as-is; VERIFY at port whether a ground `Torch` item emits light (if yes, work bites already glow and gallery-only gets stronger); radius is the blueprint's own 6, not ours.
      - **TORCHPOST CROWDING FIX — USER SCREENSHOT FLAG + BUILT + VERIFIED 2026-08-16 ("this one seems to be placing a lot of torches all together" … "there's 4 just placed in a line near the statue"). Surfaced by the gallery dress pass the same day — first time anyone was looking hard at a dressed gallery — but the fault is in this lighting pass, not in the dress tables (the flagged roll placed zero stray torches).** **Measured before touching anything:** rolls WITHOUT a gallery place exactly 1 post every time (the mouth); rolls WITH one averaged **4.3 posts, peak 12**, flanking a mean of 2.4 entrances and peaking at 9. All the crowding is entrance flanking. Post coordinates dumped from a live roll showed the mechanism exactly: `43,13 45,13 | 46,15 48,15` — two doorways three cells apart, each correctly flanked by its own pair, the four posts abutting into the line the user saw. **Root cause:** the rule is faithful to `VillageBase`, where a town square has two or three real doors, but the DEFAULT `galleryShape` is overlap-hollow — a 3-rect union whose jagged perimeter throws off many small openings — and the doorway-cluster merge only fused cells that TOUCHED (chebyshev 1), so two gaps separated by a single wall cell stayed two doors. **Built (both CLAUDE dials):** `DOORMERGE` = 2 (fuse doorways that are NEAR, not just touching, so a one-cell pillar between gaps reads as one wide door) and `POSTSPACE` = 2 (refuse a post within 2 of one already standing). The spacing number is the gallery's own grammar rather than a new invention — its pillars already refuse to sit within 2 of each other. Flanking PAIRS are kept, since the pair is the whole point of the village steal; one flank refused with the other kept is allowed, a lone sconce beside a door still reads. **The mouth post is exempt from `POSTSPACE` on purpose** — ruled fixture, not a rolled one. Refusals now print in the light line. **Verified, 80 dressed canyon-warren rolls (21 with galleries):** posts per gallery roll **4.3 → 3.3 mean, 12 → 6 max**; entrances flanked **2.4 → 1.7 mean, 9 → 3 max**; and the number that closes the flag — **largest 8-adjacent clump of posts is 1, i.e. no two posts touch anywhere, in any roll.** Only 2 posts across all 80 rolls were refused by `POSTSPACE`, so the merge dial did nearly all the work and the spacing rule is the belt that guarantees it. Tripwires clean 80/80, 0 console errors. **OWED:** the user has not eyeballed the new density yet — 3.3 posts per gallery is a feel number, and `DOORMERGE`/`POSTSPACE` are the two knobs if it wants to go darker or brighter. The alternative shape offered and NOT taken: cap flanking to the two or three widest entrances and leave the rest dark.
    - **CHEST LOOT — DESIGN BANKED FOR PORT, USER RULED 2026-08-14 ("have it use our work dressing table (approximately, ignoring trash and things that'd be weird to be in a chest) as a population table of loot — rarely something nice like a copper nugget"; gemstone fork asked and answered same sitting). NOTHING BUILT — chest contents have no pixels for the lab to judge; this is a port-time XML artifact.** Value ground truth that settled the fork (Items.xml): Copper Nugget Commerce 10 · Rough Agate 100 **Tier 1** (the base game's own tier-1 gem — it headlines the tier-1 figurine table) · Rough Jasper 200 **Tier 3** (the mid-game currency gem). **Claude read, user's instinct confirmed on jasper: AGATE IN at true jackpot rarity, JASPER OUT** — compounded rarity (chest weight 2-3 in the work tables × ~2% agate row) makes the agate a once-in-a-dozen-warrens story, while jasper would outbid the zone. AGATE RARITY = CLAUDE DIAL, user rules final. Table draft (weights all CLAUDE dials): torches + pickaxe common body, stone maquette uncommon, copper figurine + copper nugget the rare pair, rough agate the whisper; trash EXCLUDED (ruled), stacks excluded (furniture-scale, stays on the floor), natural stones excluded unless the user wants offcut-hoards. Mechanism (base recon): `Chest` carries an empty `Inventory` part, derived chests fill via `inventoryobject` rows with dice (`ChestBronzeIngots` rolls `1d3-1`) — so `TF Warren Chest` = one blueprint + rows, zero C#; VERIFY at port whether `inventoryobject` takes an `@table` population reference or the rows go inline. FLOAT on file (not spec'd): the chalk pouch (its own open thread, promoted 2026-08-13) may want a chest row as its spawn point when that design talk lands.
    - **SOLIDITY VOCABULARY + ARTICULATION GUARD — BUILT + VERIFIED 2026-08-16 (USER: "yes, let's do that!", after the statue/sculpture dressing conversation surfaced the hole). FOUNDATION PASS, NO TABLE ROWS SHIPPED — every design question from that conversation stays open.** The bug, found while costing statues: `isOpen` read *"the cell is not literally the string `wall`"* ([warren-lab.html:962](design/warren-lab.html) pre-pass), and `dressWarren()` runs at the carve tail while the reach tripwire runs later — so every dressed cell counted as walkable to the flood. Harmless to date **only by accident**: `Cleo_TerraFirma_QuarriedBlock` inherits `Furniture`, which never sets `Solid`, so no item the tables can roll has ever blocked. The first solid row would have shipped warrens severed in game with a 142/142 reach line above them. **Ground truth pulled the same sitting (StreamingAssets + ilspycmd):** the boulder line is a SOLIDITY LADDER, not one object — `SmallBoulder`/`MediumBoulder` are `Solid="false" Takeable="true"` (items you throw), `LargeBoulder` alone flips `Solid="true"` and takes the `Wall` tag; `Rubble` (Walls.xml, NOT Furniture.xml) is `Solid="true"` with **`Occluding="false"`** plus tags `Wall`+`Flyover` and `AllowMissiles=1` — blocks walking, passes sight, missiles and flyers; `Statue`/`Random Statue`/`Village Monument` are solid AND occluding. That's a three-rung obstruction grammar the dress pass can now speak. **Built:** `SOLIDITY` table (OPT-IN — an unlisted id is passable, so today's behavior is bit-identical), `isSolidId`/`isSolid`, `isOpen` now refuses solid cells exactly like rock (which makes the existing reach tripwire solid-aware for free — guard prevents, tripwire verifies, the orphan-tripwire idiom), and `canPlaceSolid` — a LOCAL cut-vertex test (open 8-neighbours must reconnect without the candidate inside a radius-5 box; 8-connectivity matches the tripwire's own flood). Wired into both placement paths: the registry tables (refusals counted separately from Nothing so a refuse-happy table is visible) and the warren-wide natural scatter (8 tries, then the attempt is spent). Palette gained two SOLID entries for hand-painting and to give the guard something real to refuse: `largeboulder` (sprite `sw_largeboulder` already extracted) and `rubble` (**glyph-only — masks are `Tiles2/sw_rubble_1-4`, one line in `extract-qud-walls.py` whenever wanted**). **Verified in-browser over http, new console harness `tfSolidStress(id)`** (walks every warren floor cell, takes the guard's verdict, then actually places the solid and measures a reach flood against a baseline — severed = the flood loses more than the one cell just filled; grid restored per cell): **25 canyon-warren rolls, 3731 warren cells tested, 3379 accepted / 352 refused, FALSE ACCEPTS 0.** False refuses 62 (1.7% of cells) — conservative by design, a legal detour that leaves the box; harmless direction to be wrong in. Same clean result for `bg.random_stone_statue` and `largeboulder`; passing a passable id is rejected outright. Regression: 20 dressed rolls, tripwires clean 20/20, 0 console errors. CLAUDE DIALS on record: `SOLIDDETOUR` = 5, natural-scatter retry = 8. **OPEN + OWED (all unruled, from the same conversation):** whose likeness statues depict (user lean: canyon fauna + stonecarvers + invented creatures, own sitting owed — note `Cleo_TerraFirma_Stonecarver`/`Hewer`/`Mason`/`Outrider` and the mod fauna already exist as legal `RandomStatue BaseBlueprint` values); the base-game decorative list the user is sending; gravestones at abandoned sites; and whether rubble/large boulders earn table rows at all. **`[verify]` at C# port time: the ZonePainter side has the same blindness by construction — the port inherits the guard, not just the vocabulary.**
    - **STAGE 3 DRESS PASS v2 — THE GALLERY, BUILT + VERIFIED 2026-08-16 (USER: "yeag, we can push the dress pass for the gallery"). Delivers on the 08-12 ruling's queued order ("gallery/communal comes second") and gives the statue conversation its first on-screen body.** The gallery carved its articulations but registered NONE of them — `placeGallery` returned only its footprint and pillars, so the dress walk passed straight over the room. **Built:** slot and bay cell-lists are now collected during the carve and handed back on the return, then pushed into `dressSpaces` **only at the resolve point** — a gallery the network never reaches reverts to rock, and registering at carve time would have left the dress count pointing at stone. Four kinds, each a different sentence about the same room: `galleryslot` (a blind niche), `gallerybay` (a shelf), `gallerypillar` (the floor margin around a lone pillar, where a piece stands to be walked around), and `galleryfloor` — the leftover open span as ONE space, so it rolls once and a gallery gets at most a single centrepiece, never a field of them. Claimed-cell bookkeeping runs slot/bay → pillar → span, so no cell sits in two spaces. New `"center"` placement hint (sibling of `"face"`): the cell nearest the space's centroid, which is what makes the centrepiece read as deliberate. Slot cells are already depth-major from the carve, so `"face"` seats a figure at the blind end for free. **Tables v1, ALL WEIGHTS CLAUDE DIALS, DRAFT:** the room-and-pillar gallery is a mining term the carvers have made literal — blocks arrive raw and leave as sculpture — so `largeboulder` is the RAW rung here (unworked stock), a different sentence from the natural table's pebbles (geology showing through the floor). **`rubble` deliberately ABSENT: a collapsed heap reads abandoned and the ruled tense is lived-in — it belongs to the campfire-remains flip.** Every statue row is solid AND occluding, so all of them run the articulation guard built earlier the same day. **Verified in-browser over http:** 60 dressed canyon-warren rolls — 13 galleries (galleryChance 0.4 on medium+, as ruled), **37 solid objects placed (29 stone statues, 6 large boulders, 2 steles), tripwires clean 60/60, 0 console errors.** Guard refusals: **0**, and that is the finding rather than a gap — a gallery is precisely the open room where solids are safe, which is why it was the right home for them; the warren-wide `tfSolidStress` sweep the same day found 352 cut vertices in 3731 cells, so the guard is demonstrably live, it simply has nothing to refuse here. **`gallerybay` is dormant at defaults BY CONSTRUCTION** — the bay carve sits inside `galleryShape != 1` and the default silhouette is 1 (overlap-hollow), so bays only exist in the drawing-11 rect mode; exercised deliberately at `galleryShape=0` (40 rolls, 7 galleries, 26 bays registered, 15 solids placed, tripwires 40/40, knob restored to default afterwards). **OWED / UNRULED:** the weights are a first draft and want the same on-screen feel round the work tables got; likeness still parked (the sim paints a glyph — `RandomStatue BaseBlueprint` is a port-time XML attribute); communal/sleeping tables still queued behind this; and the abandoned-tense flip is where rubble, gravestones and the `Unlit Torchpost` all land together.
    - **STAGE 3 DRESS PASS v3 — COMMUNAL BLOCKS AS TYPED DWELLINGS, BUILT + VERIFIED 2026-08-17 (USER: "let's try doing communal blocks"). Closes the last registered-but-undressed space kind: `carveBlock` had been pushing `{kind:"blockroom"}` since 08-12 and the dress walk skipped it with "blockrooms wait on communal tables" — geometry done, table missing.** **GROUND TRUTH, PopulationTables.xml read today, and it reshaped the whole pass:** the engine does NOT dress a dwelling from one flat table. Every hut is a TYPED room — `Villages_BuildingContents_Dwelling_{House,Storage,Seating,Crafts,Water,Luxury,Professional,Scholarly,LiquidStorage,Child,Medical,Torture}` — and each type is a `Style="pickeach"` list of rows, so a room receives SEVERAL things. Contents are chosen by TAG INTERSECTION (`*Seating,*Furniture`), never by blueprint list, and every row carries a semantic placement hint: `InsideCorner`, `AlongInsideWall`, `Inside`, `AlongOutsideFrontWall`. Two rows are identical in EVERY type: light, 1-2 of it, in an inside corner, before anything else. **Built, stealing that shape whole:** `ROOMPOOLS` (light / storage / seating / sleep / centre / liquid / crafts / hearth / books / luxury) and `ROOMTYPES`, six weighted types — house 40, storage 20, crafts 15, seating 10, hearth 8, scholarly 7 — each a row list rolled pickeach. `dressDwelling` places per row with hints mapped to our geometry: `corner` = 2+ orthogonal walls, `wall` = 1+, `inside` = anywhere, and a hint is a PREFERENCE — no legal hinted cell falls back to any legal cell, which is what the engine does. Solid items still run the articulation guard. **Threshold guard:** `carveBlock` drops the cut line from both child rooms, so a room's own door is not in its cell list and any cell orthogonally open to outside the room IS the threshold — furniture stays off it, because a chest in a doorway reads as a mistake even when nothing is mechanically blocked. **Three divergences named in the code:** (1) this BREAKS the one-item-per-space law deliberately — that is the WORK tables' law, where a socket is one bite of evidence; a dwelling is a different sentence and the engine's own grammar says pickeach. (2) `AlongOutsideFrontWall` is dropped rather than faked — a room dug inside a rock block has no outside front wall. (3) the tag intersection became an explicit pool because the sim paints palette ids; **at PORT time the pools should go back to being TAG QUERIES, which is how the mod gets new furniture for free.** ALL POOLS AND WEIGHTS ARE CLAUDE DRAFTS. **Verified, 80 dressed canyon-warren rolls:** 12 rolls carried a block, **38 block rooms dressed — 15 house / 8 storage / 6 seating / 6 crafts / 2 scholarly / 1 hearth**, tracking the weights; 25 distinct furniture blueprints placed across the batch; tripwires clean 80/80; 0 console errors. New log line `dwellings: N block room(s) — X house, Y storage…`, and the dress line's "work spaces" relabelled to "spaces" since dwellings now count in it. **FLAGGED FOR THE SLEEPING SITTING:** the `sleep` pool (bed / bedroll / folding cot) is the FIRST sleeping furniture the sim has ever placed. The 2026-07-25 architecture ruling put sleeping in perimeter ALCOVES and divot halls — but that ruling was written for the legacy camp track, and the engine's own House table sleeps its dwellings. Reconcile before sleeping is built; it is one row to remove if the user wants block rooms bedless. **NOT YET EYEBALLED BY THE USER.**
      - **FLOOR BUDGET — USER FLAG + FIXED 2026-08-17, same sitting ("okay some rooms generate with nothing, while others generate with too much stuff (imo)", with a screenshot).** **Measured before changing anything, 45 rooms:** items per room ran 0-9, **median density 0.42 of the room's floor, p90 and max 0.75**; worst cases 9 items in a 12-cell room and 9 in a 16-cell. One room in 45 came out bare. So the "too much" half was much the bigger problem and the cause was Claude's, not the engine's: the row counts were lifted from `Villages_BuildingContents_Dwelling_*` wholesale, and village huts are BIGGER than our partitions — `carveBlock`'s own den threshold is 14 cells — so rows that furnish a house bury a den. **Fix: a floor budget that scales with the room.** `budget = max(1, round(cells * DWELLDENSITY))`, spent per placement, rows stopping when it runs out. **`DWELLDENSITY` = 0.3 is NOT invented** — reading the engine's own House rows against a ~20-cell hut puts its density around 0.15-0.35, so 0.3 is the faithful middle. The floor of 1 answers the other half of the flag. Two supporting shapes: the LIGHT row keeps first call on the budget (it is row 0 in every dwelling type — the engine's own invariant), and the remaining rows are SHUFFLED per room, so a small room is not forever "light plus whatever was listed second". `DWELLSHUFFLE` on. **Verified, 100 rolls, 45 rooms:** items per room now **0-5** (0→1, 1→3, 2→7, 3→12, 4→16, 5→6), density **median 0.30, p90 0.33, max 0.33** against the old 0.42/0.75/0.75, **0 rooms over budget**, tripwires clean 100/100, 0 console errors. **Residual, measured over a wider 250-roll sweep (87 rooms): exactly ONE bare room, a 4-cell house.** Cause is the threshold guard, not the budget — in a room that small every cell touches the door, so nothing is legal. Left as-is deliberately: a 4-cell closet with nothing in it reads fine, and the alternative is furniture standing in a doorway, which is the thing the guard exists to prevent. One line to relax if the user disagrees. Per-room diagnostic kept at `window.tfDwellings()` (type / cells / put / budget). **DENSITY NOT YET EYEBALLED BY THE USER.**
      - **WARREN FLOOR + SCALED SCATTER — USER FLAG + FIXED 2026-08-17 ("some are completely empty basically?", screenshot of a whole warren, not a room).** **Measured first, 120 rolls:** objects per warren min 0 / p10 2 / **median 5** / p90 11 / max 18, and **36 of 120 rolls put 3 or fewer objects in the ENTIRE warren**. Those rolls carried no block, almost never a gallery, and averaged 5.7 registered spaces against 8.9 overall. **Nothing was broken** — the heavy Nothing weights are the 2026-08-12 ruling working exactly as written ("density variance IS the freshness") — but that law was judged per SPACE and nobody had ever looked at the warren-scale sum. Six spaces at ~58% Nothing empties a small warren three times in ten. **Two fixes, both dials, per-space variance left exactly as ruled:** **(1) WARREN FLOOR.** `rollSpace(sp, skipNothing)` factored out of the dress loop so a space that came up Nothing can be re-rolled with the Nothing row removed — same table, same hints, same guards. After the pass, if the warren total is under `max(2, round(dressable * WARRENFLOOR))` (`WARRENFLOOR` = 0.45, CLAUDE DIAL), Nothing-spaces are re-rolled until it clears. Big warrens never reach the code; only the tail is cut. Re-rolls print in the dress line. **(2) SCALED NATURAL SCATTER.** The scatter was a flat 2-5 attempts whether the warren was 60 cells or 250, which is most of why a BIG bare warren read as bare. Now `natWant += round(warrenFloorCells * NATPERCELL)` with `NATPERCELL` = 0.03, CLAUDE DIAL; `natMin`/`natMax` stay the user's knobs and still set the floor. Geology needs no fiction budget — stones on the floor are the cheapest honest texture available, and the 08-12 ruling already put them inside the warren. **Verified, 120 rolls per stage:** rolls with ≤3 objects **36 → 14 → 4**; median objects per warren **5 → 7 → 9**; p10 **2 → 3 → 4**; natural objects 402 across the batch (~3.3 per warren, was ~1.5). Tripwires clean 120/120 at every stage, 0 console errors. **RESIDUAL, named honestly: 2 rolls in 120 register ZERO dressable spaces** — a warren whose parts included no socket, foot or bay at all. No table can fill those; the answer would be registering more space kinds (halls, trunks and plain chambers currently register nothing) or accepting them. Of the 4 remaining bare rolls, 2 are exactly this case. **NOT YET EYEBALLED BY THE USER; `WARRENFLOOR` 0.45 and `NATPERCELL` 0.03 are the two dials if it wants to go further either way.**
      - **DWELLING VOCABULARY TIGHTENED — USER RULINGS 2026-08-17 ("i think we should tighten up the furniture we're dressing these common areas with?"), BUILT + VERIFIED same sitting.** Claude drafted the pools from the base furniture catalogue without reading the blueprints' own text; doing that afterwards found the errors, and the blueprints argued the case themselves. **USER RULINGS:** **(1) FUNERARY OUT, BENCHES INCLUDED** — "because it has connotations in the tomb of the eaters". `Empty Urn`'s real DisplayName is **"funerary urn"**, its description is about *"the crematory vessel"*, and it carries a `Death` stag; Claude had it in the generic STORAGE pool, which was simply wrong. **A second one was caught on the verification pass: the palette holds TWO entries both named "funerary urn" (`bg.empty_urn` and `bg.urn`) and only the first had been cut** — `bg.urn` was still in the liquid-storage pool. Both gone. The urn has an obvious right home waiting: the abandoned tense, beside the `Tombstone` epitaph generator. **(2) CHAIRS → SCRAPASAN + FLOOR CUSHIONS**, user-named. `Scrapasan` blueprint truth: inherits `BasePortableChair`, `&y`/`w`, weight 50 and TAKEABLE (camp furniture you carry), described as *"this bowl-shaped basket of scrounged textiles and padding"* — scrounged and humble is the register. Mask extracted (`Items/sw_chair_scrapasan.bmp`), palette entry added. `Ornate Bench` argued its own exclusion: *"guild furniture makers, conspiring in a bright workroom beneath the Eaters' sun"* is not warren furniture. **(3) GENERIC WORK TABLES** — plain `Table` and `Workbench` stay in the centre pool, trade-specific tables out. **(4) WOVEN BASKETS FINE** (kept as ruled). **(5) BOOKSHELVES OUT**, which retires the whole `scholarly` room type with them; its weight is dropped rather than reassigned, so every surviving type's share rises proportionally. **SAME-DAY AMENDMENTS:** canvas folding chairs back in ("canvas folding chairs are fine also"), and **the hookah reinstated over Claude's cut** ("so is hookah!") — placed on its own `comfort` row on the sitting room rather than in the seating pool, because it is not a chair. **Claude-side cuts that stood:** `Glass Furnace` (soda ash through crucibles) and `Alchemist Table` are other trades standing in a carver's room. **A REAL BUG THE VERIFICATION CAUGHT: the reinstated hookah measured ZERO placements in 120 rolls.** The row existed and never fired — a sitting room's 2-4 seats spent the whole floor budget before the comfort row was reached. A rare thing has to outrank a common one or it is not rare, it is absent. Rows can now carry `first: true`, placing them straight after the light row and ahead of the shuffle. **Verified, 180 rolls / 71 rooms:** every cut item at **0 appearances**; hookah 2 across 6 sitting rooms (~33%, matching its 35% chance); seating spread 9 scrapasan / 7 cushions / 5 folding chairs; room mix 28 house / 16 storage / 12 crafts / 9 hearth / 6 seating; tripwires clean 180/180, 0 console errors. **BOTH FLAGGED ITEMS RULED same day (user: "anvil is fine millstone is not necessary"):** the anvil stays — its own *"smoothed on top for metalworking"* stops being an objection once a camp repairs its own picks — and the millstone is cut, since nobody here farms. Re-verified 100 rolls: 0 millstones, anvil and workbench both placing, tripwires 100/100. **Thin-pool follow-up, USER 2026-08-17: "let's add pickaxe and statues to it"** — crafts pool is now workbench / anvil / pickaxe / stone statue. A statue in the WORKROOM is work in progress, a different sentence from the finished pieces in the gallery; it is solid and occluding, so it runs the articulation and threshold guards and a refused row silently drops. **That exposed a bug one level up from the hookah, now fixed: room types were not containing their own type.** Measured 8 crafts rooms holding 2 pickaxes, 1 workbench and 1 anvil between them and ZERO statues, because the light row spent the floor budget before the crafts row was ever reached. Every type's SIGNATURE row now carries the `first: true` flag built for the hookah, landing right behind light and ahead of the shuffle (`house` deliberately has none — generic is what it is). Re-verified 120 rolls / 48 rooms: signature-item share is now crafts 7/16, storage 19/35, seating 11/21; statues and hookahs both appearing; tripwires 120/120, 0 console errors. **Weakest is `hearth` at 2 signature items across 3 rooms — small sample, but its pool is only oven + campfire and may want a third.** **SLABS + MAQUETTES ADDED, USER 2026-08-17 ("quarried slabs and figurines are also fine for work rooms"):** all three stack grades join the crafts pool and expand through `STACKSETS` in the dwelling path too, so a workroom's slabs roll sprite variants the way the bays' do. **Only the STONE figurine went in** — the 2026-08-14 material ladder ruled stone common and copper the rare real-money find, and a uniform pool cannot express that 2:1, so copper stays where the ruled split lives. One line to add if the user wants it here too. **LIGHT CAP, the last piece of the budget problem:** verification samples showed 7-8 cell crafts rooms spending their whole budget on TWO BRAZIERS — the engine asks for 1-2 lamps because a village hut can afford two, and in a budget-2 den that row took everything before the signature row could fire. Light may now never take more than HALF a room's budget, floor of 1, so the every-dwelling-is-lit invariant survives intact. **Re-verified 150 rolls / 55 rooms:** light is 40% of all placed items, only 4 rooms in 55 are all-light, and crafts rooms now read `brazier + torch + pickaxe + slab` or `brazier + slab + maquette`. Slab variants rolling across all three grades; tripwires 150/150, 0 console errors. **LIGHT TRIMMED TWICE, USER 2026-08-17.** First the vocabulary: "let's take out the tall ornate braziers from generation, there's plenty of light rolling" — and the blueprint backed the instinct, `Tall Brazier`'s short being *"burnished bronze is burned to LIGHT-IN-CEREMONY by hot coal"*, the same ceremonial register the funerary cut removed. Palette entry survives for hand-painting. **That cut changed variety, not quantity** — measured 150 rolls after it and light was still 40% of everything placed, the torch and remaining brazier having simply absorbed the tall one's share. Reported as such rather than claimed as a fix. So then the count: "we could roll fewer lights, yeag" — every type's light row dropped from the engine's `1-2` to a flat **1**. Every dwelling is still lit, which is the one invariant the engine never breaks, but no room is lit twice. The half-budget clamp is belt-and-braces now rather than load-bearing and stays in case a count ever grows again. **Re-verified 150 rolls / 55 rooms: light share 40% → 31%, rooms with 2+ lights 0, tall braziers 0, one unlit room (the known 4-cell closet where the threshold guard leaves nothing legal).** Furniture now competes with light in the totals rather than losing to it: straytorch 30, basket 24, brazier 24, chest 23. Tripwires 150/150, 0 console errors. **UNBUILT PROPOSAL:** a PLAN ROOM to fill the retired `scholarly` slot — chalk lines, stone maquettes, tools laid out, using the mod's own `chalkline`/`stonefig` vocabulary. Offered, not ruled on.
    - **"WALLS IN FRONT OF DOORS" — USER SCREENSHOT FLAG 2026-08-16 ("sometimes walls are spawning in front of doors? it's still accessible, just looks weird", one cell marked). INVESTIGATED: ONE REAL BUG FOUND AND FIXED, but the flagged shape is MOSTLY THE `sealDiag` RULING WORKING AS RULED — a feel call is owed, not a fix.** Method: the finished grid can't say which rule placed a block, so `sealChamberDiagonals` was instrumented to log every fill with a 3x3 snapshot of the geometry AT SEAL TIME (`window.tfSeals()`, kept as a diagnostic). **(1) THE RULED BEHAVIOUR, not a bug.** Sampled seals in context read like this — X is the fill, attached to the wall mass, jutting one cell into open floor beside an opening: `###...# / ###...# / ###.... / ###X... / ##.#### `. That IS the 2026-08-12 ruling ("seal accidental diagonal entrances into chambers from the ROOM side — the hall keeps its line, the room goes quiet behind its real door"): the rule explicitly refuses to fill a cell with an orthogonal opening outside the part, so it always lands BESIDE the real door, which is exactly the silhouette the user marked. Rate is low — **20 seals across 120 dressed canyon-warren rolls**, ~0.17 per roll. **USER CALL OWED, three shapes offered:** keep as ruled · `sealDiag` knob to 0 (the old behaviour, corner kisses stay walkable) · or narrow the rule so it only fires where the diagonal is the room's ONLY leak. No code touched on this half, deliberately — it is a ruled behaviour and the ruling is the user's. **(2) THE REAL BUG — ORPHANED SEALS, FIXED.** Caught by comparing one seal's at-seal snapshot with the end-of-roll grid: `.## / #X. / ..#` when it landed, **all eight neighbours open by the end**. Cause is ordering. The shard sweep runs during the carve, deliberately before the seal union so a fresh seal is never sweep bait (its own comment says so) — but the MOUTH, the probed gallery doors and the BACK DOOR all carve afterwards, and a seal is only justified by the walls that were around it when it landed. Dissolve those and the fill stands alone in open floor, a block in front of a doorway that no pass ever cleans. **Fix:** the lone-shard half lifted into `sweepLoneWalls()` and run a SECOND time immediately before the tripwires, once nothing else will carve; same keepWalls/protectedShell exemptions. Safe by construction and worth stating — clearing a cell whose 8 neighbours are already open cannot create a corner kiss, and a wall with no wall neighbours cannot be sealing a diagonal, so the pass only ever removes fills that stopped meaning anything. **HONEST LIMIT: the new sweep has NOT fired in 120 post-fix rolls (20 seals).** The orphan was observed once, pre-fix, with hard evidence; it is rarer than 1-in-20-seals, so the fix is a closed hole rather than a demonstrated cure for what the user photographed. Tripwires clean 80/80 and 120/120 across the verification batches, 0 console errors.
      - **THICKENED SEAL — BUILT 2026-08-17 (USER: "alright third fix sounds good"), then **USER-REJECTED ON SIGHT THE SAME SITTING** ("this example is basically a solid wall"). Kept behind a knob, DEFAULT PENDING THE USER'S CALL. The Claude proposal was wrong and the reason is structural — recorded here so it is not re-proposed.** Context: the user's read on the 2026-08-11 original — "it was to fix one specific seed and ended up having spiralling effects" — was checked against the record and is accurate; TODO:534 is a ruling made while judging a single warren roll. **Why narrowing was rejected first:** 21 seals over 200 rolls counted by orthogonal wall neighbours at fill time — **19 of 21 landed with exactly 2, the structural minimum**, because two orthogonal walls are what MAKES a diagonal leak. Two walls = two open sides = the one-cell nub. A "flush fills only" filter would have deleted 90% of the rule rather than narrowed it. **Built instead:** remember the leaking diagonal (`leakDir`), then extend one cell along one of the two walls that made it a leak, so the fill becomes a straight three-cell run; preference to the side that joins existing mass; every law of the original applied to the second cell (chamber cells only, doorway guard, 8-dir connectivity flood reverting anything that strands); fallback to the single fill when no partner is legal. Knob `sealThick` (warren—grammar, beside `sealDiag`), dial `SEALTHICKMIN` = 9 chamber cells answering the user's own worry that "a 2-cell fill in a small den could eat more room than it's worth". Log reads `N leaks sealed (T thickened, S single)`. Measured fine on its own terms — 179 rolls, 29 seals, 21 thickened, 13 of 15 standing fills carrying 3-4 orthogonal walls, 0 orphans, tripwires 176/176 clean, 0 console errors. **THE FINDING THAT KILLED IT:** a fill that stops a diagonal leak MUST sit at the pinch between two wall masses — that is what a diagonal leak is — so sealing always WELDS those two masses together. The single fill welds them at a corner and reads as a nub; the thickened fill welds them along a line and reads as a new wall closing the space (`##X#.` bridging two masses, `#.#X#..` plugging a one-wide channel). Both complaints are the same complaint at different sizes, and thickening made it LOUDER, not quieter. There is no fill shape that avoids the weld. **OPEN — USER'S CALL, three real shapes:** (a) `sealThick` to 0, back to the minimal weld and live with the nub · (b) `sealDiag` to 0, retire the rule that has now drawn two separate aesthetic complaints from the one roll that spawned it · (c) invert the mechanism entirely — instead of sealing the corner, CARVE it into an honest doorway, which trades "a room you walk past without noticing" (the 08-11 intent) for a room with a visible second door and zero debris. Claude lean was (b) or (c). **CLOSED BY USER RULING 2026-08-17 — OPTION (a): "i think it's fine right now. i'd rather do more detailed carving or dressing rather than keep dinking with it."** `sealDiag` stays ON as ruled 08-11, `sealThick` stays 0, the nub is accepted at its measured rate of roughly one seal per ten rolls. Do not reopen this without a fresh user flag.
    - **RED ROCK AS A HOST — GROUND TRUTH + LAB PASS BUILT 2026-08-17 (USER DIRECTION: "i'm looking to potentially rework the warren quarries to be structured more like red rock" → "it's less so that i want the layered dungeon shape and more to integrate the blob carve a bit more potentially? or see how that looks", plus a standing offer to reopen the one-mouth ruling "if it meant being more base game accurate"). NOTHING RULED — three buttons to look at.** **Ground truth first (decompiled `XRL.World.ZoneBuilders.Redrock`, `NoiseMap.CreateNoiseMap`, `ZoneBuilderSandbox.TunnelTo`/`EnsureAllVoidsConnected`; the 2026-08-07 survey had only ever covered Red Rock's TABLE side, its geometry was never in our record — full section added to `design/cavegen-survey-2026-08-07.md`):** fill every cell with Shale → collect every ZoneConnection and every Stairs cell as NoiseMap **extra nodes, i.e. the doors are SEEDS** (there is no mouth search anywhere in the file — this is the base game's whole answer to attachment, and the direct rival to our `mouthMassPerPart` fitting) → `NoiseMap(80,25,10, 3,2, seedsPerSector, 80,240, 0, 4,-3, 1, nodes, -1, maxSeeds)`, clear walls at noise >= 1 → **BaseNoise 0 means the only open space is seed blobs**, which is why Red Rock reads as discrete chambers and not sponge → a second NoiseMap seeded from a SUBSET of the first's PlacedSeeds becomes `DeepBrackishPool`, each pool node tunnelling to a random WALL cell within 30 (water veins that run into the mass and stop — the opposite shape from our sealed-and-avoided pockets; NOT PORTED, deliberately) → 0-4 `TunnelTo` hauls between a random seed and the seed furthest from it, **`emptyBase` 200 against a wall's 80, so open floor costs more than twice what solid rock does and the hauls bore FRESH passage instead of threading the chambers** → `EnsureAllVoidsConnected` → per-level set pieces. **The depth ladder is this one builder with different dice and nothing else** (seeds/sector `1-3` capped at 3 total on level 1 → `0-7` on level 4; pools 0 → 3-4; hauls 0 → 3-4) — the size-class idiom we already run, one scale up. **The structural fact that matters for us: Red Rock has NO carve language.** Its geometry carries zero intent; intent arrives as a TENANT layered on finished blobs (level 2 seals its furthest-from-stairs regions with `InfluenceMapBlocker` + their own encounter table — a vault you dig into, and the closest base-game thing to our wet pocket; level 3 rolls Stockade / CaveCity / SnapjawFort; level 4 gets rivers). **BUILT in `warren-lab.html` same day, three buttons:** `Cave` (host alone, faithful port incl. the shuffled-sector seed cap, door/stair extra nodes inset one cell past the engine's border guard, the 200-vs-80 haul cost, and void-joining), `Cave Warren` (stage-2 warren with the cave as host), `Canyon + Caves` (canyon first, blobs at low seed density as voids in the band mass). **The stage-2 body was refactored to `genWarrenInHost(hostFn, hostName)` and is HOST-AGNOSTIC — it only ever keyed off `canyonFloor`/`canyonSkin`, so a new host costs one function; `genCanyonWarren` is now a two-word wrapper and the ruled canyon path adds no rng calls.** New knob group "cave host (redrock)" (16 knobs, every number from the decompiled call) + **`extraMouths` in the stage-2 group: the one-mouth ruling ON A DIAL rather than rewritten** — extra openings reuse the back-door siting rule, default 0 keeps the 2026-08-11 ruling and the rng order exactly as they stand. **Verified 60 rolls (20 each): 0 tripwire failures, 0 breaches, 0 console errors, canyon warren unregressed.** Measured, canyon / cave / hybrid: warren cells avg **156 / 102 / 122** (min 81 / 60 / 51, max 251 / 177 / 278), parts avg 15 / 12 / 14, class degradation 6 / 6 / 5 of 20. **CLAUDE READ, for the user's judgement (all observation, nothing ruled):** (1) the two hands DO read apart on the map — the cave wanders, the warren keeps its right angles — and the site gaining four or five zone-edge doors while the warren keeps its one is arguably the ruling and base-game accuracy both getting what they want; (2) the cave costs the quarry a third of its mass (102 vs 156 cells) because blobs eat the rock the warren needs, so cave warrens run small and step down a class as often as thin bands do; (3) **the hybrid does not currently tell the story it was built for** — blob voids register as host floor, so every carver REFUSES them and the warren reads as *avoiding* caves rather than *breaking into* them; a natural pocket the diggers may open would be the wet-pocket machinery inverted (sealed + rewarded instead of sealed + avoided) and is the obvious next question if this track continues; (4) cosmetic, lab-only: the floor paint is the canyon's Dirty widget, so cave floor comes out dirt-and-grass — grass underground.
  - **CARVE ARTICULATION BATCH 2026-08-07 (user: "carved sections should feel fully articulated before filling them") — geometry-only, from the ruin recon (`cavegen-survey-2026-08-07.md` § freshness):** (1) **threshold squeeze** — home pocket always sits 1-3 cells behind the corridor, entry carved 1-wide: deliberate den-mouth geometry, and it fixes the **SEALED-CAMP BUG** (deeper home retries left wall between mouth and blob tip, camps opened only by weathering luck — latent in BOTH sim and C#; **C# fix built + deployed same day**, the one articulation item already ported). **ROLE TEXTURE (mason figurines + hewer stones) — USER PITCH 2026-08-23: "i wanted to expand the roles of each stonecarver a bit, making them a bit more than just their mutations. for masons, i'm thinking a small swarm of animate stone figurines with the swarmer bonus could spawn alongside them. for hewers i'm thinking they could spawn with some rocks that when thrown, make a fissure detonation at the center of their tile, then are destroyed." DIALS + PAYLOAD RULED same day ("stone level should probably be 2, amount should be 2-3, figurine count can be 2-4. no redoubt ring"). BUILT + DEPLOYED same day on branch `items` (compile 0 errors, XML strict-valid, text-rules green, deploy hash-verified; active next restart). Ground truth decompile/base-XML verified 2026-08-23.**

- **Mason swarm — every piece is stock grammar.** `Swarmer` (decompiled): +1 melee to-hit AND +1 to penetration rolls per other swarmer beyond the first who is adjacent to the target and hostile toward it; faction-agnostic (any Swarmer carrier counts); `ExtraBonus` attr = the Swarm Alpha aura variant. Spawn-alongside skeleton = `HasGuards : IBondedLeader` (decompiled): on first `EnteredCellEvent`, roll count, `GetFirstEmptyAdjacentCell(1,2)`, add companion part bonded to the leader, `MakeActive()`. `HiredGuard` hardcodes Faction="Merchants"/Title="hired guard"/ConversationID="MerchantGuard", so it is NOT reusable: build thin `Cleo_TerraFirma_HasFigurines` (pinned blueprint, `NumberOfFigurines` attr) + `Cleo_TerraFirma_BondedFigurine : IBondedCompanion` (its `Initialize` sets `Brain.PartyLeader` + `TakeAllegiance(AllyBond)`; no honorific/title), + one new figurine creature blueprint (`Swarmer` part, `Cleo_TerraFirma_Quarriers-100`). Mole mason inherits by descent (deliberate). Canon rhyme: the 08-22 kit ruling already gives the mason the inert maquette; the pocket figurine gains walking cousins, and the Role="Summoner" tag (08-15 recon) already prices more bodies into the con. Count "2-4" RULED. BUILT: `Part_HasFigurines.cs` (both parts) + `Cleo_TerraFirma_AnimateFigurine` in Creatures.xml, render = the figurine item tile in stone grey, corpse drop = the inert stone figurine at 50% (CLAUDE dial). STILL USER'S: figurine stats (ALL PLACEHOLDER, numbers sitting owed), name + description (DRAFT, signoff owed), bespoke sprite if ever (tile reuse is the 1.0 default per the art-bar ruling); whether the mason itself carries `Swarmer` to join its own swarm (CLAUDE default: no, unruled).
- **Hewer stones — exact base precedent is the goatfolk sower** (base Creatures.xml:4663-4682): 4x `Sowers_Seed` in plain inventory, the seed being `BaseThrownWeapon` + `Stacker` + `Physics Category="Grenades"` + an `IGrenade` part (`HEGrenade`), NO special AI part anywhere — stock combat AI throws inventory grenades. `IGrenade` (decompiled): `AfterThrownEvent` -> `Detonate(ParentObject.CurrentCell, E.Actor, E.ApparentTarget)` -> abstract `DoDetonate(C, Actor, ...)`; concrete grenades destroy themselves inside `DoDetonate`. "Detonation at the center of their tile, then destroyed" is native grammar. **PAYLOAD RULED 2026-08-23 ("no redoubt ring"): pulses only** — three pulses, push, stun, pin-slam, wall bites, and never the rim. The economy argument stands recorded: cooldown 100 is priced against a permanent fortress ("the product never expires, so level must not also buy frequency"), so lootable stones must not carry ring-raising; the ring stays the mutation's signature. **Level "2" and count "2-3" RULED same message.** At Level 2 the staircase gives 3d4/2d3/1d3, stun 1 round at DC 16; PV rides the thrower's Str mod per the fissure grammar, so a looted stone scales with whoever throws it while the dice stay pinned. BUILT: `Part_FissureStone.cs` (`IGrenade` subclass, sower grammar) + `Cleo_TerraFirma_FissureStone` in ObjectBlueprints.xml (no Examiner, understood on sight; DetonatedSound = Fissure's verified slam; tile = the fallen-rock family's) + the kit line in PopulationTables.xml. The pulse core is now `Cleo_TerraFirma_Fissure.RunPulses` (extracted from the cast body with a `PulseWake` carrying breach/credit state back to the ring pass; `Sweep` and `WaveBitesWall` went static with it; the cast's behavior is unchanged line for line and the extraction is delegated plumbing). INHERITED IGrenade BEHAVIORS, flagged not chosen: stones cook off when damaged (sympathetic detonation, exempting and crediting nobody) and the holder gets an in-inventory Detonate action. Loot consequence deliberate: killing a hewer yields its unspent stones. STILL USER'S: stone name + description (DRAFT, signoff owed), the "shatter" message-queue line (CLAUDE DRAFT in code), Commerce 20 / Weight 10 (CLAUDE DRAFT). OPEN RIDERS, unruled: whether the stone carries `Cleo_TerraFirma_IsGeomancyStone` (geomancy could then move live charges, cute and dangerous); mole hewer inherits the stones by descent (deliberate, same as the mason's swarm). Prior thrown-detonation art in-repo at git tag `fissure-v2-grenade` (second iteration; outside the 08-11 quarantine, which covers v1 + its rock catalog + Lithovore — untouched by this work).

- **Ground truth (3-agent decompile/base-XML sweep, full citations in the agents' record):** the jackhammer (Items.xml:11181) is a 2d4 Cudgel two-hander (Hand,Hand,Back) with `Drill` + `DiggingTool` + `EnergyCellSocket` + its OWN AI cell-reload parts; burrower PATHING (walls become weight-4+AV floor to the pathfinder) requires a charged cell and proper wield, but the `Digging` part grant is not charge-gated, so even a dead jackhammer bashes diggable walls that block a path. Socket self-slots a chem cell 50% at creation (EnergyCellSocket defaults), 10% of those full. Base game ships burrower NPCs ON PURPOSE: Graverobber spawns wielding a Pickaxe via `IntProperties="AlwaysEquipAsWeapon:1"` (Creatures.xml:7207), and Pickaxe's Drill is ChargeUse=0, always ready. **The 08-22 veto was already half-moot: BurrowingClaws NPCs path-as-burrower BY DEFAULT** (toggle ships on, no level/AI/player gate; decomp chain BurrowingClaws -> PathAsBurrowerEvent -> Zone.CalculateNav bit 2 -> Physics.GetNavigationWeightEvent), so our mole variants have been latent tunnelers since 08-15. Quirk worth knowing: the dig-BY-MOVING branch (GameObject.Move) is gated on the player's own `Options.DigOnMove` setting for NPCs too; the ShouldAttackToReachTarget combat fallback is not. Mod-side levers if a zone ever needs protection: `PathAsBurrower="false"` on the part, or `<tag Name="Diggable" Value="*delete" />` on walls (base precedent Walls.xml:1959).
- **BUILT:** `Cleo_TerraFirma_MiningGear` in PopulationTables.xml, one pickeach of a Headgear pickone (8 rungs, commons heavy: Leather Cap 20 / Vinewood Sap Mask 20 / Goggles 13 / Witchwood Wreath 12 / Miner's Helmet 10 / Headlamp 10 / Gas Mask 8 / Knollworm Skull 7) plus a Gloves pickone at Chance 35 (Leather Gloves 45 / Boar-skin gloves 40 / Cleo_TerraFirma_Cobblefur Gloves 15). Hewer rolls the table always (his fixed mask line died into a common rung), mason at Chance 65. Jackhammer rides the HEWER BLUEPRINT (Creatures.xml, `<inventoryobject Chance="25" IntProperties="AlwaysEquipAsWeapon:1" />`, graverobber grammar); mole hewer inherits, stacking with its claws. Both kits swapped from `EtchTools` to the `Carver Knife`/`Carver Hammer` ladders (the 08-22 etch ruling landing as sequenced), which the outrider's arms already rolled, so **same-pool holds by construction and shields stay outrider-only. EtchTools SCRUBBED** (zero consumers after the swap; its load-bearing findings, flat-weights-over-{ownertier} and mismatched-pairs-as-feature, survive in the ladder comment and here). Every blueprint name verified real before wiring.
- **Deliberate riders, not accidents:** miner's helmet and headlamp are always-on light sources (r6/r3), some carvers arrive self-lit; goggles AddsRep Insects +50; witchwood wreath harvests into Witchwood Bark (a carver can wear what the hewer's dialogue asks for); knollworm skull +1 Ego; granitefur gloves spread the AddsRep Baboons -100 grudge beyond the outrider pelt.

Standing offer to revisit if the dials converge.

So: `<object Blueprint="Pickaxe" Chance="35" />` on the Quarrier Kit spine (chance CLAUDE DRAFT), a carried work tool, flavor first; the snapjaw miner already carried a plain pick (Creatures.xml), so this was the pack idiom all along. (4) **BRACER EXAM 2026-08-22 (thorough, per the user's ask; every banked engine claim re-verified against fresh decomp of `GameObject.Throw`):** scatter scaling `AimVariance x (100 - max(item, skill))/100` CONFIRMED · fall-short skip CONFIRMED (the property branch bypasses the DistanceVariance add entirely) · max(item-line, skill-line) CONFIRMED · negative-past-100 CONFIRMED (`(int)(AimVariance x negative)` goes below zero, behavior in `CalculateBulletTrajectory` UNKNOWN). **FINDING 1 (real hole): the cap-aware lend only guards OUR equip.** The Parabolic Muscular Subroutine adds a FLAT non-cap-aware 100 via `CyberneticsPropertyModifier` (base Items.xml:9142) — implant-first is safe (bracer lends 0), but **bracer-first then implant = 125 total = negative multiplier**, order-dependent overflow the receipt system cannot see. **FINDING 2 (wart): a bracer that lent 0 at the cap never re-lends** when room frees (`AppliedAmount == 0` doubles as the not-yet-lent guard). **FINDING 3 (verified non-bug): the engine's gate is `HasIntProperty` — a property KEY at value 0 still grants the fall-short skip; our `RemoveIfZero: true` on return prevents phantom keys, and the lent-0 path never writes the key. Correct by construction.** **RESOLVED BY REWORK, not by the rebalance fix — RULED 2026-08-22 (user: "swap the bonus to +2 throw range and dodge the problem altogether"): the bracer now grants `ThrowRangeBonus +2` (USER NUMBER), the same property family as the Parabolic subroutine's range half, read linearly into Range by `GetThrowProfileEvent` with no cap cliff. The entire cap-aware lend/return receipt system is DELETED with the problem it guarded. BUILT + DEPLOYED. Same sitting, GLOVES REWORKED (user: "rather than catch only thrown rocks, it could work on all thrown items mayhaps"): the IsAnyStone filter is now `Launcher == null` — the thrown-vs-shot gate, so any thrown item can be snatched but arrows/bullets never (they arrive with a launcher). Rider became a FEATURE same sitting (user design: "catching a grenade could allow you a turn before it explodes, so you can throw it back"). **Ground truth that forced it: as first built, a caught grenade detonated IN THE CATCHER'S FACE instantly — `IGrenade.Detonate` resolves a null cell through the HOLDER via `GetCurrentCell()` and drops the grenade at their feet before the boom (decomp-verified); the gloves were quietly a suicide item against grenadiers. HOT POTATO BUILT: `Cleo_TerraFirma_CaughtGrenade` marker attached at catch — cancels `BeforeDetonateEvent` while the grenade is in the catcher's reach and pulls it back into hand (Detonate dumps it floorward before asking), fuse ~1 usable turn (DRAFT dial); throw it and the next detonation sails through, marker strips itself; sit on it and the fuse expires, detonating it wherever it is. BUILT + DEPLOYED, compiles green.** Auto-ready-into-thrown-slot behavior kept for whatever gets caught. BUILT + DEPLOYED, compiles green, hash-verified.** `[verify]` next boot: bracer +2 range visible in throw reticle, stacks with itself linearly · gloves snatch a thrown non-stone item · arrows/bullets never caught · caught grenade survives the catch, sits one turn, throw-back detonates at the target, held too long detonates in hand (`[GLOV]` lines narrate the fuse). The litter ecology as your cover; third body on the accumulation signature.** **ADJACENT SCOPE RE-EXAMINED AND CONFIRMED (user challenge + ruling 2026-08-13, "i agree with that thinking"): user asked if adjacent outmuscles Yurtmat's same-tile (their impression was Yurtmat = adjacent too; decomp says same-tile only). Kept adjacent on three legs: (1) solidity split — small stones/boulders standable, but `LargeBoulder` is Solid and base `Rubble` is a solid Walls.xml heap, so same-tile would make the most cover-shaped rocks the only ones that can't give cover; (2) same-tile would coach standing ON rocks, colliding with the discharge law; (3) uptime parity — adjacent-on-sparse-transient rubble ≈ same-tile-on-dense foliage, and the packages are cousins (theirs 0/1+2, ours 1/0+2). Balance dial if play disagrees = the conditional bonus (+2→+1) at the numbers sitting, NOT the scope. **Rubble-heap inclusion RULED IN, built same exchange: base `Rubble` carries a NEW NARROW tag `Cleo_TerraFirma_IsRubbleCover` via Merges (widening IsAnyStone refused per the trade-goods narrow-tag discipline; scavenger/sweeper/gloves all Takeable-gated so no blast radius); the cloak accepts either tag.** Build note: the `--`-in-XML-comment trap struck an 8th time on this merge AND the invalid file briefly deployed (validation exception didn't halt the copy) — fixed, revalidated with try/catch-exit, redeployed; future validations should fail-closed like that. Gearset draft updated with pointers (bracers + catch gloves rows now live here). Deployed: 3 scripts + Creatures/ObjectBlueprints/PopulationTables. Everything else open, user's: what a miner snapjaw *wants* (scavenging quarry sites? aping the carvers? just digging for grubs?), spawn placement, whether it digs for real (base `Digging`/pickaxe grammar vs flavor). *(Claude float, unruled: snapjaws sniffing around quarried-stone sites would give the block-yard/chalk-border content a natural antagonist — the temptation loop from the other direction, somebody ELSE robbing the carvers.)* **PLACEMENT + TEMPERAMENT LEANS BANKED (user 2026-08-13, "i lean" — soft, confirm at build; formal roster confirm still owed): (1) can spawn in ANY snapjaw group, (2) also sometimes canyon-wild, (3) "likes digging walls as much as attacking."** **Digging recon DONE same day (decomp, Claude): the temperament is 100% stock — zero C#, the pickaxe IS the AI.** Chain: base `Pickaxe` = `MeleeWeapon` (1d3+1, Axe skill) + `Drill` (+20 pen vs walls, wall dies after 8 penetrating hits) + `DiggingTool`; `DiggingTool` installs the `Digging` part on the wielder while properly equipped (EquippedEvent → `RequirePart<Digging>`, removed on unequip via part-support); `Digging` answers `ShouldAttackToReachTargetEvent` → he swings at diggable walls between him and a target; and `Drill` ITSELF answers `PathAsBurrowerEvent` while functional → `GameObject.PathAsBurrower` → nav flag 2 in `Zone.CalculateNav` → the route planner treats walls as passable-by-digging even when just traveling. Precedents: **Worm of the Earth** (no burrow mutation at all — beak carries exactly Drill+DiggingTool, and it honeycombs zones; living proof the item alone drives tunneling) and **mopango pilgrim/tombdweller** (carry `BurrowingClaws PathAsBurrower="false"` — the knob exists because default-on wall-chewing had to be opted OUT of for village NPCs). `[verify]` at build, in-game: idle-wander digging frequency (planner digs when traveling somewhere; how often pure wandering picks wall cells = watch one wished-up miner, Player.log session). Open at build (user's): tier structure (base idiom = 0/1/2 thin children w/ scaled stats, Scavenger precedent — vs one blueprint in all three parties), party chance number (Claude will propose Hunter-ish 15–25 at build), name lock ("snapjaw miner" presumed), description (user voice per text ownership). **BUILT 2026-08-13: `Cleo_TerraFirma_Snapjaw Miner` (Creatures.xml) as the BEHAVIOR PARENT in the base two-layer idiom — recon upgrade at build time: tier children (Scavenger 0/1/2 precedent) are pure `InventoryPopulationTable` swaps on an untiered parent, so this blueprint forecloses nothing; tier ruling waits at the wiring pass as recorded. Pickaxe direct-inventoried (the AI, per the recon above); HP 6 DRAFT (Hunter-grade over base 3); identity kept NEUTRAL in the description on purpose (what he *wants* is still open). Compiled/deployed with the hound; checklist entry added.**

Build shape (Claude lean): the ruled custom anatomy grows a **gut cistern body slot** + natural-equipment cistern item with its own LiquidVolume (one object can't carry two LiquidVolume parts) — basin = creature's own tank UNSEALED (open back), cistern = sealed internal item, leach = transfer part ticking only the water fraction inward. **Free consequence to protect: basin CONCENTRATES salt as fresh migrates — visible in both readouts, and it sends herds back to pools to re-dilute (writes the grazing behavior).** Death-drop native (equipment falls with contents — the cistern IS the loot, no butchery machinery); pet decant via companion trade. Intake RULED (user, same sitting): "the rain that fills it up doesn't need to be a mechanical thing — it can just be flavor that uses waterproducer" — basin fills via LiquidProducer (the pet's own part, re-aimed), rain stays description-side; no pool-siphon/graze-fill machinery. (Claude's pool-congregation tableau dies with it — noted so it isn't re-floated.) **Unification bonus: retroactively explains the pet's existing LiquidProducer — all boar vessels purify, the Husangeion is a well-tended one. Economy note: upgrades the salty guard from wall to TIMER (fresh-filled = worthless brine, long-grazed = currency) — third creature on the accumulation signature, first where the value is literal money, so purification RATE is the load-bearing number at the numbers sitting (ceiling precedent: pet producer 200–2000 turns into 64 drams). **Rate direction RULED (user, same sitting): "they'd probably fill up pretty slow"** — slow is the shape; exact dice at the numbers sitting per the standing delegation.** Open at pickup: fill mechanism (LiquidProducer re-dial vs time-gated), herd structure, habitat, hostility/passivity (grazing prey lean), cistern confirm + rate. **BUILD ORDER (user 2026-08-11, roster talk): first tinker target — base blueprint first, verify the wild form works, THEN re-parent Husangeion onto it** (the existing-save `[verify]` rides that last step). **BUILD TALK-THROUGH RULINGS (same day, second sitting):** (1) **cistern IN v1** (user: "i would lean implement this on v1 but it depends on tech debt. since we already verified mostly everything works with husangeion, it's cheap to add something here"). **TECH DEBT RESOLVED TO ZERO C# (user asked "is the C# part necessary?"; decomp recon same exchange): the leach = TWO LiquidProducers, one per tank object** — basin (creature's unsealed tank) gets `LiquidProducer Liquid="salt" FillSelfOnly` on a part-water tank (one part does both ruled jobs: fills-with-salty-water AND the concentration consequence; IsNeeded self-terminates at full), cistern item gets its own sealed LiquidVolume + slow `LiquidProducer Liquid="water" FillSelfOnly` (pure fresh inside; producers tick on carried items — proven by our own take-stones Temporary bug). Pet's pure-water producer overrides the base's salt producer by part-name replacement. `LiquidPump` RULED OUT (intake only reads ground puddles, MaxVolume==-1 gate); `ConsumesLiquid` RULED OUT for cross-tank draw (ConsumeLiquid draws from the part's own object, not the wearer). Literal conservation dropped — intake already flavor-ruled, pet precedent for ex-nihilo water. **BUILD CORRECTION: cistern = REAL equipment auto-equipped via inventoryobject into its anatomy slot, NOT DefaultBehavior natural** (naturals don't drop on death; worn equipment drops with contents = the ruled cistern-is-the-loot). Verifies: equipped-item-with-LiquidVolume through save/load (tfserial-style probe) + long-graze eyeball of the basin's salt fraction climbing. **Cistern accessibility (user question, answered from decomp same sitting): NOT player-reachable while equipped — inventory-action menus never open on another creature's worn items; it drops SEALED on death (the loot beat). The BASIN (creature's own tank) IS reachable on a living adjacent vessel per the established pet behavior — wild = unsealed brackish, correctly worthless. Optional door if in-situ access is ever wanted: an action-relay part on the creature (StoneMill campfire-dispatch pattern) — costs the one C# part the two-producer shape eliminated; not built.** (2) **Glaze RULED fine to lie** (user: "it's fine for this to lie") — VesselTint stays majority-liquid; brackish wild vessels glaze water-blue like fresh pets; the look/interact readouts are the tell. Codex PROPOSAL verification entry, 2026-09-16: terrain-led warren experiment built and deployed to the live dev mod (five source/XML hashes matched). New claims have no assigned stage; world builds and tfwarrenv2 start with the largest budget and accept the first valid terrain-limited result, without augmentation or full-zone recasting. Generated aquifers have a seeded 20% XML gate, with 1-2 pool attempts on a positive roll; existing water is unchanged. Wish tfwarrenstats builds unvisited claims, reports success/failure, stage/layout counts, floor/room sizes and generated aquifers, and preserves per-zone measurements across save/load through string game state. Failed map-note deletion does not erase measurements. Already-built unrecorded zones are excluded; use a fresh world for a complete sample, then tfwarrens to reveal surviving sites. Compile passed (0 errors); text rules and 17 XML parses passed; synthetic survey checks passed for repeat runs, state roundtrip, failure retention, legacy exclusion and empty samples. In-game wish execution, real-world counts, access and playability remain unverified. Furnishing, alternate-layout and yard redesigns are outside this experiment.
